“Tell me,” he said gently. “You have dreamt these dreams twice?”
She nodded, wide-eyed with wonder for his knowledge.
“On the occasions of your husband’s illnesses?”
“Yes, yes!”
“What did you dream?”

===Chinese===
「告诉我，」他柔声说。「这样的梦，您做过两次？」
她点了点头，为他料事如神而睁大了眼睛。
「都在您丈夫发病的那些时候？」
「是，是！」
「您梦见了什么？」

===Original===
“Oh! can I, dare I tell you!﻿—”
“You must.”
There was pity in his voice.
“I dreamt that I lay in some very dark cavern. I could hear the sea booming, apparently over my head. But above all the noise a voice was audible, calling to me﻿—not by name; I cannot explain in what way; but calling, calling imperatively. I seemed to be clothed but scantily, in some kind of ragged garments; and upon my knees I crawled toward the voice, through a place where there were other living things that crawled also﻿—things with many legs and clammy bodies.﻿ ﻿…”
She shuddered and choked down an hysterical sob that was half a laugh.

===Chinese===
「哦！我能说吗？我敢说吗！——」
「您必须说。」
他的声音里含着怜悯。
「我梦见自己躺在一个极其黑暗的洞穴里。我听得见海涛轰鸣，仿佛就在我头顶上。可是在这一切声响之上，有一个声音听得真切，它在唤我——不是唤我的名字；那是一种怎样的唤法，我说不清；只觉得它在唤，在急切地唤。我似乎身上少有遮盖，只披着些褴褛的破衣；我双膝跪地，朝那声音爬去，爬过一片也有别的活物在爬行的地方——那些东西生着许多条腿，身子又冷又黏……」
她打了个寒噤，强咽下一声半似狂笑的抽泣。

===Original===
“My hair hung dishevelled about me and in some inexplicable way﻿—oh! am I going mad!﻿—my head seemed to be detached from my living body! I was filled with a kind of unholy anger which I cannot describe. Also, I was consumed with thirst, and this thirst.﻿ ﻿…”
“I think I understand,” said Dr. Cairn quietly. “What followed?”
“An interval﻿—quite blank﻿—after which I dreamt again. Dr. Cairn, I *cannot* tell you of the dreadful, the blasphemous and foul thoughts, that then possessed me! I found myself resisting﻿—resisting﻿—something, some power that was dragging me back to that foul cavern with my thirst unslaked! I was frenzied; I dare not name, I tremble to think, of the ideas which filled my mind. Then, again came a blank, and I awoke.”
She sat trembling. Dr. Cairn noted that she avoided his gaze.

===Chinese===
「我披头散发，乱发四垂；而且不知怎的——哦！我是要疯了吗！——我的头颅似乎同我那活着的身子分了家！我心里灌满了一种说不出的邪异怒火。还有，我渴得五内如焚，而这股渴……」
「我想我明白了，」凯恩博士平静地说。「后来呢？」
「中间隔着一段空白——全然的空白——之后我又做了梦。凯恩博士，那之后占据我心的那些念头，何等骇人、何等渎神、何等污秽，我『没法』说给您听！我发觉自己在抗拒——抗拒——某种东西，某种正把我拖回那污秽洞穴的力量，拖回去时我的焦渴还未得纾解！我简直狂乱了；当时充塞在我头脑里的那些念头，我不敢说出口，想起来就发抖。随后又是一片空白，我醒了。」
她坐在那里发抖。凯恩博士留意到，她避开了他的目光。

===Original===
“You awoke,” he said, “on the first occasion, to find that your husband had met with a strange and dangerous accident?”
“There was﻿—something else.”
Lady Lashmore’s voice had become a tremulous whisper.
“Tell me; don’t be afraid.”
She looked up; her magnificent eyes were wild with horror.

===Chinese===
「您醒来时，」他说，「第一次那回，发现您丈夫遭遇了一场离奇而凶险的意外？」
「还有——别的事。」
拉什莫尔夫人的声音已化作颤抖的耳语。
「告诉我；别怕。」
她抬起头来；那双美丽的眸子里盛满狂乱的惊怖。

===Original===
“I believe you know!” she breathed. “Do you?”
Dr. Cairn nodded.
“And on the second occasion,” he said, “you awoke earlier?”
Lady Lashmore slightly moved her head.
“The dream was identical?”

===Chinese===
「我相信您是知道的！」她气声道。「是不是？」
凯恩博士点了点头。
「那么第二次，」他说，「您醒得更早？」
拉什莫尔夫人微微颔首。
「两回的梦一模一样？」

===Original===
“Yes.”
“Excepting these two occasions, you never dreamt it before?”
“I dreamt *part* of it on several other occasions; or only remembered part of it on waking.”
“Which part?”
“The first; that awful cavern﻿—”

===Chinese===
「一模一样。」
「除了这两回，您以前从未做过这个梦？」
「另外有几次，我梦到过它的『一部分』；或者说，醒来后只记得一部分。」
「哪一部分？」
「开头那段；那个可怕的洞穴——」

===Original===
“And now, Lady Lashmore﻿—you have recently been present at a spiritualistic séance.”
She was past wondering at his power of inductive reasoning, and merely nodded.
“I suggest﻿—I do not know﻿—that the séance was held under the auspices of Mr. Antony Ferrara, ostensibly for amusement.”
Another affirmative nod answered him.
“You proved to be mediumistic?”
It was admitted.

===Chinese===
「还有，拉什莫尔夫人——您近来出席过一场招魂术的降神会（spiritualistic séance）。」
他这层层推知的本事，已不再引得她惊异，她只点了点头。
「我猜想——我并不确知——那场降神会是安东尼奥·费拉拉（Antony Ferrara）先生一手张罗的，名义上只图个消遣。」
又是一记肯定的颔首，回答了他。
「结果您显出了灵媒之质（mediumistic）？」
她承认了。

===Original===
“And now, Lady Lashmore”﻿—Dr. Cairn’s face was very stern﻿—“I will trouble you no further.”
He prepared to depart; when﻿—
“Dr. Cairn!” whispered Lady Lashmore, tremulously, “some dreadful thing, something that I cannot comprehend but that I fear and loathe with all my soul, has come to me. Oh﻿—for pity’s sake, give me a word of hope! Save for you, I am alone with a horror I cannot name. Tell me﻿—”
At the door, he turned.
“Be brave,” he said﻿—and went out.
Lady Lashmore sat still as one who had looked upon Gorgon, her beautiful eyes yet widely opened and her face pale as death; for he had not even told her to hope.
---

===Chinese===
「那么现在，拉什莫尔夫人」——凯恩博士的神色十分严峻——「我就不再打扰您了。」
他起身预备告辞；就在这时——
「凯恩博士！」拉什莫尔夫人颤声低语，「有个可怕的东西，有个我无法理解、却叫我整个灵魂又怕又憎的东西，缠上了我。哦——发发慈悲，给我一句指望吧！除了您，我孤身一人，守着一个我叫不出名目的恐怖。告诉我——」
到了门口，他回过身。
「勇敢些，」他说——随即走了出去。
拉什莫尔夫人僵坐不动，像一个亲眼见过戈耳工（Gorgon）的人；她那双美丽的眼睛依旧睁得大大的，脸白得像死；因为，他甚至没有劝她存一线希望。
---

===Original===
Robert Cairn was sitting smoking in the library, a bunch of notes before him, when Dr. Cairn returned to Half-Moon Street. His face, habitually fresh coloured, was so pale that his son leapt up in alarm. But Dr. Cairn waved him away with a characteristic gesture of the hand.
“Sit down, Rob,” he said, quietly; “I shall be all right in a moment. But I have just left a woman﻿—a young woman and a beautiful woman﻿—whom a fiend of hell has condemned to that which my mind refuses to contemplate.”
Robert Cairn sat down again, watching his father.
“Make out a report of the following facts,” continued the latter, beginning to pace up and down the room.
He recounted all that he had learnt of the history of the house of Dhoon and all that he had learnt of recent happenings from Lord and Lady Lashmore. His son wrote rapidly.

===Chinese===
凯恩博士回到半月街（Half-Moon Street）时，罗伯特·凯恩（Robert Cairn）正坐在书房里抽烟，面前摊着一叠字条。博士素来红润的脸色苍白得吓人，儿子惊得跳了起来。但凯恩博士以他特有的手势挥了挥手，止住了他。
「坐下，罗布（Rob），」他平静地说，「我一会儿就好。可我刚离开一个女人——一个年轻的女人，一个美丽的女人——一个地狱的恶鬼已把她判给了那样一种下场，连我的头脑都拒绝去细想。」
罗伯特·凯恩重新坐下，望着父亲。
「把下列事实整理成一份报告，」博士接着说，一面开始在屋里踱起步来。
他把打听到的那部杜恩家（house of Dhoon）的家史，连同从拉什莫尔勋爵夫妇口中得知的近期种种变故，一一讲述了一遍。他的儿子奋笔疾书。

===Original===
“And now,” said the doctor, “for our conclusions. Mirza, the Polish Jewess, who became Lady Lashmore in 1615, practised sorcery in life and became, after death, a ghoul﻿—one who sustained an unholy existence by unholy means﻿—a vampire.”
“But, sir! Surely that is but a horrible superstition of the Middle Ages!”
“Rob, I could take you to a castle not ten miles from Krakow in Poland where there are﻿—certain relics, which would forever settle your doubts respecting the existence of vampires. Let us proceed. The son of Mirza, Paul Dhoon, inherited the dreadful proclivities of his mother, but his shadowy existence was cut short in the traditional, and effective, manner. Him we may neglect.
“It is Mirza, the sorceress, who must engage our attention. She was decapitated by her husband. This punishment prevented her, in the unhallowed life which, for such as she, begins after ordinary decease, from practising the horrible rites of a vampire. Her headless body could not serve her as a vehicle for nocturnal wanderings, but the evil spirit of the woman might hope to gain control of some body more suitable.
“Nurturing an implacable hatred against all of the house of Dhoon, that spirit, disembodied, would frequently be drawn to the neighbourhood of Mirza’s descendants, both by hatred and by affinity. Two horrible desires of the Spirit Mirza would be gratified if a Dhoon could be made her victim﻿—the desire for blood and the desire for vengeance! The fate of Lord Lashmore would be sealed if that spirit could secure incarnation!”

===Chinese===
「现在，」博士说，「来说说我们的结论。米尔扎（Mirza），那个 1615 年成为拉什莫尔男爵夫人的波兰犹太女子，生前修习黑巫术，死后化作食尸鬼（ghoul）——一个以不洁手段维持不洁生存的东西——一个吸血鬼。」
「可是，先生！那不过是中世纪一种可怕的迷信罢了！」
「罗布，我能带你去波兰（Poland）克拉科夫（Krakow）城外不到十英里的一座城堡，那里存着——某些遗物，足以永远了结你对吸血鬼是否存在的怀疑。我们继续往下说。米尔扎的儿子保罗·杜恩（Paul Dhoon）承袭了他母亲的可怕癖性，但他那幽影般的性命，早早被人以那种传统的、也是行之有效的方式了结了。这个人，我们可以撇开不论。
「必须叫我们上心的，是女巫米尔扎。她被丈夫斩了首。这一刑罚，使得她在那种亵渎神明的生涯里——对她们这等人而言，寻常的死只是这种生涯的开端——再无法施行吸血鬼的那些可怕仪轨。她那无头的尸身，无法充当她夜间游荡的躯壳，可这女人的邪灵，未必不能指望夺得一副更合适的身子。
「那股离体的幽魂，怀着对杜恩满门斩不尽的刻骨仇恨，又兼血亲的牵引，屡屡被引到米尔扎后人的左近。倘若能叫一个杜恩家的子孙落进她的掌握，米尔扎幽魂的两桩可怖欲念便都得了满足——嗜血之欲与复仇之欲！那幽魂若能夺得一副肉身托形，拉什莫尔勋爵的命运便就此封定！」

===Original===
Dr. Cairn paused, glancing at his son, who was writing at furious speed. Then﻿—
“A magician more mighty and more evil than Mirza ever was or could be,” he continued, “a master of the Black Art, expelled a woman’s spirit from its throne and temporarily installed in its place the bloodlustful spirit of Mirza!”
“My God, sir!” cried Robert Cairn, and threw down his pencil. “I begin to understand!”
“Lady Lashmore,” said Dr. Cairn, “since she was weak enough to consent to be present at a certain séance, has, from time to time, been *possessed*; she has been possessed by the spirit of a vampire! Obedient to the nameless cravings of that control, she has sought out Lord Lashmore, the last of the House of Dhoon. The horrible attack made, a mighty will which, throughout her temporary incarnation, has held her like a hound in leash, has dragged her from her prey, has forced her to remove, from the garments clothing her borrowed body, all traces of the deed, and has cast her out again to the pit of abomination where her headless trunk was thrown by the third Baron Lashmore!
“Lady Lashmore’s brain retains certain memories. They have been received at the moment when possession has taken place and at the moment when the control has been cast out again. They thus are memories of some secret cavern near Dhoon Castle, where that headless but deathless body lies, and memories of the poignant moment when the vampire has been dragged back, her ‘thirst unslaked,’ by the ruling Will.”

===Chinese===
凯恩博士停了停，瞥了一眼正奋笔疾书的儿子。随后——
「一个术士——比米尔扎生前更强、更恶，也远非她所能企及，」他接着说，「一个黑巫术的大师，把一个女人的灵魂从它的王座上逐了出去，又把米尔扎那嗜血成性的幽魂，暂时安插到了那王座之上！」
「我的上帝，先生！」罗伯特·凯恩叫出声来，把铅笔一摔。「我开始明白了！」
「拉什莫尔夫人，」凯恩博士说，「自从她一时软弱，点头答应出席那场降神会，便时不时地遭到『附体』；附上她身的，是一个吸血鬼的幽魂！在那控制灵（control）种种无名渴欲的驱遣下，她寻上了拉什莫尔勋爵——杜恩家最后的传人。那场可怖的袭击得手之后，一股强大的意志——在她暂借肉身的整段期间，始终像皮带拴猎犬一般拴着她——把她从猎物跟前拖开，逼着她从那具借来的身子所穿的衣裳上，抹去那桩罪行的一切痕迹，然后把她重新掷回那座污秽的渊薮——当初第三代拉什莫尔男爵抛下她那无头躯干的地方！」
「拉什莫尔夫人的头脑里留有某些记忆。它们是在附体发生的那一刻、和那控制灵被再度逐出的那一刻接收下来的。因此，那些记忆里，既有杜恩城堡（Dhoon Castle）近旁某处秘密洞穴的景象——那具无头却死不了的身体就躺在那里——也有那吸血鬼被主宰的意志硬生生拽回去时、『焦渴未解』的痛切一刻。」

===Original===
“Merciful God!” muttered Robert Cairn, “Merciful God, can such things be!”
“They can be﻿—they are! Two ways have occurred to me of dealing with the matter,” continued Dr. Cairn quietly. “One is to find that cavern and to kill, in the occult sense, by means of a stake, the vampire who lies there; the other which, I confess, might only result in the permanent ‘possession’ of Lady Lashmore﻿—is to get at the power which controls this disembodied spirit﻿—kill Antony Ferrara!”
Robert Cairn went to the sideboard, and poured out brandy with a shaking hand.
“What’s his object?” he whispered.
Dr. Cairn shrugged his shoulders.
“Lady Lashmore would be the wealthiest widow in society,” he replied.

===Chinese===
「仁慈的上帝！」罗伯特·凯恩喃喃道，「仁慈的上帝，竟会有这种事！」
「会有——就有！我想到了两条处置此事的路子，」凯恩博士平静地接着说。「其一，是找到那处洞穴，用一根木桩，以秘术意义上的死法，了结躺在里面的那个吸血鬼；其二——我承认，它可能只会落得拉什莫尔夫人被永久『附体』——便是直捣控制着这具离魂的那股力量——杀了安东尼奥·费拉拉！」
罗伯特·凯恩走到餐具柜前，用发抖的手倒了一杯白兰地。
「他图的是什么？」他低声问。
凯恩博士耸了耸肩。
「拉什莫尔夫人会成为社交界最有钱的寡妇，」他答道。

===Original===
“*He* will know now,” continued the younger man unsteadily, “that you are up against him. Have you﻿—”
“I have told Lord Lashmore to lock, at night, not only his outer door but also that of his dressing-room. For the rest﻿—?” he dropped into an easy-chair﻿—“I cannot face the facts, I﻿—”
The telephone bell rang.
Dr. Cairn came to his feet as though he had been electrified; and as he raised the receiver to his ear, his son knew, by the expression on his face, from where the message came and something of its purport.
“Come with me,” was all that he said, when he had replaced the instrument on the table.

===Chinese===
「『他』这下该知道了，」年轻人声音发颤地接着说，「知道您已在与他为敌。您可曾——」
「我已嘱咐拉什莫尔勋爵，夜间不但要锁上外门，还要锁上更衣室的门。至于其余——？」他跌坐进一把安乐椅——「那些事实，我不敢去想，我——」
电话铃响了。
凯恩博士像通了电似的霍地站起；他把听筒举到耳边，儿子从他脸上的神情，便知道这通电话来自何处，也猜到了几分内容。
「跟我来，」他把听筒放回桌上后，只说了这一句。

===Original===
They went out together. It was already past midnight, but a cab was found at the corner of Half-Moon Street, and within the space of five minutes they were at Lord Lashmore’s house.
Excepting Chambers, Lord Lashmore’s valet, no servants were to be seen.
“They ran away, sir, out of the house,” explained the man, huskily, “when it happened.”
Dr. Cairn delayed for no further questions, but raced upstairs, his son close behind him. Together they burst into Lord Lashmore’s bedroom. But just within the door they both stopped, aghast.
Sitting bolt upright in bed was Lord Lashmore, his face a dingy grey and his open eyes, though filming over, yet faintly alight with a stark horror﻿ ﻿… dead. An electric torch was still gripped in his left hand.

===Chinese===
两人一同出了门。时间早已过了午夜，好在半月街的街角还叫得到一辆马车，不出五分钟，他们便到了拉什莫尔勋爵的宅邸。
除了钱伯斯（Chambers）——拉什莫尔勋爵的贴身男仆——看不见一个仆人的影子。
「出事的时候，先生，仆人们都吓跑了，逃出宅子去了，」那人沙哑着嗓子解释道。
凯恩博士没再多问一句，径直冲上楼去，儿子紧随其后。两人一起撞开了拉什莫尔勋爵卧室的门。可刚进门内，两人便都骇然站住了。
拉什莫尔勋爵直挺挺地坐在床上，脸色灰败，睁着的两眼虽已蒙上翳影，却仍隐隐燃着一种赤裸裸的惊怖……死了。他左手还紧攥着一只电筒。

===Original===
Bending over someone who lay upon the carpet near the bedside they perceived Sir Elwin Groves. He looked up. Some little of his usual self-possession had fled.
“Ah, Cairn!” he jerked. “We’ve both come too late.”
The prostrate figure was that of Lady Lashmore, a loose kimono worn over her night-robe. She was white and still and the physician had been engaged in bathing a huge bruise upon her temple.
“She’ll be all right,” said Sir Elwin; “she has sustained a tremendous blow, as you see. But Lord Lashmore﻿—”
Dr. Cairn stepped closer to the dead man.

===Chinese===
只见埃尔温·格罗夫斯爵士正俯身在床边地毯上一个躺着的人影上。他抬起头来。他素有的那份镇定，已经丢掉了几分。
「啊，凯恩！」他短促地说。「咱们俩都来晚了一步。」
那倒卧的人影正是拉什莫尔夫人，睡袍外面松松罩着一件和服式的宽大衣衫（kimono）。她面白如纸，一动不动；医生方才一直在替她清洗太阳穴上一大块淤伤。
「她会没事的，」埃尔温爵士说，「你也看见了，她挨了狠狠的一击。可拉什莫尔勋爵——」
凯恩博士走近那死者。

===Original===
“Heart,” he said. “He died of sheer horror.”
He turned to Chambers, who stood in the open doorway behind him.
“The dressing-room door is open,” he said. “I had advised Lord Lashmore to lock it.”
“Yes, sir; his lordship meant to, sir. But we found that the lock had been broken. It was to have been replaced tomorrow.”
Dr. Cairn turned to his son.
“You hear?” he said. “No doubt you have some idea respecting which of the visitors to this unhappy house took the trouble to break that lock? It was to have been replaced tomorrow; hence the tragedy of tonight.” He addressed Chambers again. “Why did the servants leave the house tonight?”

===Chinese===
「心脏，」他说。「他是活活吓死的。」
他转向钱伯斯，后者正站在他身后敞开的门口。
「更衣室的门开着，」他说。「我劝过拉什莫尔勋爵，叫他把那门锁上。」
「是的，先生；老爷本来是打算锁的，先生。可我们发现，那把锁已经被人弄坏了。本来明天就要换新的。」
凯恩博士转向儿子。
「你听见了吗？」他说。「光顾过这座不幸宅子的访客当中，是哪一位费心弄坏了那把锁——想必你多少有了些想法？本来明天就要换新锁；于是便有了今晚的惨剧。」他又向钱伯斯发问。「仆人们今晚为什么离宅而去？」

===Original===
The man was shaking pitifully.
“It was the laughter, sir! the laughter! I can never forget it! I was sleeping in an adjoining room and I had the key of his lordship’s door in case of need. But when I heard his lordship cry out﻿—quick and loud, sir﻿—like a man that’s been stabbed﻿—I jumped up to come to him. Then, as I was turning the doorknob﻿—of my room, sir﻿—someone, something, began to *laugh*! It was in here; it was in here, gentlemen! It wasn’t﻿—her ladyship; it wasn’t like *any* woman. I can’t describe it; but it woke up every soul in the house.”
“When you came in?”
“I daren’t come in, sir! I ran downstairs and called up Sir Elwin Groves. Before he came, all the rest of the household huddled on their clothes and went away﻿—”
“It was I who found him,” interrupted Sir Elwin﻿—“as you see him now; with Lady Lashmore where she lies. I have phoned for nurses.”

===Chinese===
那仆人抖得可怜。
「是那笑声，先生！那笑声！我一辈子都忘不掉！我睡在隔壁房间，手里存着老爷房门的钥匙，以备不时之需。可我听见老爷喊出声来——又急又响，先生——像个挨了刀的人——我便跳下床，要赶过去救他。就在我拧动门把手的时候——我那间房的门把手，先生——有人，有什么东西，开始『笑』了！就在这屋里；就在这屋里，先生们！那不是——夫人；那不像『任何』女人。那是个什么东西，我说不上来；可它把宅里每一个睡着的人都惊醒了。」
「那后来您进去的时候呢？」
「我不敢进去，先生！我跑下楼，打电话请来了埃尔温·格罗夫斯爵士。还没等他赶到，宅里其余的人都胡乱套上衣裳，逃出去了——」
「是我发现他的，」埃尔温爵士插话道——「就是你眼下看到的这副样子；拉什莫尔夫人也躺在她现在躺着的地方。我已经打电话去叫看护了。」

===Original===
“Ah!” said Dr. Cairn; “I shall come back, Groves, but I have a small matter to attend to.”
He drew his son from the room. On the stair:
“You understand?” he asked. “The spirit of Mirza came to him again, clothed in his wife’s body. Lord Lashmore felt the teeth at his throat, awoke instantly and struck out. As he did so, he turned the torch upon her, and recognised﻿—his wife! His heart completed the tragedy, and so﻿—to the laughter of the sorceress﻿—passed the last of the house of Dhoon.”
The cab was waiting. Dr. Cairn gave an address in Piccadilly, and the two entered. As the cab moved off, the doctor took a revolver from his pocket, with some loose cartridges, charged the five chambers, and quietly replaced the weapon in his pocket again.

===Chinese===
「唉！」凯恩博士说，「我还会回来的，格罗夫斯，不过眼下有一件小事要去料理。」
他把儿子拽出房间。在楼梯上：
「你明白了吗？」他问。「米尔扎的幽魂又一次找上他，裹在他妻子的身子里。拉什莫尔勋爵觉出牙齿咬上了喉咙，霎时惊醒，挥拳打了出去。就在这时，他把电筒照向她，认出了——自己的妻子！他的心脏给这场悲剧画上了句点。就这样——在女巫的笑声中——杜恩家最后的传人咽了气。」
马车还在候着。凯恩博士报了个皮卡迪利（Piccadilly）的地址，两人上了车。马车驶动时，博士从口袋里掏出一把左轮手枪，又摸出几发散装的子弹，给五个弹膛一一压上，随即不动声色地把枪放回了口袋。

===Original===
One of the big doors of the block of chambers was found to be ajar, and a porter proved to be yet in attendance.
“Mr. Ferrara?” began Dr. Cairn.
“You are five minutes too late, sir,” said the man. “He left by motor at ten past twelve. He’s gone abroad, sir.”

===Chinese===
那座公寓大楼的一道大门虚掩着，还有一名门房仍在值守。
「费拉拉先生呢？」凯恩博士开口问道。
「您晚了五分钟，先生，」那人说。「他十二点十分乘汽车走了。他出国去了，先生。」

## XI Cairo / XI 开罗

===Original===
The exact manner in which mental stress will effect a man’s physical health is often difficult to predict. Robert Cairn was in the pink of condition at the time that he left Oxford to take up his London appointment; but the tremendous nervous strain wrought upon him by this series of events wholly outside the radius of normal things had broken him up physically, where it might have left unscathed a more highly strung, though less physically vigorous man.

Those who have passed through a nerve storm such as this which had laid him low will know that convalescence seems like a welcome awakening from a dreadful dream. It was indeed in a state between awaking and dreaming that Robert Cairn took counsel with his father—the latter more pale than was his wont and somewhat anxious-eyed—and determined upon an Egyptian rest-cure.

“I have made it all right at the office, Rob,” said Dr. Cairn. “In three weeks or so you will receive instructions at Cairo to write up a series of local articles. Until then, my boy, complete rest and—don’t worry; above all, don’t worry. You and I have passed through a saturnalia of horror, and you, less inured to horrors than I, have gone down. I don’t wonder.”

===Chinese===
精神压力究竟会以怎样的方式损及一个人的身体健康，往往难以预料。罗伯特·凯恩离开牛津赴伦敦就任新职的时候，身体正当巅峰状态；然而，这一连串全然超出常理范畴的事件在他身上造成的巨大神经损耗，硬生生拖垮了他的体魄——若换作一个神经更敏感、体格却不及他强健的人，反倒可能安然无恙。

凡是经历过这样一场将他击倒在地的神经风暴的人都知道，康复期就像从一场可怖的噩梦中欣然苏醒。罗伯特·凯恩正是在这种半醒半梦的状态下与父亲商议——后者脸色比平日苍白，眼里带着几分忧色——定下了去埃及休养疗法的主意。

「办公室那边我都替你安排妥当了，罗布，」凯恩博士说。「三个星期左右，你就会在开罗（Cairo）接到指示，去写一组当地通讯。在那之前，我的孩子，彻底休息，还有——别发愁；千万千万，别发愁。你我刚经历了一场恐怖的狂欢，你对恐怖的耐受力不如我，倒下了。我不觉得奇怪。」

===Original===
“Where is Antony Ferrara?”

Dr. Cairn shook his head and his eyes gleamed with a sudden anger. “For God’s sake don’t mention his name!” he said. “That topic is taboo, Rob. I may tell you, however, that he has left England.”

In this unreal frame of mind, then, and as one but partly belonging to the world of things actual, Cairn found himself an invalid, who but yesterday had been a hale man; found himself shipped for Port Said; found himself entrained for Cairo; and with an awakening to the realities of life, an emerging from an ill-dream to lively interest in the novelties of Egypt, found himself following the red-jerseyed Shepheard’s porter along the corridor of the train and out on to the platform.

A short drive through those singular streets where East meets West and mingles, in the sudden, violet dusk of Lower Egypt, and he was amid the bustle of the popular hotel.

===Chinese===
「安东尼奥·费拉拉在哪儿？」

凯恩博士摇了摇头，眼里蓦地燃起一阵怒火。「看在上帝份上，别提他的名字！」他说。「这个话题是禁忌，罗布。不过我可以告诉你：他已经离开英国了。」

就这样，怀着这种不真切的心境，像个只算半个身子留在现实世界里的人，凯恩发觉自己成了个病号——就在昨天，他还是个结实健壮的人；发觉自己上了开往塞得港（Port Said）的轮船；发觉自己坐上了开赴开罗的火车；而随着对现实生活重新苏醒、从一场噩梦里渐渐浮出、对埃及的种种新奇生出鲜活的兴味，他又发觉自己正跟在一个穿红色针织衫的谢普赫德饭店（Shepheard’s）搬运工身后，沿着火车过道走去，走出车门，踏上站台。

马车驶过那些东方与西方相遇交融的奇异街道，在下埃及骤然降临的紫色暮霭中行了短短一程，他便置身于这家宾客盈门的饭店的喧腾之中了。

===Original===
Sime was there, whom he had last seen at Oxford, Sime the phlegmatic. He apologised for not meeting the train, but explained that his duties had rendered it impossible. Sime was attached temporarily to an archaeological expedition as medical man, and his athletic and somewhat bovine appearance contrasted oddly with the unhealthy gauntness of Cairn.

“I only got in from Wasta ten minutes ago, Cairn. You must come out to the camp when I return; the desert air will put you on your feet again in no time.”

Sime was unemotional, but there was concern in his voice and in his glance, for the change in Cairn was very startling. Although he knew something, if but very little, of certain happenings in London—gruesome happenings centering around the man called Antony Ferrara—he avoided any reference to them at the moment.

===Chinese===
西姆正在那里——凯恩上次见到他还是在牛津，还是那个不动声色的西姆。他为没去车站迎车道了歉，解释说公务在身，实在无法分身。西姆当时临时附在一家考古探险队里当队医，他那运动员似的、又颇有几分牛一般敦实的仪表，与凯恩病态的清瘦恰成古怪的对照。

「我十分钟前才从瓦斯塔（Wasta）赶回来，凯恩。等我返回营地的时候，你一定得去住上一阵；沙漠的空气保管叫你很快重新站起来。」

西姆不动感情，可他的嗓音里、目光中都含着关切，因为凯恩的变化着实惊人。伦敦的某些事变——围绕着那个名叫安东尼奥·费拉拉的人发生的一桩桩可怕事变——他虽然略有所闻，其实所知极少，此刻便只字不提。

===Original===
Seated upon the terrace, Robert Cairn studied the busy life in the street below with all the interest of a new arrival in the Capital of the Near East. More than ever, now, his illness and the things which had led up to it seemed to belong to a remote dream existence. Through the railings at his feet a hawker was thrusting fly-whisks, and imploring him in complicated English to purchase one. Vendors of beads, of fictitious “antiques,” of sweetmeats, of whatnot; fortune-tellers—and all that chattering horde which some obscure process of gravitation seems to hurl against the terrace of Shepheard’s, buzzed about him. Carriages and motor cars, camels and donkeys mingled, in the Shâria Kâmel Pasha. Voices American, voices Anglo-Saxon, guttural German tones, and softly murmured Arabic merged into one indescribable chord of sound; but to Robert Cairn it was all unspeakably restful. He was quite contented to sit there sipping his whisky and soda, and smoking his pipe. Sheer idleness was good for him and exactly what he wanted, and idling amid that unique throng is idleness *de luxe*.

Sime watched him covertly, and saw that his face had acquired lines—lines which told of the fires through which he had passed. Something, it was evident—something horrible—had seared his mind. Considering the many indications of tremendous nervous disaster in Cairn, Sime wondered how near his companion had come to insanity, and concluded that he had stood upon the frontiers of that grim land of phantoms, and had only been plucked back in the eleventh hour.

Cairn glanced around with a smile, from the group of hawkers who solicited his attention upon the pavement below.

===Chinese===
罗伯特·凯恩坐在露台上，怀着初到近东之都的人特有的十足兴致，观览楼下街市的繁忙景象。到了此刻，他的病、以及引出这场病的种种事端，比以往任何时候都更像是属于一段遥远的梦中生涯。栏杆外，一个小贩把驱蝇拂直递到他脚前，用百转千回的英语恳求他买上一把。卖珠子的、卖假「古董」的、卖糖果蜜饯的、卖杂七杂八小玩意儿的，还有算命的——这一大群喋喋不休的人马，仿佛被某种说不清道不明的引力甩掷到谢普赫德饭店的露台跟前，围着他嗡嗡营营。凯梅尔帕夏街（Shâria Kâmel Pasha）上，马车与汽车、骆驼与毛驴混杂一处。美国腔、盎格鲁-撒克逊腔、喉音沉沉的德语，还有呢喃轻软的阿拉伯语，汇成一支难以名状的和声；然而在罗伯特·凯恩听来，这一切却安神舒泰得无以言表。他心满意足地坐在那里，呷着威士忌苏打，抽着烟斗。纯粹的无所事事对他有好处，也正中他的下怀；而在那样独一无二的人潮里偷闲，堪称『de luxe』（法文：顶级奢华）的闲适。

西姆暗暗观察着他，看出他脸上添了纹路——一道道诉说着他从中穿行而过的烈火的纹路。显然，有什么东西——某种可怕的东西——灼伤了他的心智。看着凯恩身上那许多神经大损的迹象，西姆暗忖这位同伴究竟曾离疯癫有多近，最后得出结论：他曾在那片阴惨的幻影之国的边界上驻足，只是在最后关头才被人拽了回来。

凯恩含笑从下面人行道上那群拉他生意的小贩身上收回目光，环顾四周。

===Original===
“This is a delightful scene,” he said. “I could sit here for hours; but considering that it’s some time after sunset it remains unusually hot, doesn’t it?”

“Rather!” replied Sime. “They are expecting khamsin—the hot wind, you know. I was up the river a week ago and we struck it badly in Assouan. It grew as black as night and one couldn’t breathe for sand. It’s probably working down to Cairo.”

“From your description I am not anxious to make the acquaintance of khamsin!”

===Chinese===
「这景象真令人愉快，」他说。「在这儿坐上几个钟头我都愿意；只是日落已有些时辰，天还是热得出奇，不是吗？」

「可不是！」西姆答道。「他们正盼着喀新风（khamsin）呢——就是那种热风，你知道。一星期前我溯河而上，在阿斯旺（Assouan）狠狠领教了它一场。天黑得像深夜，沙子呛得人喘不上气。眼下它多半正朝开罗一路刮下来。」

「听你这番描述，我可无意结识什么喀新风！」

===Original===
Sime shook his head, knocking out his pipe into the ashtray.

“This is a funny country,” he said reflectively. “The most weird ideas prevail here to this day—ideas which properly belong to the Middle Ages. For instance”—he began to recharge the hot bowl—“it is not really time for khamsin, consequently the natives feel called upon to hunt up some explanation of its unexpected appearance. Their ideas on the subject are interesting, if idiotic. One of our Arabs (we are excavating in the Fayûm, you know), solemnly assured me yesterday that the hot wind had been caused by an Afreet, a sort of Arabian Nights’ demon, who has arrived in Egypt!”

He laughed gruffly, but Cairn was staring at him with a curious expression. Sime continued:

===Chinese===
西姆摇了摇头，把烟斗在烟灰缸里磕空。

「这是个古怪的国家，」他若有所思地说。「直到今天，这里还盛行着最离奇的想法——些按理只该属于中世纪的念头。比如说」——他往滚烫的斗钵里重新装着烟丝——「眼下其实还没到喀新风的时节，于是当地人便觉得非得寻出个缘由，来解释它这不期而至。他们在这题目上的想法挺有意思，虽说蠢得可以。我们队里的一个阿拉伯人（你知道，我们正在法尤姆（Fayûm）发掘）昨天一本正经地向我担保：这阵热风是一个阿弗里特（Afreet）——《一千零一夜》里那类恶魔——兴起来的，说他已经到埃及来了！」

他粗声粗气地笑了，凯恩却神情异样地盯着他。西姆接着说：

===Original===
“When I got to Cairo this evening I found news of the Afreet had preceded me. Honestly, Cairn, it is all over the town—the native town, I mean. All the shopkeepers in the Mûski are talking about it. If a puff of khamsin should come, I believe they would permanently shut up shop and hide in their cellars—if they have any! I am rather hazy on modern Egyptian architecture.”

Cairn nodded his head absently.

“You laugh,” he said, “but the active force of a superstition—what we call a superstition—is sometimes a terrible thing.”

===Chinese===
「今晚我一到开罗，就发现阿弗里特的传闻早已先我而至。说实在的，凯恩，这事已经传遍全城——我是说本地人的城区。穆斯基街（Mûski）上的店家个个都在谈论它。倘使喀新风真刮来一口，我相信他们会干脆永久歇业，躲进自家的地窖里去——如果他们有地窖的话！现代埃及的建筑样式，我实在不甚了然。」

凯恩心不在焉地点了点头。

「你笑归笑，」他说，「可一种迷信——就是我们所谓的迷信——一旦活动起来，那力量有时是桩可怕的事。」

===Original===
Sime stared.

“Eh!” The medical man had suddenly come uppermost; he recollected that this class of discussion was probably taboo.

“You may doubt the existence of Afreets,” continued Cairn, “but neither you nor I can doubt the creative power of thought. If a trained hypnotist, by sheer concentration, can persuade his subject that the latter sits upon the brink of a river fishing when actually he sits upon a platform in a lecture-room, what result should you expect from a concentration of thousands of native minds upon the idea that an Afreet is visiting Egypt?”

===Chinese===
西姆瞪眼望着他。

「呃！」医生的一面突然占了上风；他记起这类讨论多半属于禁忌。

「你可以不信阿弗里特的存在，」凯恩继续说，「但你我都无法怀疑思想的创造力。倘若一名训练有素的催眠师单凭凝神专注，就能叫他的受术者深信自己正坐在河边垂钓——而实际上他坐在讲堂的台上——那么，成千上万颗当地人的心神全都凝注在『一个阿弗里特正在降临埃及』这个念头上，你以为会有什么结果？」

===Original===
Sime stared in a dull way peculiar to him.

“Rather a poser,” he said. “I have a glimmer of a notion what you mean.”

“Don’t you think—”

===Chinese===
西姆以一种他特有的木然神情瞪视着。

「这话真有点把人问住了，」他说。「你的意思，我约略明白几分。」

「你不觉得——」

===Original===
“If you mean don’t I think the result would be the creation of an Afreet, no, I don’t!”

“I hardly mean that, either,” replied Cairn, “but this wave of superstition cannot be entirely unproductive; all that thought energy directed to one point—”

Sime stood up.

“We shall get out of our depth,” he replied conclusively. He considered the ground of discussion an unhealthy one; this was the territory adjoining that of insanity.

===Chinese===
「要是你想问的是：我认为结果会不会当真造出一个阿弗里特来——不，我不这么认为！」

「倒也不全是这个意思，」凯恩答道，「可这股迷信的浪潮决不会全无结果；那么多思想能量统统指向一个点——」

西姆站起身来。

「再谈下去，咱们就要蹚进深水了。」他斩钉截铁地说。他认为这片论域有碍健康；那可是与疯癫接壤的地带。

===Original===
A fortune-teller from India proffered his services incessantly.

“*Imshi! imshi!*” growled Sime.

“Hold on,” said Cairn smiling; “this chap is not an Egyptian; let us ask him if he has heard the rumour respecting the Afreet!”

Sime reseated himself rather unwillingly. The fortune-teller spread his little carpet and knelt down in order to read the palm of his hypothetical client, but Cairn waved him aside.

===Chinese===
一个从印度来的算命师不住地凑上前来兜揽生意。

『Imshi! imshi!』（走开！走开！）西姆低声吼道。

「且慢，」凯恩含笑道；「这家伙不是埃及人；咱们问问他听没听说过关于阿弗里特的传闻！」

西姆老大不情愿地重新落座。算命师铺开他的小地毯，跪下来要给这位假想的主顾看手相，凯恩却挥手把他撇开。

===Original===
“I don’t want my fortune told!” he said; “but I will give you your fee,”—with a smile at Sime—“for a few minutes’ conversation.”

“Yes, sir, yes, sir!” The Indian was all attention.

“Why”—Cairn pointed forensically at the fortune-teller—“why is khamsin come so early this year?”

The Indian spread his hands, palms upward.

===Chinese===
「我不要算命！」他说；「不过我愿意付你卦金」——说着朝西姆一笑——「买你几分钟的谈话。」

「是的，先生，是的，先生！」那印度人顿时凝神恭听。

「请问」——凯恩像法庭质证似的指着算命师——「今年的喀新风为什么来得这样早？」

那印度人摊开双手，掌心朝上。

===Original===
“How should I know?” he replied in his soft, melodious voice. “I am not of Egypt; I can only say what is told to me by the Egyptians.”

“And what is told to you?”

Sime rested his hands upon his knees, bending forward curiously. He was palpably anxious that Cairn should have confirmation of the Afreet story from the Indian.

“They tell me, sir,”—the man’s voice sank musically low—“that a thing very evil”—he tapped a long brown finger upon his breast—“not as I am”—he tapped Sime upon the knee—“not as he, your friend”—he thrust the long finger at Cairn—“not as you, sir; not a man at all, though something like a man! not having any father and mother—”

===Chinese===
「我怎么会知道？」他以柔和悦耳的声音答道。「我不是埃及人；埃及人对我讲什么，我只能照着说什么。」

「那他们对你讲了什么？」

西姆双手按在膝上，好奇地俯身向前。他明摆着急于让凯恩从这印度人口中得到阿弗里特传闻的印证。

「他们告诉我，先生，」——那人的声音如乐曲般低沉下来——「有一个极其邪恶的东西」——他用一根细长的棕色手指点了点自己的胸膛——「不像我这样」——他又点了点西姆的膝盖——「也不像他，您的这位朋友」——那根长手指直指向凯恩——「也不像您，先生；压根儿不是人，虽然生得有几分像人！没有父亲，也没有母亲——」

===Original===
“You mean,” suggested Sime, “a spirit?”

The fortune-teller shook his head.

“They tell me, sir, not a spirit—a man, but not as other men; a very, very bad man; one that the great king, long, long ago, the king you call Wise—”

“Solomon?” suggested Cairn.

“Yes, yes, Suleyman!—one that he, when he banish all the tribe of the demons from earth—one that he not found.”

===Chinese===
「你是说，」西姆提示道，「一个精灵？」

算命师摇了摇头。

「他们告诉我，先生，不是精灵——是人，可又跟别的人不同；一个非常非常坏的人；很久很久以前，有一位伟大的王——你们称作智者的那位王——」

「所罗门？」凯恩提示道。

「对，对，苏莱曼（Suleyman）！——当年他把鬼魔那一族统统逐出大地的时候——独独这一个，他没有找到。」

===Original===
“One he overlooked?” jerked Sime.

“Yes, yes, overlook! A very evil man, my gentlemen. They tell me he has come to Egypt. He come not from the sea, but across the great desert—”

“The Libyan Desert?” suggested Sime.

The man shook, his head, seeking for words.

===Chinese===
「漏掉的一个？」西姆猛地插嘴。

「对，对，漏掉了！一个非常坏的人，两位先生。他们告诉我，他到埃及来了。他不是从海路来的，是横穿大沙漠来的——」

「利比亚沙漠？」西姆提示道。

那人摇着头，苦苦搜寻着字眼。

===Original===
“The Arabian Desert?”

“No, no! Away beyond, far up in Africa”—he waved his long arms dramatically—“far, far up beyond the Sûdan.”

“The Sahara Desert?” proposed Sime.

“Yes, yes! it is Sahara Desert!—come across the Sahara Desert, and is come to Khartûm.”

===Chinese===
「阿拉伯沙漠？」

「不，不！还要往外，远在非洲腹地」——他戏剧性地挥舞着长臂——「远远地，远在苏丹（Sûdan）以外。」

「撒哈拉沙漠？」西姆试着问。

「对，对！就是撒哈拉沙漠！——横穿撒哈拉沙漠过来的，已经到了喀土穆（Khartûm）。」

===Original===
“How did he get there?” asked Cairn.

The Indian shrugged his shoulders.

“I cannot say, but next he come to Wady Halfa, then he is in Assouan, and from Assouan he come down to Luxor! Yesterday an Egyptian friend told me khamsin is in the Fayûm. Therefore *he* is there—the man of evil—for he bring the hot wind with him.”

The Indian was growing impressive, and two American tourists stopped to listen to his words.

===Chinese===
「他是怎么到那里的？」凯恩问。

印度人耸了耸肩。

「这我说不上；反正接下来他到了瓦迪哈勒法（Wady Halfa），随后现身阿斯旺，又从阿斯旺顺流而下到了卢克索（Luxor）！昨天一位埃及朋友告诉我，喀新风正在法尤姆。所以『他』就在那里——那个恶人——因为热风是他随身携来的。」

那印度人越说越动人心魄，两个美国游客停下脚步听他讲述。

===Original===
“Tonight—tomorrow,”—he spoke now almost in a whisper, glancing about him as if apprehensive of being overheard—“he may be here, in Cairo, bringing with him the scorching breath of the desert—the scorpion wind!”

He stood up, casting off the mystery with which he had invested his story, and smiling insinuatingly. His work was done; his fee was due. Sime rewarded him with five piastres, and he departed, bowing.

“You know, Sime—” Cairn began to speak, staring absently the while after the fortune-teller, as he descended the carpeted steps and rejoined the throng on the sidewalk below—“you know, if a man—anyone, could take advantage of such a wave of thought as this which is now sweeping through Egypt—if he could cause it to concentrate upon him, as it were, don’t you think that it would enable him to transcend the normal, to do phenomenal things?”

===Chinese===
「今夜——明天，」——此刻他几乎是耳语了，一边左右张望，仿佛怕人偷听——「他也许就到这里了，来到开罗，随身携着沙漠灼人的呼吸——蝎风！」

他站起身来，卸去方才给故事披上的神秘外衣，换上一副曲意讨好的笑容。他的活计做完了；该付酬金了。西姆赏了他 5 皮阿斯特（piastres），他便打躬作揖地去了。

「你知道，西姆——」凯恩开口说道，一面茫然地目送那算命师走下铺地毯的台阶、重新汇入下面人行道上的人潮——「你知道，倘若一个人——随便哪个人——能乘上眼下正横扫埃及的这样一股思潮之力——倘若他能叫这股思潮凝聚到自己身上，譬如这么说——你不觉得，这就能让他超越常轨，干出惊世骇俗的事情来吗？」

===Original===
“By what process should you propose to make yourself such a focus?”

“I was speaking impersonally, Sime. It might be possible—”

“It might be possible to dress for dinner,” snapped Sime, “if we shut up talking nonsense! There’s a carnival here tonight; great fun. Suppose we concentrate our brainwaves on another Scotch and soda?”

===Chinese===
「那你打算用什么法子，把自己变成这样一个焦点呢？」

「我说的并非我自己，西姆。这或许是有可能的——」

「要是咱们闭上嘴不再胡说八道，倒是有可能赶上换礼服吃晚饭呢！」西姆没好气地说。「今晚这儿有一场狂欢会，热闹得很。咱们把脑波集中到再来一杯苏格兰威士忌苏打上，怎么样？」

## XII The Mask of Set / XII 赛特的面具

===Original===
Above the palm trees swept the jewelled vault of Egypt’s sky, and set amid the clustering leaves gleamed little red electric lamps; fairy lanterns outlined the winding paths and paper Japanese lamps hung dancing in long rows, whilst in the centre of the enchanted garden a fountain spurned diamond spray high in the air, to fall back coolly plashing into the marble home of the golden carp. The rustling of innumerable feet upon the sandy pathway and the ceaseless murmur of voices, with pealing laughter rising above all, could be heard amid the strains of the military band ensconced in a flower-covered arbour.

Into the brightly lighted places and back into the luminous shadows came and went fantastic forms. Sheikhs there were with flowing robes, dragomans who spoke no Arabic, Sultans and priests of Ancient Egypt, going arm-in-arm. Dancing girls of old Thebes, and harem ladies in silken trousers and high-heeled red shoes. Queens of Babylon and Cleopatras, many Geishas and desert Gypsies mingled, specks in a giant kaleidoscope. The thick carpet of confetti rustled to the tread; girls ran screaming before those who pursued them armed with handfuls of the tiny paper disks. Pipers of a Highland regiment marched piping through the throng, their Scottish kilts seeming wildly incongruous amid such a scene. Within the hotel, where the mosque lanterns glowed, one might catch a glimpse of the heads of dancers gliding shadowlike.

“A tremendous crowd,” said Sime, “considering it is nearly the end of the season.”

===Chinese===
棕榈树梢之上，横展着埃及那缀珠嵌玉般的夜空穹顶；簇簇枝叶间，闪烁着一盏盏小小的红色电灯；仙子似的灯笼勾勒出蜿蜒的小径，日式纸灯一排排长长地悬垂轻舞；而在这座恍如幻境的花园中央，一座喷泉把钻石似的水花高高踢上空中，复又清凉地回落，淙淙溅入金鲤安身的大理石池中。军乐队驻在一座繁花覆盖的凉亭里，乐声悠扬；其间听得见无数双脚踏过沙土小径的沙沙声，听得见绵延不绝的人语低嗡，还有盖过这一切的阵阵朗笑。

明亮灯下、幽光朦胧的暗影里，奇装异服的身影来来往往。有长袍飘垂的谢赫（Sheikh），有压根不会说阿拉伯语的译导（dragoman），有挽臂同行的苏丹和古埃及祭司。有古都底比斯（Thebes）的舞姬，有穿绸裤、蹬红色高跟鞋的后宫佳丽。巴比伦的王后们和一个个克利奥帕特拉、众多的艺伎和沙漠里的吉卜赛人混作一处，像一只巨大万花筒里的点点碎彩。厚厚的五彩纸屑地毯踩上去窸窣作响；姑娘们尖声叫着奔逃，后面追的人手里握着大把大把的小圆纸片。一支苏格兰高地团队的风笛手一路吹奏着穿过人群，他们的苏格兰短褶裙在这样的场面中显得格外荒诞刺眼。饭店里面，清真寺灯盏荧荧发光，隐约可见舞者们的头影如鬼魅般悄然滑行。

「人可真不少，」西姆说，「想想看，眼下都快到旺季的尾声了。」

===Original===
Three silken ladies wearing gauzy white yashmaks confronted Cairn and the speaker. A gleaming of jewelled fingers there was and Cairn found himself half-choked with confetti, which filled his eyes, his nose, his ears, and of which quite a liberal amount found access to his mouth. The three ladies of the yashmak ran screaming from their vengeance-seeking victims, Sime pursuing two, and Cairn hard upon the heels of the third. Amid this scene of riotous carnival all else was forgotten, and only the madness, the infectious madness of the night, claimed his mind. In and out of the strangely attired groups darted his agile quarry, all but captured a score of times, but always eluding him.

Sime he had hopelessly lost, as around fountain and flowerbed, arbour and palm trunk he leapt in pursuit of the elusive yashmak.

Then, in a shadowed corner of the garden, he trapped her. Plunging his hand into the bag of confetti, which he carried, he leapt, exulting, to his revenge: when a sudden gust of wind passed sibilantly through the palm tops, and glancing upward, Cairn saw that the blue sky was overcast and the stars gleaming dimly, as through a veil. That moment of hesitancy proved fatal to his project, for with a little excited scream the girl dived under his outstretched arm and fled back towards the fountain. He turned to pursue again, when a second puff of wind, stronger than the first, set waving the palm fronds and showered dry leaves upon the confetti carpet of the garden. The band played loudly, the murmur of conversation rose to something like a roar, but above it whistled the increasing breeze, and there was a sort of grittiness in the air.

===Chinese===
三位披着轻薄白色亚什马克（yashmak）面纱的绸衣女郎，迎面拦住了凯恩和说话的人。珠光宝气的手指一闪，凯恩便觉得自己被五彩纸屑呛得半死——纸屑灌满了他的眼睛、鼻子、耳朵，还有相当可观的一把直进了他的嘴。三位面纱女郎尖笑着，从前来寻仇的受害人身边跑开，西姆追两个，凯恩对第三个紧追不舍。在这片恣意狂欢的乱阵中，其余一切都被忘到了脑后，唯有那股疯狂——那一夜里传染开来的疯狂——占据了他的头脑。他那身手矫健的猎物在奇装异服的人群中东躲西闪，眼看得手不下二十回，却回回都叫她溜了。

西姆他早就追丢了；他绕着喷泉与花坛、凉亭与棕榈树干一路蹦跳腾挪，追赶那个飘忽难捉的面纱女郎。

后来，在花园一处背阴的角落里，他把她逼住了。他把手插进随身提着的纸屑袋，得意洋洋地一跃而起，正要施行报复：恰在此时，一阵风嘶地掠过棕榈树梢，凯恩抬眼一望，只见蓝天已经阴沉下来，星光黯淡，仿佛隔着一层薄纱。就是这迟疑的一瞬断送了他的大计——那姑娘一声又惊又喜的尖叫，从他张开的手臂底下钻过去，朝喷泉方向逃了回去。他转身要追，第二阵风又扑面而来，比头一阵更猛，吹得棕榈叶翻卷摇曳，把枯叶簌簌地抖落在花园的纸屑地毯上。乐队高声奏乐，人语的嗡嗡声涨成近乎咆哮的轰响，但盖过这一切的，是越刮越紧的风声呼啸，空气里带上了某种沙涩的粗粝。

===Original===
Then, proclaimed by a furious lashing of the fronds above, burst the wind in all its fury. It seemed to beat down into the garden in waves of heat. Huge leaves began to fall from the tree tops and the mast-like trunks bent before the fury from the desert. The atmosphere grew hazy with impalpable dust; and the stars were wholly obscured.

Commenced a stampede from the garden. Shrill with fear, rose a woman’s scream from the heart of the throng:

“A scorpion! a scorpion!”

Panic threatened, but fortunately the doors were wide, so that, without disaster the whole fantastic company passed into the hotel; and even the military band retired.

Cairn perceived that he alone remained in the garden, and glancing along the path in the direction of the fountain, he saw a blotchy drab creature, fully four inches in length, running zigzag towards him. It was a huge scorpion; but, even as he leapt forward to crush it, it turned and crept in amid the tangle of flowers beside the path, where it was lost from view.

===Chinese===
紧接着，头顶棕榈叶一阵狂怒的鞭打宣告了它的降临——狂风挟着全部凶暴迸发了。它仿佛化作一波波热浪，朝花园里狠狠压下来。巨大的叶片开始从树顶坠落，桅杆似的树干在来自沙漠的暴怒面前弯下了腰。空气因无形无质的尘土而变得迷蒙；星辰完全隐没了。

花园里开始了一场大溃逃。人群正中，响起一个女人惊怖尖利的叫喊：

「蝎子！蝎子！」

惊乱眼看就要酿成，幸而门户大开，于是这满园奇装异服的人马得以安然无恙地涌进饭店；连军乐队也撤了进去。

凯恩察觉只剩自己一个人留在花园里了；顺着小径朝喷泉的方向望去，他看见一只灰褐斑驳的东西，足有 4 英寸长，之字形地朝他跑来。那是一只巨大的蝎子；可就在他跃身上前要踩死它的一刹那，它一转身，钻进了小径旁纠结的花丛，没了踪影。

===Original===
The scorching wind grew momentarily fiercer, and Cairn, entering behind a few straggling revellers, found something ominous and dreadful in its sudden fury. At the threshold, he turned and looked back upon the gaily lighted garden. The paper lamps were thrashing in the wind, many extinguished; others were in flames; a number of electric globes fell from their fastenings amid the palm tops, and burst bomb-like upon the ground. The pleasure garden was now a battlefield, beset with dangers, and he fully appreciated the anxiety of the company to get within doors. Where chrysanthemum and yashmak turban and tarboosh, uraeus and Indian plume had mingled gaily, no soul remained; but yet﻿—he was in error﻿ … someone did remain.

As if embodying the fear that in a few short minutes had emptied the garden, out beneath the waving lanterns, the flying debris, the whirling dust, pacing sombrely from shadow to light, and to shadow again, advancing towards the hotel steps, came the figure of one sandalled, and wearing the short white tunic of Ancient Egypt. His arms were bare, and he carried a long staff; but rising hideously upon his shoulders was a crocodile-mask, which seemed to grin﻿—the mask of Set, Set the Destroyer, God of the underworld.

Cairn, alone of all the crowd, saw the strange figure, for the reason that Cairn alone faced towards the garden. The gruesome mask seemed to fascinate him; he could not take his gaze from that weird advancing god; he felt impelled hypnotically to stare at the gleaming eyes set in the saurian head. The mask was at the foot of the steps, and still Cairn stood rigid. When, as the sandalled foot was set upon the first step, a breeze, dust-laden, and hot as from a furnace door, blew fully into the hotel, blinding him. A chorus arose from the crowd at his back; and many voices cried out for doors to be shut. Someone tapped him on the shoulder, and spun him about.

===Chinese===
灼热的风一刻凶似一刻，凯恩跟在几个掉队的寻欢客身后走进饭店，只觉得这风的骤然狂暴里含着某种不祥的、可怖的东西。在门槛上，他回过头，望着那座灯火欢腾的花园。纸灯在风里猛烈抽打，许多已经熄灭，另一些已燃起火苗；好几只电灯泡从棕榈树梢的固定位上坠落，像炸弹似的在地上炸开。这座欢乐花园此刻成了危机四伏的战场，他深深体会到了众人急于躲进屋里的心情。菊花冠饰、亚什马克头巾、塔布什帽（tarboosh）、圣蛇标（uraeus）与印度羽翎曾经欢然杂处的地方，此刻一个人也不剩了；然而——他想错了……确还有一个人留在那里。

仿佛那短短几分钟内把花园吓得空无一人的恐惧，凝成了人形：在翻卷的灯笼下、纷飞的碎屑中、回旋的尘土里，一个身影从暗处踱入光中，又没回暗处，阴沉沉地朝着饭店的台阶走来。那身影脚穿凉鞋，身披古埃及式的白色短衣。他双臂赤裸，手拄一根长杖；而狰狞地耸立在他双肩之上的，是一副鳄鱼面具，那面具仿佛在狞笑——那是赛特（Set）的面具：毁灭者赛特，冥界之神。

满场宾客中，唯独凯恩看见了那奇怪的身影，原因很简单：唯独凯恩是面朝花园站着的。那狰狞的面具仿佛慑住了他，他无法把目光从那步步逼近的怪神身上挪开；他像受了催眠一般，身不由己地死盯着镶在鳄形头颅上那双灼灼放光的眼睛。面具已经到了台阶脚下，凯恩却仍僵立不动。直到那只穿凉鞋的脚踏上第一级台阶，一阵风——满载尘土、热得像从炉门里喷出来——猛地灌进饭店，迷住了他的眼睛。他背后的人群齐声惊叫起来；许多嗓音喊着要关门。有人拍了拍他的肩膀，把他的身子猛地拨转过来。

===Original===
“By God!”﻿—it was Sime who now had him by the arm﻿—“khamsin has come with a vengeance! They tell me that they have never had anything like it!”

The native servants were closing and fastening the doors. The night was now as black as Erebus, and the wind was howling about the building with the voices of a million lost souls. Cairn glanced back across his shoulder. Men were drawing heavy curtains across the doors and windows.

“They have shut him out, Sime!” he said.

Sime stared in his dull fashion.

===Chinese===
「天哪！」——抓住他胳膊的正是西姆——「喀新风这回来势汹汹，狠狠地发作了！听他们说，这样厉害的阵仗，他们还从来没有见识过！」

本地仆役们正在关门落闩。夜此刻黑得如同厄瑞玻斯（Erebus，冥晦），风绕着楼房呼啸，带着千百万个迷失魂灵的声音。凯恩回头瞥了一眼。人们正把厚重的帘幕拉上，遮住一扇扇门窗。

「他们把他关在外面了，西姆！」他说。

西姆以一种他特有的木然神情瞪视着他。

===Original===
“You surely saw him?” persisted Cairn irritably; “the man in the mask of Set﻿—he was coming in just behind me.”

Sime strode forward, pulled the curtains aside, and peered out into the deserted garden.

“Not a soul, old man,” he declared. “You must have seen the Afreet!”

===Chinese===
「你总该看见他了吧？」凯恩焦躁地追问；「那个戴赛特面具的人——他正好就跟在我身后要进来。」

西姆大步上前，拉开帘幕，朝那座空荡荡的花园里窥望。

「一个人影也没有，老兄，」他断言道。「你看见的准是阿弗里特！」

## XIII The Scorpion Wind / XIII 蝎风

===Original===
This sudden and appalling change of weather had sadly affected the mood of the gathering. That part of the carnival planned to take place in the garden was perforce abandoned, together with the firework display. A halfhearted attempt was made at dancing, but the howling of the wind, and the omnipresent dust, perpetually reminded the pleasure-seekers that khamsin raged without﻿—raged with a violence unparalleled in the experience of the oldest residents. This was a full-fledged sandstorm, a terror of the Sahara descended upon Cairo.
But there were few departures, although many of the visitors who had long distances to go, especially those from Mena House, discussed the advisability of leaving before this unique storm should have grown even worse. The general tendency, though, was markedly gregarious; safety seemed to be with the crowd, amid the gaiety, where music and laughter were, rather than in the sand-swept streets.

===Chinese===
这场骤然降临、骇人听闻的天气剧变，把聚会的兴致败坏得不轻。原定在花园里举行的那部分狂欢活动只得作罢，焰火表演也一并取消。跳舞倒还勉强试了一阵，然而风声的呼啸、无处不在的尘沙，时刻提醒着这些寻欢作乐的人：外面喀新风（khamsin）正在肆虐——肆虐之烈，连最年长的老住户也平生未见。这是一场不折不扣的大沙暴，是撒哈拉沙漠（Sahara）的恐怖降临开罗。
不过真正离去的客人寥寥无几，虽说许多要赶远路的来客——尤其是从米娜宫饭店（Mena House）来的那些——已在商量，是否该趁这场独一无二的风暴还没变得更糟之前先行离去。然而总的趋向分明是人趋群而居：安乐似乎在人群之中、在欢愉之间、在有音乐和笑声的地方，而不在黄沙横扫的街头上。

===Original===
“Guess we’ve outstayed our welcome!” confided an American lady to Sime. “Egypt wants to drive us all home now.”
“Possibly,” he replied with a smile. “The season has run very late, this year, and so this sort of thing is more or less to be expected.”
The orchestra struck up a lively one-step, and a few of the more enthusiastic dancers accepted the invitation, but the bulk of the company thronged around the edge of the floor, acting as spectators.

===Chinese===
「看来我们待得人家都嫌了！」一位美国太太对西姆（Sime）吐露道。「埃及这就要撵我们大家回家了。」
「也许吧，」他含笑答道。「今年的旺季拖得太晚，这种天气多少也在意料之中。」
乐队奏起一支轻快的一步舞曲（one-step），几位兴致较高的舞客应邀下了场，但宾客中的大多数仍挤在舞池边上，充当看客。

===Original===
Cairn and Sime wedged a way through the heterogeneous crowd to the American Bar.
“I prescribe a ‘tango,’ ” said Sime.
“A ‘tango’ is﻿—?”
“A ‘tango,’ ” explained Sime, “is a new kind of cocktail sacred to this buffet. Try it. It will either kill you or cure you.”

===Chinese===
凯恩（Cairn）与西姆在五方杂处的人群里挤出一条路，来到美国酒吧（American Bar）。
「我开一味『探戈』，」西姆说。
「『探戈』是——？」
「『探戈』嘛，」西姆解释道，「是这家酒吧特供的一种新式鸡尾酒。尝尝看。它要么治得了你，要么要得了你的命。」

===Original===
Cairn smiled rather wanly.
“I must confess that I need bucking up a bit,” he said: “that confounded sand seems to have got me by the throat.”
Sime briskly gave his orders to the bar attendant.
“You know,” pursued Cairn, “I cannot get out of my head the idea that there was someone wearing a crocodile mask in the garden a while ago.”

===Chinese===
凯恩笑得颇为惨淡。
「我得承认，我确实需要提一提神，」他说，「那该死的沙子像是掐住了我的喉咙。」
西姆利落地向酒保吩咐了酒水。
「你知道，」凯恩接着说，「我心里总甩不掉一个念头：方才花园里有个戴鳄鱼面具的人。」

===Original===
“Look here,” growled Sime, studying the operations of the cocktail manufacturer, “suppose there were﻿—what about it?”
“Well, it’s odd that nobody else saw him.”
“I suppose it hasn’t occurred to you that the fellow might have removed his mask?”
Cairn shook his head slowly.
“I don’t think so,” he declared; “I haven’t seen him anywhere in the hotel.”

===Chinese===
「我说，」西姆咕哝道，一面端详着调酒师傅手上的一套操作，「就算真有——那又怎样？」
「呃，蹊跷就蹊跷在，旁人谁都没有看见他。」
「你怕是没想到，那家伙兴许已经摘掉了面具？」
凯恩缓缓地摇了摇头。
「我不这么想，」他断言道，「饭店里上下，我哪儿都没再见到他。」

===Original===
“Seen him?” Sime turned his dull gaze upon the speaker. “How should you know him?”
Cairn raised his hand to his forehead in an oddly helpless way.
“No, of course not﻿—it’s very extraordinary.”
They took their seats at a small table, and in mutual silence loaded and lighted their pipes. Sime, in common with many young and enthusiastic medical men, had theories﻿—theories of that revolutionary sort which only harsh experience can shatter. Secretly he was disposed to ascribe all the ills to which flesh is heir primarily to a disordered nervous system. It was evident that Cairn’s mind persistently ran along a particular groove; something lay back of all this erratic talk; he had clearly invested the Mask of Set with a curious individuality.

===Chinese===
「再见到他？」西姆把自己那双略显迟钝的目光转向说话的人。「你又怎么认得他？」
凯恩抬手掩住额头，那姿态透着一股说不出的无助。
「认不得，自然认不得——这事实在蹊跷至极。」
两人在一张小桌旁落座，又默默无言地各自装烟、点烟。西姆和许多血气方刚的年轻医生一样，自有一套理论——那种唯有严酷的经验才能击碎的革命派理论。私底下，他倾向于把肉体凡胎所承的一切疾苦，首先归咎于紊乱的神经系统。显而易见，凯恩的心思执拗地顺着某一道辙痕打转；这番颠三倒四的言语背后另有隐情；他分明已经把塞特的面具（the Mask of Set）看成了一种奇特而独立的存在。

===Original===
“I gather that you had a stiff bout of it in London?” Sime said suddenly.
Cairn nodded.
“Beastly stiff. There is a lot of sound reason in your nervous theory, Sime. It was touch and go with me for days, I am told; yet, pathologically, I was a hale man. That would seem to show how nerves can kill. Just a series of shocks﻿—horrors﻿—one piled upon another, did as much for me as influenza, pneumonia, and two or three other ailments together could have done.”
Sime shook his head wisely; this was in accordance with his ideas.

===Chinese===
「我听说，你在伦敦狠狠病了一场？」西姆突然开口。
凯恩点了点头。
「惨不堪言。你那套神经致病的理论大有道理，西姆。据说一连好几天，我都在鬼门关前打转；可在病理学上，我原本是个康健无病的人。这似乎足以证明，神经也照样能杀人。只不过一连串的打击——惊怖——一桩压着一桩，在我身上造下的损耗，抵得上流感、肺炎外加两三种别的病痛加在一处。」
西姆一副心领神会的样子摇了摇头；这正合他的理论。

===Original===
“You know Antony Ferrara?” continued Cairn. “Well, he has done this for me. His damnable practices are worse than any disease. Sime, the man is a pestilence! Although the law cannot touch him, although no jury can convict him﻿—he is a murderer. He controls﻿—forces﻿—”
Sime was watching him intently.
“It will give you some idea, Sime, of the pitch to which things had come, when I tell you that my father drove to Ferrara’s rooms one night, with a loaded revolver in his pocket﻿—”
“For”﻿—Sime hesitated﻿—“for protection?”
“No.” Cairn leant forward across the table﻿—“to shoot him, Sime, shoot him on sight, as one shoots a mad dog!”

===Chinese===
「你认识安东尼奥·费拉拉（Antony Ferrara）吧？」凯恩继续道。「唉，这一切就是他一手造下的。他那些该诅咒的勾当，比任何疾病都更可怕。西姆，这个人就是一场瘟疫！尽管法律动不了他一根毫毛，尽管任何陪审团都无法给他定罪——他是个杀人凶手。他支配着——种种力量——」
西姆目不转睛地注视着他。
「西姆，我说一件事给你听，你就知道事情闹到了什么地步：一天夜里，我父亲驱车去了费拉拉的寓所，口袋里揣着一支上了膛的左轮手枪——」
「为了」——西姆迟疑了一下——「为了防身？」
「不是。」凯恩隔着桌子俯身向前——「为了毙了他，西姆，见着就毙，像打疯狗一样！」

===Original===
“Are you serious?”
“As God is my witness, if Antony Ferrara had been in his rooms that night, my father would have killed him!”
“It would have been a shocking scandal.”
“It would have been a martyrdom. The man who removes Antony Ferrara from the earth will be doing mankind a service worthy of the highest reward. He is unfit to live. Sometimes I cannot believe that he does live; I expect to wake up and find that he was a figure of a particularly evil dream.”

===Chinese===
「此话当真？」
「上帝为我作证，那天夜里安东尼奥·费拉拉若是在寓所里，我父亲必定杀了他！」
「那准会闹出一桩骇人听闻的丑闻。」
「那将是一场殉道。谁若把安东尼奥·费拉拉从这世上除掉，便是为人类立下一桩配得最高奖赏的功业。他不配活在世上。有时候我简直不能相信他真活着；我总盼着一觉醒来，发现他不过是一场格外邪恶的梦里的一个身影。」

===Original===
“This incident﻿—the call at his rooms﻿—occurred just before your illness?”
“The thing which he had attempted that night was the last straw, Sime; it broke me down. From the time that he left Oxford, Antony Ferrara has pursued a deliberate course of crime, of crime so cunning, so unusual, and based upon such amazing and unholy knowledge that no breath of suspicion has touched him. Sime, you remember a girl I told you about at Oxford one evening, a girl who came to visit him?”
Sime nodded slowly.
“Well﻿—he killed her! Oh! there is no doubt about it; I saw her body in the hospital.”

===Chinese===
「这件事——上他寓所去的那一趟——就发生在你患病之前？」
「他那晚图谋的事，是压垮我的最后一根稻草，西姆；它把我彻底压垮了。自打离开牛津，安东尼奥·费拉拉便蓄意走上了一条犯罪之途——那罪案如此狡诈、如此离奇，又建立在那样惊人而渎神的知识之上，以致一丝疑云都不曾沾过他的身。西姆，你可记得有一个晚上，我在牛津跟你讲起过一个姑娘——一个上他那里拜访过他的姑娘？」
西姆缓缓点头。
「这个——是他杀了她！哦！这一点毫无疑问；我在医院里见过她的遗体。」

===Original===
“*How* had he killed her, then?”
“How? Only he and the God who permits him to exist can answer that, Sime. He killed her without coming anywhere near her﻿—and he killed his adoptive father, Sir Michael Ferrara, by the same unholy means!”
Sime watched him, but offered no comment.
“It was hushed up, of course; there is no existing law which could be used against him.”

===Chinese===
「那么，他究竟是『怎么』杀了她？」
「怎么杀的？这只有他，和那个容许他存活的上帝，答得上来，西姆。他不曾挨近她一步，就把她杀了——他又用同样渎神的手段，杀了他的养父迈克尔·费拉拉爵士（Sir Michael Ferrara）！」
西姆望着他，却不置一词。
「事情当然给压了下去；现行法律没有一条治得了他。」

===Original===
“*Existing* law?”
“They are ruled out, Sime, the laws that *could* have reached him; but he would have been burnt at the stake in the Middle Ages!”
“I see.” Sime drummed his fingers upon the table. “You had those ideas about him at Oxford; and does Dr. Cairn seriously believe the same?”
“He does. So would you﻿—you could not doubt it, Sime, not for a moment, if you had seen what we have seen!” His eyes blazed into a sudden fury, suggestive of his old, robust self. “He tried night after night, by means of the same accursed sorcery, which everyone thought buried in the ruins of Thebes, to kill *me*! He projected﻿—things﻿—”

===Chinese===
「『现行』的法律？」
「那些法律都给排除在外了，西姆，那些『本可以』够得着他的法律；可在中世纪，他早就被绑上火刑柱烧死了！」
「我明白了。」西姆用手指敲着桌面。「你在牛津时就对他抱着这些想法；那么凯恩博士（Dr. Cairn）也当真这么相信？」
「他信。你也会信的——你要是见过我们所见的种种，西姆，你就没法不信，一刻也没法不信！」他的眼里骤然燃起一股狂怒，隐约透出他旧日那副血气刚健的气概。「他一夜又一夜，用那套人人以为早已埋进底比斯（Thebes）废墟里的可诅咒的巫术，要害死『我』！他投射出——种种东西——」

===Original===
“Suggested these﻿—things, to your mind?”
“Something like that. I saw, or thought I saw, and smelt﻿—pah!﻿—I seem to smell them now!﻿—beetles, mummy-beetles, you know, from the skull of a mummy! My rooms were thick with them. It brought me very near to Bedlam, Sime. Oh! it was not merely imaginary. My father and I caught him red-handed.” He glanced across at the other. “You read of the death of Lord Lashmore? It was just after you came out.”
“Yes﻿—heart.”
“It was his heart, yes﻿—but Ferrara was responsible! That was the business which led my father to drive to Ferrara’s rooms with a loaded revolver in his pocket.”

===Chinese===
「是把这些——东西，暗示进了你的头脑？」
「差不多是那么回事。我看见——或者自以为看见——还闻到了——呸！我这会儿仿佛还闻得见它们！——甲虫，木乃伊的皮蠹，你知道吧，从木乃伊颅骨里生出来的那种！我的房间里密密麻麻全是。那一阵险些把我逼进疯人院，西姆。哦！那绝不单是想象。我父亲和我当场拿住过他的现行。」他瞥了对面的人一眼。「拉什莫尔勋爵（Lord Lashmore）的死讯你读到了吧？就在你出国之后不久。」
「读到了——是心脏。」
「是他的心脏不假——可那正是费拉拉造的孽！就是那桩事，引得我父亲揣着上了膛的左轮手枪，驱车去了费拉拉的寓所。」

===Original===
The wind was shaking the windows, and whistling about the building with demoniacal fury as if seeking admission; the band played a popular waltz; and in and out of the open doors came and went groups representative of many ages and many nationalities.
“Ferrara,” began Sime slowly, “was always a detestable man, with his sleek black hair, and ivory face. Those long eyes of his had an expression which always tempted me to hit him. Sir Michael, if what you say is true﻿—and after all, Cairn, it only goes to show how little we know of the nervous system﻿—literally took a viper to his bosom.”
“He did. Antony Ferrara was his adopted son, of course; God knows to what evil brood he really belongs.”
Both were silent for a while. Then:

===Chinese===
风摇撼着窗子，绕着楼身发出恶魔般的呼啸，仿佛在乞求放它进去；乐队奏着一支流行的圆舞曲；敞开的大门口，各种年纪、各个国籍的人群来来去去。
「费拉拉这个人，」西姆缓缓开口道，「从来就可憎：一头乌亮油滑的黑发，一张象牙色的脸。他那双细长的眼睛里有种神情，总惹得我想给他一拳。迈克尔爵士——倘若你说的属实——说到底，凯恩，这只不过证明我们对神经系统何其无知——简直是实实在在把一条蝰蛇揣进了怀里。」
「他确实揣了。安东尼奥·费拉拉当然是他收养的义子；天知道他究竟出身哪一门邪恶的孽种。」
两人都沉默了片刻。随即——

===Original===
“Gracious heavens!”
Cairn started to his feet so wildly as almost to upset the table.
“Look, Sime! look!” he cried.
Sime was not the only man in the bar to hear, and to heed his words. Sime, looking in the direction indicated by Cairn’s extended finger, received a vague impression that a grotesque, long-headed figure had appeared momentarily in the doorway opening upon the room where the dancers were; then it was gone again, if it had ever been there, and he was supporting Cairn, who swayed dizzily, and had become ghastly pale. Sime imagined that the heated air had grown suddenly even more heated. Curious eyes were turned upon, his companion, who now sank back into his chair, muttering:
“The Mask, the Mask!”

===Chinese===
「天哪！」
凯恩猛地跳起身来，动作激烈得险些掀翻桌子。
「看，西姆！看！」他喊道。
酒吧里听见他这句话、并且留意在心的，不止西姆一人。西姆顺着凯恩伸指的方向望去，只得到一个模糊的印象：一个怪诞的长头身影，在通往舞厅的门口乍现了一瞬；随即又不见了——倘若它真出现过的话——而他已一把扶住凯恩，后者晕晕忽忽地摇晃着，脸白得吓人。西姆只觉得，那本已灼热的空气仿佛骤然又灼热了几分。好奇的目光纷纷投向他的同伴；后者这时瘫回椅中，嘴里喃喃念着：
「那面具！那面具！」

===Original===
“I think I saw the chap who seems to worry you so much,” said Sime soothingly. “Wait here; I will tell the waiter to bring you a dose of brandy; and whatever you do, don’t get excited.”
He made for the door, pausing and giving an order to a waiter on his way, and pushed into the crowd outside. It was long past midnight, and the gaiety, which had been resumed, seemed of a forced and feverish sort. Some of the visitors were leaving, and a breath of hot wind swept in from the open doors.
A pretty girl wearing a yashmak, who, with two similarly attired companions, was making her way to the entrance, attracted his attention; she seemed to be on the point of swooning. He recognised the trio for the same that had pelted Cairn and himself with confetti earlier in the evening.

===Chinese===
「我想，我瞧见那个叫你这么心神不宁的家伙了，」西姆宽慰道。「在这儿等着；我去吩咐侍者给你送一剂白兰地来；还有，无论怎样，千万别激动。」
他朝门口走去，半途停下脚步，向一名侍者交代了一句，随即挤进门外的人群。此刻早已过了午夜，重新拾起来的欢愉，透着一股勉强而亢奋的味道。有些来客正在离去，一股热风从敞开的门外卷进来。
一个戴面纱（yashmak）的俊俏姑娘引起了他的注意；她与两个同样装束的同伴正朝大门口走去，看样子几乎要晕倒了。他认出这三个人，正是傍晚早些时候朝凯恩和他抛掷彩纸屑的那三位。

===Original===
“The sudden heat has affected your friend,” he said, stepping up to them. “My name is Dr. Sime; may I offer you my assistance?”
The offer was accepted, and with the three he passed out on to the terrace, where the dust grated beneath the tread, and helped the fainting girl into an *arabîyeh*. The night was thunderously black, the heat almost insufferable, and the tall palms in front of the hotel bowed before the might of the scorching wind.
As the vehicle drove off, Sime stood for a moment looking after it. His face was very grave, for there was a look in the bright eyes of the girl in the yashmak which, professionally, he did not like. Turning up the steps, he learnt from the manager that several visitors had succumbed to the heat. There was something furtive in the manner of his informant’s glance, and Sime looked at him significantly.
“Khamsin brings clouds of septic dust with it,” he said. “Let us hope that these attacks are due to nothing more than the unexpected rise in the temperature.”

===Chinese===
「骤然起来的热气，让您这位朋友吃不消了，」他趋前说道。「我姓西姆，是个医生；可否容我略效绵薄？」
这番好意被接受了，他随着三位女子步出露台——沙尘在脚下咯吱作响——把那晕厥的姑娘扶进一辆『arabîyeh』（阿拉伯式马车）。夜色黑得沉甸甸的像压着雷，暑热几乎叫人无法忍受，饭店门前高高的棕榈在灼热狂风的威力前深深俯首。
马车驶动时，西姆伫立片刻，目送着它远去。他神色十分凝重，因为那戴面纱的姑娘明亮的眼中有一种神色，以职业的眼光看，是他所不愿见到的。他转身走上台阶，从经理口中得知，已有好几位来客中了暑。报信人的目光里带着几分躲闪，西姆便意味深长地看了他一眼。
「喀新风会带来大团大团的腐毒尘土，」他说。「但愿这些发作不过是气温骤升所致，别无他因。」

===Original===
An air of uneasiness prevailed now throughout the hotel. The wind had considerably abated, and crowds were leaving, pouring from the steps into the deserted street, a dreamlike company.
Colonel Royland took Sime aside, as the latter was making his way back to the buffet. The Colonel, whose regiment was stationed at the Citadel, had known Sime almost from childhood.
“You know, my boy,” he said, “I should never have allowed Eileen” (his daughter) “to remain in Cairo, if I had foreseen this change in the weather. This infernal wind, coming right through the native town, is loaded with infection.”
“Has it affected her, then?” asked Sime anxiously.

===Chinese===
此刻，一种惶惶不安的气氛弥漫了整座饭店。风势已减退了许多，人群正纷纷离去，从台阶上涌下，泻入空寂的街道，恍如一队梦中的人影。
西姆正要折回酒吧，罗伊兰上校（Colonel Royland）把他拉到一旁。这位上校所属的团驻扎在开罗城堡（the Citadel），他打西姆孩提时就认识他。
「要知道，我的孩子，」他说，「我要是料到天气会这样翻脸，说什么也不会让艾琳（Eileen）」——他的女儿——「留在开罗。这股邪风一路穿过本地人的城区，裹挟着满满的疫气。」
「那么她也被这风伤了？」西姆焦切地问。

===Original===
“She nearly fainted in the ballroom,” replied the Colonel. “Her mother took her home half an hour ago. I looked for you everywhere, but couldn’t find you.”
“Quite a number have succumbed,” said Sime.
“Eileen seemed to be slightly hysterical,” continued the Colonel. “She persisted that someone wearing a crocodile mask had been standing beside her at the moment that she was taken ill.”
Sime started; perhaps Cairn’s story was not a matter of imagination after all.

===Chinese===
「她在舞厅里险些晕倒，」上校答道。「她母亲半小时前送她回家了。我到处找你，可哪儿也找不着。」
「中了暑的着实不少，」西姆说。
「艾琳当时像是有些歇斯底里，」上校接着说。「她一口咬定，她发病的那一刻，有个戴鳄鱼面具的人一直站在她身旁。」
西姆悚然一惊；如此说来，凯恩讲的那件事，或许终究并非出于想象。

===Original===
“There is someone here, dressed like that, I believe,” he replied, with affected carelessness. “He seems to have frightened several people. Any idea who he is?”
“My dear chap!” cried the Colonel, “I have been searching the place for him! But I have never once set eyes upon him. I was about to ask if *you* knew anything about it!”
Sime returned to the table where Cairn was sitting. The latter seemed to have recovered somewhat; but he looked far from well. Sime stared at him critically.
“I should turn in,” he said, “if I were you. Khamsin is playing the deuce with people. I only hope it does not justify its name and blow for fifty days.”

===Chinese===
「我想，这儿倒真有一个这般打扮的人，」他答道，故意装出满不在乎的口气。「他似乎吓着了好几个人。知道他是谁吗？」
「亲爱的伙计！」上校叫道，「我满场都找遍了他！可我连一眼也不曾见过他。我正要问的，倒是『你』知道些什么没有呢！」
西姆回到凯恩坐着的桌前。后者似乎缓过来了一些；可气色仍远说不上好。西姆细细地端详着他。
「我要是你，就上床歇着去，」他说。「喀新风正把人们折腾得够呛。我只盼它别应了自己名字的本义（喀新风得名于阿拉伯语的『五十』），当真刮足五十天。」

===Original===
“Have you seen the man in the mask!” asked Cairn.
“No,” replied Sime, “but he’s here alright; others have seen him.”
Cairn stood up rather unsteadily, and with Sime made his way through the moving crowd to the stairs. The band was still playing, but the cloud of gloom which had settled upon the place, refused to be dissipated.
“Good night, Cairn,” said Sime, “see you in the morning.”

===Chinese===
「你见到那个戴面具的人了吧！」凯恩问。
「没有，」西姆答道，「不过他确实在这儿；别人见到过他。」
凯恩站起身来，脚步仍有些虚浮，随着西姆穿过走动的人群向楼梯走去。乐队还在演奏，可笼罩在这地方的阴云，却怎么也驱不散。
「晚安，凯恩，」西姆说，「明早见。」

===Original===
Robert Cairn, with aching head and a growing sensation of nausea, paused on the landing, looking down into the court below. He could not disguise from himself that he felt ill, not nervously ill as in London, but physically sick. This superheated air was difficult to breathe; it seemed to rise in waves from below.
Then, from a weary glancing at the figures beneath him, his attitude changed to one of tense watching.
A man, wearing the crocodile mask of Set, stood by a huge urn containing a palm, looking up to the landing!
Cairn’s weakness left him, and in its place came an indescribable anger, a longing to drive his fist into that grinning mask. He turned and ran lightly down the stairs, conscious of a sudden glow of energy. Reaching the floor, he saw the mask making across the hall, in the direction of the outer door. As rapidly as possible, for he could not run, without attracting undesirable attention, Cairn followed. The figure of Set passed out on to the terrace, but when Cairn in turn swung open the door, his quarry had vanished.

===Chinese===
罗伯特·凯恩（Robert Cairn）头很疼，恶心之感阵阵上涌，在楼梯平台上停住脚，俯望着楼下的中庭。他无法对自己掩饰：他是真的病了——不像在伦敦时那样是神经上的病，而是身体上的难受。这过度灼热的空气很难呼吸；它仿佛正从楼下一波一波地升腾上来。
起初，他只是倦怠地瞥一眼脚下那些人影；随即，他的神态一变，成了紧张的凝注。
一个戴着塞特的鳄鱼面具的男子，立在一口栽着棕榈的大陶缸旁，正仰望着这方平台！
凯恩的虚弱霎时无影无踪，取而代之的是一股难以名状的怒火，一股想把拳头捣进那副咧嘴而笑的面具里的渴望。他转过身，脚步轻快地奔下楼梯，只觉浑身骤然涌起一股劲道。到了楼下，他看见那面具正横穿门厅，朝临街的大门而去。凯恩便尽快跟了上去——他不能撒腿奔跑，一跑便会招来不必要的注目。塞特的身影出门上了露台，可待凯恩也推门而出时，他的猎物已经无影无踪。

===Original===
Then, in an *arabîyeh* just driving off, he detected the hideous mask. Hatless as he was, he ran down the steps and threw himself into another. The carriage-controller was in attendance, and Cairn rapidly told him to instruct the driver to follow the *arabîyeh* which had just left. The man lashed up his horses, turned the carriage, and went galloping on after the retreating figure. Past the Esbekîya Gardens they went, through several narrow streets, and on to the quarter of the Mûski. Time after time he thought he had lost the carriage ahead, but his own driver’s knowledge of the tortuous streets enabled him always to overtake it again. They went rocking along lanes so narrow that with outstretched arms one could almost have touched the walls on either side; past empty shops and unlighted houses. Cairn had not the remotest idea of his whereabouts, save that he was evidently in the district of the bazaars. A right-angled corner was abruptly negotiated﻿—and there, ahead of him, stood the pursued vehicle! The driver was turning his horses around, to return; his fare was disappearing from sight into the black shadows of a narrow alley on the left.

===Chinese===
就在这时，他瞧见一辆刚刚驶动的『arabîyeh』里，赫然是那副狰狞的面具。他顾不得头上无帽，奔下台阶，一头钻进另一辆。马车调度员正在旁边当值，凯恩急急吩咐他去叫车夫追赶那辆刚离开的『arabîyeh』。那人扬鞭策马，调转车头，朝着那远去的身影疾追而去。他们驶过埃兹贝基亚花园（Esbekîya Gardens），穿过好几条窄街，进入穆斯基街（Mûski）一带。一次又一次，他以为前头那辆车已经追丢，可他自家车夫对九曲回肠的街巷烂熟于胸，总能重新赶上去。马车摇摇晃晃地驶过一条条窄巷，巷子窄得人平伸双臂几乎能同时触到两边的高墙；驶过一家家空铺面、一座座没有灯火的人家。凯恩对自己身在何处全无半点概念，只晓得自己显然身在巴扎（bazaars，集市）区域。马车猛地拐过一处直角街角——前头，那辆被追的马车赫然停在眼前！车夫正勒转马头准备返回；他那乘客的身影正没入左手一条窄巷的黑影里，眼看就要从视野里消失。

===Original===
Cairn leaped from the *arabîyeh*, shouting to the man to wait, and went dashing down the sloping lane after the retreating figure. A sort of blind fury possessed him, but he never paused to analyse it, never asked himself by what right he pursued this man, what wrong the latter had done him. His action was wholly unreasoning; he knew that he wished to overtake the wearer of the mask and to tear it from his head; upon that he acted!
He discovered that despite the tropical heat of the night, he was shuddering with cold, but he disregarded this circumstance, and ran on.
The pursued stopped before an iron-studded door, which was opened instantly; he entered as the runner came up with him. And, before the door could be reclosed, Cairn thrust his way in.

===Chinese===
凯恩从『arabîyeh』上一跃而下，喊了声叫车夫等着，便顺着那条下坡的窄巷朝远去的身影疾追下去。一种盲目的狂怒攫住了他，可他从不曾停下来细想，从不曾问过自己：他凭什么追这个人？这人又何曾亏待过他？他的行动全然没了理性；他只知道，他要追上那个戴面具的人，把面具从他头上撕下来；他便照着这一念行事！
他发觉，尽管夜里热得赛过热带，自己却在寒战；但他没把这放在心上，继续奔跑。
被追的人在一扇钉满铁钉的门前停住，门立时应声而开；他闪身进去，恰在追者赶到之际。不等那扇门重新合拢，凯恩已侧身挤了进去。

===Original===
Blackness, utter blackness, was before him. The figure which he had pursued seemed to have been swallowed up. He stumbled on, gropingly, hands outstretched, then fell﻿—fell, as he realised in the moment of falling, down a short flight of stone steps.
Still amid utter blackness, he got upon his feet, shaken but otherwise unhurt by his fall. He turned about, expecting to see some glimmer of light from the stairway, but the blackness was unbroken. Silence and gloom hemmed him in. He stood for a moment, listening intently.
A shaft of light pierced the darkness, as a shutter was thrown open. Through an iron-barred window the light shone; and with the light came a breath of stifling perfume. That perfume carried his imagination back instantly to a room at Oxford, and he advanced and looked through into the place beyond. He drew a swift breath, clutched the bars, and was silent﻿—stricken speechless.

===Chinese===
黑暗，彻底的黑暗，横在他面前。他追的那个身影仿佛已被吞没。他伸出双手，一路摸索着跌撞前行，随即跌了下去——在跌落的一瞬他才明白——跌下了一小截石阶。
仍是在彻底的黑暗中，他站起身来；这一跤摔得他浑身发颤，除此之外倒没受伤。他转过身，指望看见石阶那头透出一线微光，可黑暗依旧密不透风。寂静与幽暗四面围困着他。他伫立片刻，凝神细听。
一扇窗板倏然推开，一道光刺破黑暗。灯光从一扇装着铁栅的窗里照出来；随着光，还飘来一股令人窒息的香气。那股香气霎时把他的想象带回牛津的一间屋子；他上前一步，透过窗格向里面的所在望去。他急促地吸了口气，一把攥住铁栅，默然无声——被惊得哑口无言。

===Original===
He looked into a large and lofty room, lighted by several hanging lamps. It had a carpeted divan at one end and was otherwise scantily furnished, in the Eastern manner. A silver incense-burner smoked upon a large praying-carpet, and by it stood the man in the crocodile mask. An Arab girl, fantastically attired, who had evidently just opened the shutters, was now helping him to remove the hideous headdress.
She presently untied the last of the fastenings and lifted the thing from the man’s shoulders, moving away with the gliding step of the Oriental, and leaving him standing there in his short white tunic, bare-legged and sandalled.
The smoke of the incense curled upward and played around the straight, slim figure, drew vaporous lines about the still, ivory face﻿—the handsome, sinister face, sometimes partly veiling the long black eyes and sometimes showing them in all their unnatural brightness. So the man stood, looking towards the barred window.
It was Antony Ferrara!

===Chinese===
他望进一间宽大高敞的屋子，几盏吊灯照着它。屋子一端设着一方铺毯的矮榻，除此之外陈设寥寥，一派东方格调。一张大祈祷毯上，一只银香炉正袅袅冒着烟；炉边立着那个戴鳄鱼面具的人。一个装束奇艳的阿拉伯少女——方才显然正是她推开的窗板——此时正帮他卸下那副骇人的头面。
她不多时解开最后一道系结，把那东西从男子肩头捧了下来，以东方人滑行般的步子悄然退去，留他立在原地——一身白色短褂，赤着双腿，趿着凉鞋。
熏香的烟袅袅上升，绕着那挺拔颀长的身影萦回嬉戏，在那张沉静的象牙色面孔四周——那张英俊而阴鸷的面孔——描出朦胧的烟线，时而半掩那双细长的黑眼睛，时而又任它们全然显露，亮得不近自然。那人就这样立着，朝铁栅窗这边望来。
那是安东尼奥·费拉拉！

===Original===
“Ah, dear Cairn﻿—” the husky musical voice smote upon Cairn’s ears as the most hated sound in nature﻿—“you have followed me. Not content with driving me from London, you would also render Cairo﻿—my dear Cairo﻿—untenable for me.”
Cairn clutched the bars but was silent.
“How wrong of you, Cairn!” the soft voice mocked. “This attention is so harmful﻿—to you. Do you know, Cairn, the Sudanese formed the extraordinary opinion that I was an afreet, and this strange reputation has followed me right down the Nile. Your father, my dear friend, has studied these odd matters, and he would tell you that there is no power, in Nature, higher than the human will. Actually, Cairn, they have ascribed to me the direction of the khamsin, and so many worthy Egyptians have made up their minds that I travel with the storm﻿—or that the storm follows me﻿—that something of the kind has really come to pass! Or is it merely coincidence, Cairn? Who can say?”

===Chinese===
「啊，亲爱的凯恩——」那沙哑而音乐般的嗓音敲在凯恩的耳鼓上，一如天地间最可憎的声音——「你追来了。把我逐出伦敦还不甘心，你还要叫开罗——我亲爱的开罗——也容不得我存身。」
凯恩攥紧铁栅，却一言不发。
「你多不该啊，凯恩！」那轻柔的嗓音嘲弄道。「这份殷勤危害至深——对你自己。你可知道，凯恩，苏丹人形成了一个离奇的看法，认定我是阿弗里特（afreet），这份怪名声便顺尼罗河一路传了下来。你父亲——我亲爱的朋友——研究过这些奇事，他会告诉你：天地之间，没有任何力量高过人的意志。说实在的，凯恩，他们竟把喀新风的行止都归到了我名下；那么多可敬的埃及人认定我是携暴风同行——或者说暴风随我而至——结果，这类事情竟真的应验了！又或者，这不过是巧合罢了，凯恩？谁说得准呢？」

===Original===
Motionless, immobile, save for a slow smile, Antony Ferrara stood, and Cairn kept his eyes upon the evil face, and with trembling hands clutched the bars.
“It is certainly odd, is it not,” resumed the taunting voice, “that khamsin, so violent, too, should thus descend upon the Cairene season? I only arrived from the Fayûm this evening, Cairn, and, do you know, they have the pestilence there! I trust the hot wind does not carry it to Cairo; there are so many distinguished European and American visitors here. It would be a thousand pities!”
Cairn released his grip of the bars, raised his clenched fists above his head, and in a voice and with a maniacal fury that were neither his own, cursed the man who stood there mocking him. Then he reeled, fell, and remembered no more.
---

===Chinese===
安东尼奥·费拉拉立在原地，纹丝不动，唯有一抹缓缓浮起的笑意；凯恩的目光死死钉在那张邪恶的脸上，双手颤抖着攥住铁栅。
「说来确也离奇，不是吗，」那讥嘲的声音又起了，「这喀新风——还这般狂暴——偏偏就这样降临到开罗的旺季头上？我今晚才从法尤姆（Fayûm）过来，凯恩，你可知道，那边闹起了瘟疫！但愿热风别把它带到开罗来；此地有多少位显赫的欧美来客啊。那可就万分可惜了！」
凯恩松开攥着铁栅的手，双拳紧握举过头顶，以一种全然不属于他的嗓音、一种全然不属于他的癫狂怒火，咒骂着那个立在那里讥嘲他的人。随后他身子一晃，栽倒在地，此后便什么都不知道了。
---

===Original===
“All right, old man﻿—you’ll do quite nicely now.”
It was Sime speaking.
Cairn struggled upright﻿ ﻿… and found himself in bed! Sime was seated beside him.

===Chinese===
「好了，老伙计——你这会儿没什么大碍了。」
说话的是西姆。
凯恩挣扎着坐起身来……却发现自己躺在床上！西姆坐在他的床边。

===Original===
“Don’t talk!” said Sime, “you’re in hospital! I’ll do the talking; you listen. I saw you bolt out of Shepheard’s last night﻿—shut up! I followed, but lost you. We got up a search party, and with the aid of the man who had driven you, ran you to earth in a dirty alley behind the mosque of El-Azhar. Four kindly mendicants, who reside upon the steps of the establishment, had been awakened by your blundering in among them. They were holding you﻿—yes, you were raving pretty badly. You are a lucky man, Cairn. You were inoculated before you left home?”

===Chinese===
「别说话！」西姆说，「你在医院里！话由我来讲，你只管听。昨儿夜里，我看见你从谢普赫德饭店（Shepheard's）里窜了出去——闭嘴！我追了，可把你追丢了。我们组织了一支搜索队，靠那个替你赶车的车夫帮忙，在爱资哈尔清真寺（El-Azhar）后面一条肮脏的小巷里把你寻了个正着。四个好心的托钵僧——他们平日就栖身在那寺的台阶上——被你跌跌撞撞闯进他们中间闹醒了。他们死死按住了你——不错，你当时胡话说得挺凶。你是个走运的人，凯恩。你离家之前接种过疫苗吧？」

===Original===
Cairn nodded weakly.
“Saved you. Be all right in a couple of days. That damned khamsin has brought a whiff of the plague from somewhere! Curiously enough, over fifty percent of the cases spotted so far are people who were at the carnival! Some of them, Cairn﻿—but we won’t discuss that now. I was afraid of it, last night. That’s why I kept my eye on you. My boy, you were delirious when you bolted out of the hotel!”
“Was I?” said Cairn wearily, and lay back on the pillow. “Perhaps I was.”

===Chinese===
凯恩虚弱地点了点头。
「救了你的命。过两三天就没事了。那该死的喀新风，不知从哪儿捎来了一股鼠疫！说来也怪，迄今查出的病例，百分之五十以上都是参加过那场狂欢会的人！其中有几位，凯恩——不过这个咱们现在不谈。昨天夜里我就怕这个。所以我才一直盯着你。我的孩子，你从饭店里窜出去的时候，人已经在说胡话了！」
「是吗？」凯恩倦倦地说，躺回枕上。「也许吧。」

## XIV Dr. Cairn Arrives / XIV 凯恩博士到来

===Original===
Dr. Bruce Cairn stepped into the boat which was to take him ashore, and as it swung away from the side of the liner sought to divert his thoughts by a contemplation of the weird scene. Amid the smoky flare of many lights, amid rising clouds of dust, a line of laden toilers was crawling ant-like from the lighters into the bowels of the big ship; and a second line, unladen, was descending by another gangway. Above, the jewelled velvet of the sky swept in a glorious arc; beyond, the lights of Port Said broke through the black curtain of the night, and the moving ray from the lighthouse intermittently swept the harbour waters; whilst, amid the indescribable clamour, the grimily picturesque turmoil, so characteristic of the place, the liner took in coal for her run to Rangoon.

Dodging this way and that, rounding the sterns of big ships, and disputing the waterway with lesser craft, the boat made for shore.

The usual delay at the Custom House, the usual soothing of the excited officials in the usual way, and his *arabîyeh* was jolting Dr. Cairn through the noise and the smell of those rambling streets, a noise and a smell entirely peculiar to this clearing-house of the Near East.

===Chinese===
布鲁斯·凯恩博士踏进送他上岸的小艇；小艇从邮轮船舷边荡开时，他试图借凝望这片奇异的景象来排遣思绪。在烟雾腾腾的点点火光里，在扬扬上升的尘雾中，一列肩负重载的苦力正像蚂蚁似的从驳船爬进大船的腹内；另一列空着手的苦力则沿着另一道舷梯鱼贯而下。头顶上方，缀珠嵌宝的天鹅绒夜幕划出一道壮丽的弧线；再远处，塞得港的灯火刺破夜的黑色帷幕，灯塔上移动的光束一下一下扫过港湾的水面；而在那难以名状的喧嚣、那肮脏中自有画意的纷乱——正是此地独有的景象——之中，邮轮正在为驶往仰光的航程添煤。

小艇东躲西闪，绕过一艘艘大船的船尾，与更小的船只争抢着航道，朝岸边驶去。

照例在海关耽搁一阵，照例用老法子安抚好那些激动起来的官员，然后，他那辆『arabîyeh』（阿拉伯马车）便载着凯恩博士，在那些曲折街巷的喧闹与怪味中一路颠簸——这喧闹与怪味，正是这座近东集散地所独有的。

===Original===
He accepted the room which was offered to him at the hotel, without troubling to inspect it, and having left instructions that he was to be called in time for the early train to Cairo, he swallowed a whisky and soda at the buffet, and wearily ascended the stairs. There were tourists in the hotel, English and American, marked by a gaping wonderment, and loud with plans of sightseeing; but Port Said, nay all Egypt, had nothing of novelty to offer Dr. Cairn. He was there at great inconvenience; a practitioner of his repute may not easily arrange to quit London at a moment's notice. But the business upon which he was come was imperative. For him the charm of the place had not existence, but somewhere in Egypt his son stood in deadly peril, and Dr. Cairn counted the hours that yet divided them. His soul was up in arms against the man whose evil schemes had led to his presence in Port Said, at a time when many sufferers required his ministrations in Half-Moon Street. He was haunted by a phantom, a ghoul in human shape; Antony Ferrara, the adopted son of his dear friend, the adopted son, who had murdered his adopter, who whilst guiltless in the eyes of the law, was blood-guilty in the eyes of God!

Dr. Cairn switched on the light and seated himself upon the side of the bed, knitting his brows and staring straight before him, with an expression in his clear grey eyes whose significance he would have denied hotly, had any man charged him with it. He was thinking of Antony Ferrara's record; the victims of this fiendish youth (for Antony Ferrara was barely of age) seemed to stand before him with hands stretched out appealingly.

"You alone," they seemed to cry, "know who and what he is! You alone know of our awful wrongs; you alone can avenge them!"

===Chinese===
旅馆替他备下的房间，他懒得查看便接受了；又留下话，届时叫醒他赶去开罗的早班车，随后在酒柜边匆匆灌下一杯威士忌苏打，疲乏地登上楼梯。旅馆里住着游客，英国人、美国人，人人一脸瞠目的惊奇，高声谈论着观光的行程；可塞得港——不，整个埃及——对凯恩博士再没有什么新鲜可言。他到这儿来实属万般无奈；像他这样名望的开业医生，岂能轻易说走就走、随时撇下伦敦。但他此来要办的事刻不容缓。此地的风情于他根本不存在，他的儿子正在埃及的某处陷于致命的危险之中，凯恩博士一小时一小时地数着还隔在他们之间的时光。他满腔义愤，痛恨那个人——正是那人的恶毒阴谋害得他此刻身滞塞得港，而半月街上多少病患正等着他去诊治。一个幽灵纠缠着他，一个披着人形的食尸鬼——安东尼奥·费拉拉，他挚友的养子，那个养子，谋害了收养自己的人，那个在法律眼中清白无罪、在上帝眼中却血债累累的人！

凯恩博士拧亮电灯，在床沿上坐下，眉头紧锁，直勾勾地瞪着前方，一双清澈的灰眼睛里透着一种神情——倘若有谁指了出来，他定会勃然大怒地否认那神情里的含义。他心里想着的是安东尼奥·费拉拉的累累罪案；这个恶魔般的年轻人（安东尼奥·费拉拉不过刚刚成年）手下的受害者们，仿佛一个个站在他面前，伸出一双双手哀哀恳求着。

「只有你，」他们仿佛在哭喊，「知道他是谁、是什么东西！只有你知道我们蒙受了何等可怕的冤屈；也只有你，能为我们复仇！」

===Original===
And yet he had hesitated! It had remained for his own flesh and blood to be threatened ere he had taken decisive action. The viper had lain within his reach, and he had neglected to set his heel upon it. Men and women had suffered and had died of its venom; and he had not crushed it. Then Robert, his son, had felt the poison fang, and Dr. Cairn, who had hesitated to act upon the behalf of all humanity, had leapt to arms. He charged himself with a parent's selfishness, and his conscience would hear no defence.

Dimly, the turmoil from the harbour reached him where he sat. He listened dully to the hooting of a syren—that of some vessel coming out of the canal.

His thoughts were evil company, and, with a deep sigh, he rose, crossed the room and threw open the double windows, giving access to the balcony.

===Chinese===
然而他却犹豫过！直到自己的亲骨肉受到威胁，他才拿出果断的行动来。那条毒蛇分明就卧在他伸手可及的地方，他却疏于踏上一脚、置之死地。多少男人和女人受了它毒液的荼毒、死去，而他没有把它碾死。后来，他的儿子罗伯特尝到了那毒牙的滋味，这位连为全人类出面都曾犹豫不决的凯恩博士，这才奋然而起。他给自己安上一个做父亲的私心之罪，而他的良心不听半句辩护。

他坐在那里，港湾的嘈杂隐隐约约地传来。他木然听着一声汽笛的长鸣——那是某艘正驶出运河的船。

这样的思绪是凶险的伴侣；他深深叹了口气，起身穿过房间，猛地推开通往阳台的那两扇窗扉。

===Original===
Port Said, a panorama of twinkling lights, lay beneath him. The beam from the lighthouse swept the town searchingly like the eye of some pagan god lustful for sacrifice. He imagined that he could hear the shouting of the gangs coaling the liner in the harbour; but the night was full of the remote murmuring inseparable from that gateway of the East. The streets below, white under the moon, looked empty and deserted, and the hotel beneath him gave up no sound to tell of the many birds of passage who sheltered within it. A stunning sense of his loneliness came to him; his physical loneliness was symbolic of that which characterised his place in the world. He, alone, had the knowledge and the power to crush Antony Ferrara. He, alone, could rid the world of the unnatural menace embodied in the person bearing that name.

The town lay beneath his eyes, but now he saw nothing of it; before his mental vision loomed—exclusively—the figure of a slim and strangely handsome young man, having jet black hair, lustreless, a face of uniform ivory hue, long dark eyes wherein lurked lambent fires, and a womanish grace expressed in his whole bearing and emphasised by his long white hands. Upon a finger of the left hand gleamed a strange green stone.

Antony Ferrara! In the eyes of this solitary traveller, who stood looking down upon Port Said, that figure filled the entire landscape of Egypt!

===Chinese===
塞得港，一幅灯火明灭的全景，铺展在他脚下。灯塔的光束探照般扫过全城，宛如某位渴求血祭的异教神祇的眼睛。他恍然觉得听得见港口里那些为邮轮加煤的班组的呼喝；但这夜晚本就充满低远的絮语，与这座东方的大门须臾难分。脚下的街道在月色里一片白，看上去空空荡荡、寂无人迹；身下的旅馆也悄无声息，全然听不出里面栖身着多少过往的候鸟。一种震慑人心的孤独感攫住了他；他这形体的孤独，正象征着他在这个世上独有的处境。唯有他，具备粉碎安东尼奥·费拉拉的知识与力量。唯有他，能将那个顶着这个名字的人所体现的逆天祸患从世界上除掉。

城池就在他眼底，此刻他却什么也看不见了；在他心眼前浮现的——唯独——是一个身影：一个清瘦而俊美得出奇的年轻人，一头乌黑的头发了无光泽，一张脸是匀净无瑕的象牙色，狭长的黑眼睛里潜伏着幽幽的火光，通身的举止透着女人似的娟秀，又因那双修长白皙的手而愈加显豁。左手的一根手指上，闪动着一颗奇异的绿石。

安东尼奥·费拉拉！在这位伫立俯视塞得港的孤身旅人眼中，那个身影占满了整个埃及的风景！

===Original===
With a weary sigh, Dr. Cairn turned and began to undress. Leaving the windows open, he switched off the light and got into bed. He was very weary, with a weariness rather of the spirit than of the flesh, but it was of that sort which renders sleep all but impossible. Around and about one fixed point his thoughts circled; in vain he endeavoured to forget, for a while, Antony Ferrara and the things connected with him. Sleep was imperative, if he would be in fit condition to cope with the matters which demanded his attention in Cairo.

Yet sleep defied him. Every trifling sound from the harbour and the canal seemed to rise upon the still air to his room. Through a sort of mist created by the mosquito curtains, he could see the open windows, and look out upon the stars. He found himself studying the heavens with sleepless eyes, and idly working out the constellations visible. Then one very bright star attracted the whole of his attention, and, with the dogged persistency of insomnia, he sought to place it, but could not determine to which group it belonged.

So he lay with his eyes upon the stars until the other veiled lamps of heaven became invisible, and the patch of sky no more than a setting for that one white orb.

===Chinese===
凯恩博士疲惫地叹了口气，转过身开始宽衣。窗户就这么开着，他熄了灯，上床躺下。他累极了，这份疲惫与其说在肉体，不如说在精神，而偏偏是这样的疲惫，最叫人难以入睡。他的思绪绕着一个定点回环打转；他想暂时忘掉安东尼奥·费拉拉、忘掉与他相关的一切，却是枉然。睡眠必不可少，否则他便无法以良好的状态去应对开罗那些等着他料理的事。

然而睡眠偏偏与他作对。港湾和运河传来的每一丝细碎声响，似乎都循着凝滞的空气升上了他的房间。隔着蚊帐织成的一层薄雾，他望得见敞开的窗，望得见窗外的星。他发觉自己正睁着无眠的眼睛察看天穹，百无聊赖地辨认着一个个看得见的星座。接着，一颗极亮的星吸尽了他全部的注意；他怀着失眠者那种执拗的韧劲，非要认定它的方位，却断不定它属于哪一星座。

他就这样躺着，眼睛望着群星，直到天幕上其余蒙纱的灯盏一一隐去，那一方天空不过成了那一轮白色光球的衬底。
