西姆明白了。前面既然有一个狠毒的敌人，他们正爬行的这个岩孔便是一只必死无疑的陷坑。他想起那些无头的蝙蝠，想起自己爬进前头那口竖井、探头出去的时候，无异于把身子亮给对方，随时可能遭逢同样的下场！

===Original===
Dr. Cairn moved slowly onward. Despite their anxiety to avoid noise, neither he nor his companion could control their heavy breathing. Both were panting for air. The temperature was now deathly. A candle would scarcely have burnt in the vitiated air; and above that odour of ancient rottenness which all explorers of the monuments of Egypt know, rose that other indescribable odour which seemed to stifle one’s very soul.

Dr. Cairn stopped again.

Sime knew, having performed this journey before, that his companion must have reached the end of the passage, that he must be lying peering out into the shaft, for which they were making. He extinguished his lamp.

===Chinese===
凯恩博士缓缓向前挪去。尽管两人一心要避免声响，却谁也管不住自己沉重的呼吸，都喘着粗气。此刻的温度已如死域。在这样污浊的空气里，一支蜡烛恐怕都点不着；而在埃及古迹的探察者们人人识得的那股古老腐朽的气味之上，又升起了另一种难以名状的气味，仿佛要把人的魂魄生生闷熄。

凯恩博士又停了下来。

西姆从前走过这一程，知道同伴必定已到甬道尽头，正趴在那里向外窥探他们要去的竖井。他熄了自己的灯。

===Original===
Again Dr. Cairn moved forward. Stretching out his hand, Sime found only emptiness. He wriggled forward, in turn, rapidly, all the time groping with his fingers. Then:

“Take my hand,” came a whisper. “Another two feet, and you can stand upright.”

He proceeded, grasped the hand which was extended to him in the impenetrable darkness, and panting, temporarily exhausted, rose upright beside Dr. Cairn, and stretched his cramped limbs.

Side by side they stood, mantled about in such a darkness as cannot be described; in such a silence as dwellers in the busy world cannot conceive; in such an atmosphere of horror that only a man morally and physically brave could have retained his composure.

===Chinese===
凯恩博士又向前挪去。西姆伸手一探，只摸到一片空。他也跟着迅速往前扭动，一路上用手指摸索。接着：

「抓住我的手，」一声低语传来。「再进两英尺，你就能站直身子了。」

他继续向前，在伸手不见五指的黑暗里抓住递来的那只手，气喘吁吁、一时力竭，在凯恩博士身旁直起身来，舒展僵痛的四肢。

两人并肩而立，裹在一种无法形容的黑暗里；裹在一种喧嚣尘世中人所无法想象的寂静里；裹在一种唯有身心俱勇之人方能保持镇定的恐怖气氛里。

===Original===
Dr. Cairn bent to Sime’s ear.

“We *must* have the light for the ascent,” he whispered. “Have your pistol ready; I am about to press the button of the lamp.”

A shaft of white light shone suddenly up the rocky sides of the pit in which they stood, and lost itself in the gloom of the chamber above.

===Chinese===
凯恩博士俯到西姆耳边。

「往上去，我们『必须』有光，」他悄声道。「把你的手枪备好；我要按灯的开关了。」

一道白光蓦地打亮了他们所立竖井的石壁，向上射去，没入上方墓室的幽暗里。

===Original===
“On to my shoulders,” jerked Sime. “You are lighter than I. Then, as soon as you can reach, place your lamp on the floor above and mount up beside it. I will follow.”

Dr. Cairn, taking advantage of the rugged walls, and of the blocks of stone amid which they stood, mounted upon Sime’s shoulders.

“Could you carry your revolver in your teeth?” asked the latter. “I think you might hold it by the trigger-guard.”

“I proposed to do so,” replied Dr. Cairn grimly. “Stand fast!”

===Chinese===
「上我的肩膀，」西姆急促地说。「您比我轻。等您够得着了，就把灯搁在上面的地上，再翻上去，到灯旁边待着。我随后跟上来。」

凯恩博士借着崎岖的井壁和两人脚边堆着的石块，蹬上了西姆的肩膀。

「您能用牙叼着左轮手枪吗？」后者问道。「我想您可以扣住扳机护圈叼稳。」

「我正打算这么办，」凯恩博士冷冷地答道。「站稳了！」

===Original===
Gradually he rose upright upon the other’s shoulders; then, placing his foot in a cranny of the rock, and with his left hand grasping a protruding fragment above, he mounted yet higher, all the time holding the lighted lamp in his right hand. Upward he extended his arms, and upward, until he could place the lamp upon the ledge above his head, where its white beam shone across the top of the shaft.

“Mind it does not fall!” panted Sime, craning his head upward to watch these operations.

Dr. Cairn, whose strength and agility were wonderful, twisted around sideways, and succeeded in placing his foot on a ledge of stone on the opposite side of the shaft. Resting his weight upon this, he extended his hand to the lip of the opening, and drew himself up to the top, where he crouched fully in the light of the lamp. Then, wedging his foot into a crevice a little below him, he reached out his hand to Sime. The latter, following much the same course as his companion, seized the extended hand, and soon found himself beside Dr. Cairn.

Impetuously he snatched out his own lamp and shone its beams about the weird apartment in which they found themselves﻿—the so-called King’s Chamber of the pyramid. Right and left leapt the searching rays, touching the ends of the wooden beams, which, practically fossilised by long contact with the rock, still survive in that sepulchral place. Above and below and all around he directed the light﻿—upon the litter covering the rock floor, upon the blocks of the higher walls, upon the frowning roof.

They were alone in the King’s Chamber!

===Chinese===
他在对方肩头渐渐直起身来；随即一脚踩进一道岩缝，左手抓住上方凸出的一块岩石，又攀高了些，右手自始至终举着点亮的那盏灯。他的手臂向上伸，再向上，直到能把灯搁上头顶的岩台，灯的白光便横照过井口的顶面。

「留神别让它摔下来！」西姆仰起脖子看着这一连串动作，喘着气喊道。

凯恩博士的膂力和身手矫健得惊人，他侧身一拧，竟把脚蹬上了竖井对面的一道石台。他把体重压上去，伸手够到井口的边沿，引体向上翻到井顶，整个人蹲进了灯光里。然后他一脚楔进身下方的一道裂缝，把手伸向西姆。后者走的路数与同伴大同小异，一把抓住那只伸来的手，很快便到了凯恩博士身边。

他急不可待地抽出自己的灯，让光束扫遍他们置身其中的这间怪异屋子——金字塔里那所谓的国王墓室。搜索的光柱左右腾跃，掠过一根根木梁的端头；那些木梁与岩石长年相触，几乎已经石化，却仍在这墓葬之地存留至今。他把灯光上下四方地照——照着石地上覆盖的杂物，照着高处石砌的墙体，照着那阴沉沉压顶的窟顶。

国王墓室里，只有他们两个！

## XIX Anthropomancy / XIX 人牲占卜

===Original===
“There is no one here!”

Sime looked about the place excitedly.

“Fortunately for us!” answered Dr. Cairn.

He breathed rather heavily yet with his exertions, and, moreover, the air of the chamber was disgusting. But otherwise he was perfectly calm, although his face was pale and bathed in perspiration.

“Make as little noise as possible.”

Sime, who, now that the place proved to be empty, began to cast off that dread which had possessed him in the passageway, found something ominous in the words.

===Chinese===
「这里没有人！」

西姆激动地四下环顾这地方。

「真是我们的万幸！」凯恩博士答道。

方才那番剧烈攀爬仍使他喘息粗重，何况这墓室里的空气又叫人作呕。但除此之外他镇定自若，只是脸色苍白，满头是汗。

「尽量别弄出声响。」

这地方既已证明空无一人，西姆在通道里曾被攫住的那股恐惧本已渐渐卸去，此刻却从这句话里听出了某种不祥的意味。

===Original===
Dr. Cairn, stepping carefully over the rubbish of the floor, advanced to the east corner of the chamber, waving his companion to follow. Side by side they stood there.

“Do you notice that the abominable smell of the incense is more overpowering here than anywhere?”

Sime nodded.

“You are right. What does that mean?”

Dr. Cairn directed the ray of light down behind a little mound of rubbish into a corner of the wall.

===Chinese===
凯恩博士小心地踩过地上的碎石瓦砾，走向墓室的东角，招手示意同伴跟上。两人并肩在那里站定。

「你注意到没有，那股神香的可憎气味，在这里比哪儿都更呛人？」

西姆点了点头。

「您说得对。这意味着什么？」

凯恩博士把光柱压低，照进一小堆瓦砾后面、靠墙的一角。

===Original===
“It means,” he said, with a subdued expression of excitement, “that we have got to crawl in *there*!”

Sime stifled an exclamation.

One of the blocks of the bottom tier was missing, a fact which he had not detected before by reason of the presence of the mound of rubbish before the opening.

“Silence again!” whispered Dr. Cairn.

He lay down flat, and, without hesitation, crept into the gap. As his feet disappeared, Sime followed. Here it was possible to crawl upon hands and knees. The passage was formed of square stone blocks. It was but three yards or so in length; then it suddenly turned upward at a tremendous angle of about one in four. Square footholds were cut in the lower face. The smell of incense was almost unbearable.

===Chinese===
「这意味着，」他说，神情里压着一种按捺的兴奋，「我们得从『那儿』爬进去！」

西姆强忍住一声惊呼。

底层那排石块缺了一块——先前正因为洞口前堆着那堆瓦砾，他没有察觉。

「再别出声！」凯恩博士低声道。

他直挺挺地趴下，毫不犹豫地钻进缺口。他的双脚一消失，西姆便跟了进去。到这里可以手脚并用地爬了。这条通道由方正的石块砌成，长仅三码左右，随即以约一比四的惊人陡角猛然向上折起。朝下的那一面凿出了一方方踏脚孔。神香的气味几乎令人无法忍受。

===Original===
Dr. Cairn bent to Sime’s ear.

“Not a word, now,” he said. “No light﻿—pistol ready!”

He began to mount. Sime, following, counted the steps. When they had mounted sixty he knew that they must have come close to the top of the original *mastabah*, and close to the first stage of the pyramid. Despite the shaft beneath, there was little danger of falling, for one could lean back against the wall while seeking for the foothold above.

===Chinese===
凯恩博士俯到西姆耳边。

「现在一个字也别出口，」他说。「不许亮光——枪备好！」

他开始向上攀爬。西姆跟在后面，数着级数。攀满六十级时，他知道他们必定已临近原初马斯塔巴（mastabah，古埃及平顶墓）的顶点，也临近金字塔的第一层台基了。尽管脚下便是深井，坠落的危险却甚微，因为伸手向上摸索踏脚孔时，人尽可仰背抵住井壁。

===Original===
Dr. Cairn mounted very slowly, fearful of striking his head upon some obstacle. Then on the seventieth step, he found that he could thrust his foot forward and that no obstruction met his knee. They had reached a horizontal passage.

Very softly he whispered back to Sime:

“Take my hand. I have reached the top.”

They entered the passage. The heavy, sickly sweet odour almost overpowered them, but, grimly set upon their purpose, they, after one moment of hesitancy, crept on.

===Chinese===
凯恩博士爬得极慢，生怕一头撞上什么障碍物。到第七十级上，他发觉自己能把脚伸向前去，膝盖也没有碰到任何阻拦。他们到达了一条水平的通道。

他极轻地回头对西姆耳语：

「拉住我的手。我到顶了。」

他们进入通道。那股沉滞的甜腻气味几乎将他们熏倒，但两人咬牙认定了此行的目的，迟疑片刻，便继续向前爬去。

===Original===
A fitful light rose and fell ahead of them. It gleamed upon the polished walls of the corridor in which they now found themselves﻿—that inexplicable light burning in a place which had known no light since the dim ages of the early Pharaohs!

The events of that incredible night had afforded no such emotion as this. This was the crowning wonder, and, in its dreadful mystery, the crowning terror of Méydûm.

When first that lambent light played upon the walls of the passage both stopped, stricken motionless with fear and amazement. Sime, who would have been prepared to swear that the Méydûm Pyramid contained no apartment other than the King’s Chamber, now was past mere wonder, past conjecture. But he could still fear. Dr. Cairn, although he had anticipated this, temporarily also fell a victim to the supernatural character of the phenomenon.

===Chinese===
前方有一阵忽明忽暗的光起伏着。它照亮了他们此刻置身其间的走廊那磨光的四壁——那不可解的光，竟燃烧在一个自早期法老的蒙昧年代以来就不曾识得光为何物的地方！

那个难以置信之夜的种种变故，都未曾带来这样的情绪。这是登峰造极的奇观，而以它那可怖的神秘，也是美杜姆（Méydûm）登峰造极的恐怖。

那幽幽的光初次在通道四壁上闪动时，两人都停住了，被恐惧与惊骇钉在原地。西姆本可以起誓说，美杜姆金字塔里除了国王墓室（King's Chamber）再无别室，此刻他早已越过了惊奇，越过了揣测。但他还懂得害怕。凯恩博士虽有预料，这时也暂时成了这超自然现象的俘虏。

===Original===
They advanced.

They looked into a square chamber of about the same size as the King’s Chamber. In fact, although they did not realise it until later, this second apartment, no doubt was situated directly above the first.

The only light was that of a fire burning in a tripod, and by means of this illumination, which rose and fell in a strange manner, it was possible to perceive the details of the place. But, indeed, at the moment they were not concerned with these; they had eyes only for the black-robed figure beside the tripod.

It was that of a man, who stood with his back towards them, and he chanted monotonously in a tongue unfamiliar to Sime. At certain points in his chant he would raise his arms in such a way that, clad in the black robe, he assumed the appearance of a gigantic bat. Each time that he acted thus the fire in the tripod, as if fanned into new life, would leap up, casting a hellish glare about the place. Then, as the chanter dropped his arms again, the flame would drop also.

A cloud of reddish vapour floated low in the apartment. There were a number of curiously-shaped vessels upon the floor, and against the farther wall, only rendered visible when the flames leapt high, was some motionless white object, apparently hung from the roof.

===Chinese===
他们向前走去。

他们望进一间方形墓室，大小与国王墓室相仿。事实上——虽然他们后来才意识到——这第二间墓室，毫无疑问正位于第一间的正上方。

唯一的光，是三足鼎里燃烧的一堆火；借着这忽高忽低、变化奇诡的照明，才看得清室内的种种细节。不过眼下他们根本顾不上这些；他们眼中只有三足鼎旁那个黑袍身影。

那是一个男人，背朝着他们站着，用一种西姆听不懂的语言单调地吟诵着。每到吟诵的某些节拍，他便扬起双臂，这样一来看着黑袍裹身，活脱脱像一只巨蝠。每逢他这样动作，三足鼎中的火便仿佛被扇得重新旺起来，骤然蹿升，把一片地狱般的光焰投向四壁。随后吟诵者垂下手臂，火焰也随之低伏。

一团淡红的雾气低低地浮在室中。地上摆着若干形状古怪的器皿；而在远处的墙前——唯有火焰蹿高时才看得见——有个纹丝不动的白色物体，看样子是从屋顶悬吊下来的。

===Original===
Dr. Cairn drew a hissing breath and grasped Sime’s wrist.

“We are too late!” he said strangely.

He spoke at a moment when his companion, peering through the ruddy gloom of the place, had been endeavouring more clearly to perceive that ominous shape which hung, horrible, in the shadow. He spoke, too, at a moment when the man in the black robe, raised his arms﻿—when, as if obedient to his will, the flames leapt up fitfully.

Although Sime could not be sure of what he saw, the recollection came to him of words recently spoken by Dr. Cairn. He remembered the story of Julian the Apostate, Julian the Emperor﻿—the Necromancer. He remembered what had been found in the Temple of the Moon after Julian’s death. He remembered that Lady Lashmore﻿—

And thereupon he experienced such a nausea that but for the fact that Dr. Cairn gripped him he must have fallen.

===Chinese===
凯恩博士嘶地倒吸一口气，一把攥住西姆的手腕。

「我们来迟了！」他语声怪异地说。

他说这话的一刻，他的同伴正透过室内红暗的幽光，竭力想看清那悬在阴影里的骇人不祥之形。他说这话的一刻，也恰是黑袍人扬起双臂的一刻——火焰仿佛听命于他的意志，一阵一阵地蹿跳起来。

西姆虽不能确信自己看见了什么，凯恩博士新近说过的话却浮上他的记忆。他想起叛教者尤利安（Julian the Apostate）的故事——皇帝尤利安——那位亡灵巫师。他想起尤利安死后人们在月神庙（Temple of the Moon）里的发现。他想起拉什莫尔夫人——

随即一阵恶心涌上心头，若不是凯恩博士紧紧攥住他，他必定已经栽倒在地。

===Original===
Tutored in a materialistic school, he could not even now admit that such monstrous things could be. With a necromantic operation taking place before his eyes; with the unholy perfume of the secret incense all but suffocating him; with the dreadful Oracle dully gleaming in the shadows of that temple of evil﻿—his reason would not accept the evidences. Any man of the ancient world﻿—of the middle ages﻿—would have known that he looked upon a professed wizard, upon a magician, who, according to one of the most ancient formulae known to mankind, was seeking to question the dead respecting the living.

But how many modern men are there capable of realising such a circumstance? How many who would accept the statement that such operations are still performed, not only in the East, but in Europe? How many who, witnessing this mass of Satan, would accept it for verity, would not deny the evidence of their very senses?

He could not believe such an orgy of wickedness possible. A Pagan emperor might have been capable of these things, but today﻿—wondrous is our faith in the virtue of “today!”

===Chinese===
他自幼受的是唯物学派的教化，即便到了此刻，也无法承认这等怪诞之事竟会存在。一场亡灵巫术的仪轨正在他眼前进行；秘香那不洁的芬芳几乎令他窒息；那可怖的神谕（Oracle）在邪庙的阴影里幽幽发亮——可他的理智不肯接受这些证据。任何一个古代世界的人——任何一个中世纪的人——都会知道，自己眼前是一个公开行术的巫师，一个魔法师，正依照人类已知最古老的法仪之一，试图向亡者叩问生者的事。

可是，又有多少现代人能领会这样的境况？多少人肯相信这样的法仪至今仍在举行——不仅在东方，就在欧洲也一样？多少人目睹了这场撒旦的弥撒，肯信它为真，而不否认自己感官的证据？

他不信这样一场邪恶的狂欢竟有可能。一位异教皇帝或做得来这些事，可是今天——我们对于「今天」之德性的信心，实在奇妙得很！

===Original===
“Am I mad?” he whispered hoarsely, “or﻿—”

A thinly-veiled shape seemed to float out from that still form in the shadows; it assumed definite outlines; it became a woman, beautiful with a beauty that could only be described as awful.

She wore upon her brow the uraeus of Ancient Egyptian royalty; her sole garment was a robe of finest gauze. Like a cloud, like a vision, she floated into the light cast by the tripod.

A voice﻿—a voice which seemed to come from a vast distance, from somewhere outside the mighty granite walls of that unholy place﻿—spoke. The language was unknown to Sime, but the fierce handgrip upon his wrist grew fiercer. That dead tongue, that language unspoken since the dawn of Christianity, was known to the man who had been the companion of Sir Michael Ferrara.

===Chinese===
「我是疯了吗？」他声音嘶哑地低语，「还是——」

一个薄纱轻笼的形体，仿佛从阴影中那具静止之躯里飘荡而出；它显出清晰的轮廓；它成为一个女子，美得只能用「骇人」来形容。

她额上戴着古埃及王室的圣蛇标；她唯一的衣裳是一袭至薄的轻纱袍。像一朵云，像一场幻象，她飘进了三足鼎投下的光里。

一个声音开口了——那声音仿佛来自极远之处，来自那不洁之地的巍峨花岗岩石墙之外的某处。这语言西姆不懂，可他腕上那只凶狠紧攥的手攥得更凶了。那种死亡的语言，那种自基督教发端以来再无人说出的语言，这位曾与迈克尔·费拉拉爵士为伴的人是通晓的。

===Original===
In upon Sime swept a swift conviction﻿—that one could not witness such a scene as this and live and move again amongst one’s fellow-men! In a sort of frenzy, then, he wrenched himself free from the detaining hand, and launched a retort of modern science against the challenge of ancient sorcery.

Raising his Browning pistol, he fired﻿—shot after shot﻿—at that bat-like shape which stood between himself and the tripod!

A thousand frightful echoes filled the chamber with a demon mockery, boomed along those subterranean passages beneath, and bore the conflict of sound into the hidden places of the pyramid which had known not sound for untold generations.

“My God﻿—!”

===Chinese===
一个迅疾的信念猛然袭上西姆心头——目睹过这样一幕的人，绝不能再活着回到同类中间起居行走！于是他在一阵狂乱中挣脱了那只挽留的手，以现代科学回击了古代巫术的挑衅。

他举起勃朗宁手枪，朝着那个立在他与三足鼎之间的蝠形黑影——一枪接一枪地开火！

千百声可怕的回响带着恶魔的嘲弄灌满了墓室，在下方那些地下通道里隆隆滚过，把这声浪的冲撞送进金字塔中那些不知多少世代未曾有过声音的隐秘角落。

「我的天——！」

===Original===
Vaguely he became aware that Dr. Cairn was seeking to drag him away. Through a cloud of smoke he saw the black-robed figure turn; dream fashion, he saw the pallid, glistening face of Antony Ferrara; the long, evil eyes, alight like the eyes of a serpent, were fixed upon him. He seemed to stand amid a chaos, in a mad world beyond the borders of reason, beyond the dominions of God. But to his stupefied mind one astounding fact found access.

He had fired at least seven shots at the black-robed figure, and it was not humanly possible that all could have gone wide of their mark.

Yet Antony Ferrara lived!

Utter darkness blotted out the evil vision. Then there was a white light ahead; and feeling that he was struggling for sanity, Sime managed to realise that Dr. Cairn, retreating along the passage, was crying to him, in a voice rising almost to a shriek, to run﻿—run for his life﻿—for his salvation!

===Chinese===
恍惚间他觉察到凯恩博士正竭力把他拖走。透过一团烟雾，他看见那黑袍身影转过身来；如在梦中，他看见了安东尼奥·费拉拉那张苍白发亮的脸；那双细长的邪魅眼睛，像蛇眼似的灼灼亮着，直盯住他。他仿佛置身一片混沌之中，置身一个越出理性疆界、越出上帝治域的疯狂世界。但在那惊呆的头脑里，有一个惊人的事实还是钻了进来。

他朝那黑袍身影至少开了七枪，而七枪全都脱靶，这在人力上是绝无可能的。

然而安东尼奥·费拉拉还活着！

一片漆黑抹去了那邪恶的景象。接着前方亮起一道白光；西姆觉得自己正在为守住神智而挣扎，总算明白了凯恩博士正沿通道退走，几乎是尖声喊叫着叫他快跑——逃命——为求灵魂的得救！

===Original===
“*You should not have fired!*” he seemed to hear.

Unconscious of any contact with the stones﻿—although afterwards he found his knees and shins to be bleeding﻿—he was scrambling down that long, sloping shaft.

He had a vague impression that Dr. Cairn, descending beneath him, sometimes grasped his ankles and placed his feet into the footholes. A continuous roaring sound filled his ears, as if a great ocean were casting its storm waves against the structure around him. The place seemed to rock.

“Down flat!”

===Chinese===
他仿佛听见一句：「『你不该开枪的！』」

他全然不觉自己何曾磕碰到石头——尽管事后发现膝盖和小腿都在流血——就这样手脚并用地滑下了那条长长的斜井。

他模糊地觉得，在他下方下井的凯恩博士不时抓住他的脚踝，把他的脚放进一个个踏脚孔里。一阵连绵的轰响灌满他的耳朵，仿佛一片大洋正把风暴的巨浪砸向他四周的构筑。整个地方仿佛都在摇晃。

「趴下！」

===Original===
Some sense of reality was returning to him. Now he perceived that Dr. Cairn was urging him to crawl back along the short passage by which they had entered from the King’s Chamber.

Heedless of hurt, he threw himself down and pressed on.

A blank, like the sleep of exhaustion which follows delirium, came. Then Sime found himself standing in the King’s Chamber, Dr. Cairn, who held an electric lamp in his hand, beside him, and half supporting him.

The realities suddenly reasserting themselves,

===Chinese===
某种现实感正回到他身上。此刻他看清了，凯恩博士正催着他沿那条短通道爬回去——他们从国王墓室进那撒旦邪庙时，走的就是此道。

他不顾伤痛，扑倒在地，奋力向前。

一阵空白袭来，如同谵妄过后精疲力竭的沉睡。随后西姆发觉自己已站在国王墓室里，凯恩博士手持电筒立在他身旁，半搀着他。

现实骤然间又重新占了上风，

===Original===
“I have dropped my pistol!” muttered Sime.

He threw off the supporting arm, and turned to that corner behind the heap of debris where was the opening through which they had entered the Satanic temple.

No opening was visible!

“He has closed it!” cried Dr. Cairn. “There are six stone doors between here and the place above! If he had succeeded in shutting *one* of them before we﻿—?”

“My God!” whispered Sime. “Let us get out! I am nearly at the end of my tether!”

===Chinese===
「我把手枪弄丢了！」西姆喃喃道。

他甩开搀扶的手臂，转身朝瓦砾堆后那个角落望去——他们进那座撒旦邪庙的洞口本在那里。

洞口竟看不见了！

「他把门关上了！」凯恩博士喊道。「从这里到上面的地方，隔着六道石门！要是他抢在我们前头，哪怕关上了『一道』——？」

「我的天！」西姆低声道。「快出去！我快要撑不住了！」

===Original===
Fear lends wings, and it was with something like the lightness of a bird that Sime descended the shaft. At the bottom﻿—

“On to my shoulders!” he cried, looking up.

Dr. Cairn lowered himself to the foot of the shaft. “You go first,” he said.

He was gasping, as if nearly suffocated, but retained a wonderful self-control. Once over into the Borderland, and bravery assumes a new guise; the courage which can face physical danger undaunted, melts in the fires of the unknown.

Sime, his breath whistling sibilantly between his clenched teeth, hauled himself through the low passage, with incredible speed. The two worked their way arduously, up the long slope. They saw the blue sky above them.﻿  ﻿

===Chinese===
恐惧借人以翼，西姆下那口竖井时，身轻得几乎像一只飞鸟。到了井底——

「踩到我肩膀上来！」他仰起头喊道。

凯恩博士自己缓缓下到井脚。「你先走。」他说。

他喘得透不过气来，像是几乎窒息，却仍保有惊人的自制。一旦踏过这道幽明的边界（the Borderland），勇毅便换了一副面目；那直面血肉之危而毫无惧色的胆气，在未知的烈火面前便消融了。

西姆咬紧牙关，齿缝间咝咝地抽着气，以难以置信的速度拖着自己钻过了那条低矮的通道。两人又艰苦地爬上长长的陡坡。他们看见了头顶的蓝天……

---

===Original===
“Something like a huge bat,” said Robert Cairn, “crawled out upon the first stage. We both fired﻿—”

Dr. Cairn raised his hand. He lay exhausted at the foot of the mound.

“He had lighted the incense,” he replied, “and was reciting the secret ritual. I cannot explain. But your shots were wasted. We came too late﻿—”

“Lady Lashmore﻿—”

“Until the Pyramid of Méydûm is pulled down, stone by stone, the world will never know her fate! Sime and I have looked in at the gate of hell! Only the hand of God plucked us back! Look!”

He pointed to Sime. He lay, pallid, with closed eyes﻿—and his hair was abundantly streaked with white!

===Chinese===
「有个东西，像一只巨蝠，」罗伯特·凯恩说，「爬上了第一层台基。我们俩都开了枪——」

凯恩博士抬起一只手。他力竭地躺在土丘脚下。

「他点起了神香，」他答道，「正在诵那秘传的法仪。我无法解释。可你们的子弹都白费了。我们来迟了——」

「拉什莫尔夫人——」

「除非把美杜姆金字塔一块石头一块石头地拆掉，这世界永远不会知道她的下场！我和西姆朝地狱之门里张望了一眼！唯有上帝之手把我们拽了回来！你看！」

他指向西姆。西姆躺在那里，面色苍白，双眼紧闭——而他满头的头发，已经斑斑驳驳地生出大片白发！

## XX The Incense / XX 神香

===Original===
To Robert Cairn it seemed that the boat-train would never reach Charing Cross. His restlessness was appalling. He perpetually glanced from his father, with whom he shared the compartment, to the flying landscape with its vistas of hop-poles; and Dr. Cairn, although he exhibited less anxiety, was, nevertheless, strung to highest tension.

That dash from Cairo homeward had been something of a fevered dream to both men. To learn, whilst one is searching for a malign and implacable enemy in Egypt, that that enemy, having secretly returned to London, is weaving his evil spells around “some we loved, the loveliest and the best,” is to know the meaning of ordeal.

In pursuit of Antony Ferrara﻿—the incarnation of an awful evil﻿—Dr. Cairn had deserted his practice, had left England for Egypt. Now he was hurrying back again; for whilst he had sought in strange and dark places of that land of mystery for Antony Ferrara, the latter had been darkly active in London!

Again and again Robert Cairn read the letter which, surely as a royal command, had recalled them. It was from Myra Duquesne. One line in it had fallen upon them like a bomb, had altered all their plans, had shattered the one fragment of peace remaining to them.

In the eyes of Robert Cairn, the whole universe centred around Myra Duquesne; she was the one being in the world of whom he could not bear to think in conjunction with Antony Ferrara. Now he knew that Antony Ferrara was beside her, was, doubtless at this very moment, directing those Black Arts of which he was master, to the destruction of her mind and body﻿—perhaps of her very soul.

===Chinese===
在罗伯特·凯恩看来，这趟接驳邮轮的列车仿佛永远到不了查令十字（Charing Cross）车站。他那坐立不安的劲儿简直骇人。他一会儿看看同坐一节车厢的父亲，一会儿又望望车窗外飞掠而过的田野——一排排蛇麻（啤酒花）架杆绵延入目；凯恩博士虽然显得不那么焦灼，神经却也早已绷到了极点。

从开罗一路奔回英国的仓促行程，对两人来说都像是发了一场高热的梦。一个人正在埃及搜寻某个狠毒而誓不两立的仇敌，却在这当口得知：那仇敌已悄然返回伦敦，正围着「一些我们所爱的人——那最可爱、最美好的人」编织他的邪咒——这才叫尝到了炼狱般煎熬的滋味。

为了追猎安东尼奥·费拉拉——一个可怖的邪恶化身——凯恩博士抛下了自己的诊所，离开英格兰去了埃及。如今他又火急火燎地赶了回来；因为当他在那片神秘之土的种种奇诡幽暗的角落里搜寻安东尼奥·费拉拉时，后者却一直在伦敦暗中兴妖作怪！

罗伯特·凯恩把那封信读了一遍又一遍——它确凿得像一道御诏，把他们召了回来。信是迈拉·迪凯纳写来的。其中一行字如炸弹般落在他们头上，改变了他们全部的计划，也把他们仅存的那一角安宁击得粉碎。

在罗伯特·凯恩眼里，整个宇宙都以迈拉·迪凯纳为中心；这世上他唯一无法忍受把她和安东尼奥·费拉拉放在一起去想的，就是她。如今他知道了，安东尼奥·费拉拉就在她身边，而且多半就在此刻，正把他所精通的那些黑巫术施展出来，用以摧残她的心智与肉体——也许还要摧残她的灵魂。

===Original===
Again he drew the worn envelope from his pocket and read that ominous sentence, which, when his eyes had first fallen upon it, had blotted out the sunlight of Egypt.

“… And you will be surprised to hear that Antony is back in London﻿ … and is a frequent visitor here. It is quite like old times.﻿ …”

Raising his haggard eyes, Robert Cairn saw that his father was watching him.

===Chinese===
他又一次从口袋里掏出那只磨旧了的信封，重读那句不祥的话——当初他的目光初初落到它上面时，它曾把埃及的阳光整个抹灭。

「……你们听到安东尼奥回了伦敦，一定会吃惊……他如今是这里的常客。真像回到了从前的好时光。……」

罗伯特·凯恩抬起憔悴的双眼，正看见父亲在注视着他。

===Original===
“Keep calm, my boy,” urged the doctor; “it can profit us nothing, it can profit Myra nothing, for you to shatter your nerves at a time when real trials are before you. You are inviting another breakdown. Oh! I know it is hard; but for everybody’s sake try to keep yourself in hand.”

“I am trying, sir,” replied Robert hollowly.

Dr. Cairn nodded, drumming his fingers upon his knee.

“We must be diplomatic,” he continued. “That James Saunderson proposed to return to London, I had no idea. I thought that Myra would be far outside the Black maelström in Scotland. Had I suspected that Saunderson would come to London, I should have made other arrangements.”

“Of course, sir, I know that. But even so we could never have foreseen this.”

Dr. Cairn shook his head.

===Chinese===
「沉住气，孩子，」博士劝道，「真正的考验已摆在眼前，这时候你若把自己的神经搞垮，于我们无益，于迈拉也无益。你这是在招来第二次崩溃。噢！我知道这难受；可看在众人的份上，千万克制住自己。」

「我在尽力，先生。」罗伯特空洞地答道。

凯恩博士点了点头，手指在膝头敲着鼓点。

「咱们得讲点手腕，」他接着说。「詹姆斯·桑德森（James Saunderson）竟打算回伦敦来，我事先一点也不知道。我原以为迈拉待在苏格兰，会远在这场黑巫术大漩涡之外。早料到桑德森会来伦敦，我自会另作安排。」

「当然，先生，这我知道。可即便如此，咱们也决计预见不到这一步。」

凯恩博士摇了摇头。

===Original===
“To think that whilst we have been scouring Egypt from Port Said to Assouan﻿—*he* has been laughing at us in London!” he said. “Directly after the affair at Méydûm he must have left the country﻿—how, Heaven only knows. That letter is three weeks old, now?”

Robert Cairn nodded. “What may have happened since﻿—what may have happened!”

“You take too gloomy a view. James Saunderson is a Roman guardian. Even Antony Ferrara could make little headway there.”

“But Myra says that﻿—Ferrara is﻿—a frequent visitor.”

“And Saunderson,” replied Dr. Cairn with a grim smile, “is a Scotchman! Rely upon his diplomacy, Rob. Myra will be safe enough.”

“God grant that she is!”

===Chinese===
「想想吧，咱们从塞得港到阿斯旺，把埃及搜了个遍——『他』却在伦敦笑话咱们！」他说。「准是美杜姆那场变故一了，他就立刻离了境——怎么出去的，只有天知道。那封信到现在有三个星期了吧？」

罗伯特·凯恩点了点头。「这中间可能出了什么事——可能出了什么事啊！」

「你把事情看得太悲观了。詹姆斯·桑德森是个罗马式的监护人。哪怕是安东尼奥·费拉拉，在那里也难有寸进。」

「可迈拉信上说——费拉拉——是这里的常客。」

「而桑德森，」凯恩博士带着冷峭的微笑答道，「是个苏格兰人！尽管放心倚仗他的圆滑周旋吧，罗布。迈拉保险得很。」

「但愿上帝保佑她真的平安！」

===Original===
At that, silence fell between them, until punctually to time, the train slowed into Charing Cross. Inspired by a common anxiety, Dr. Cairn and his son were first among the passengers to pass the barrier. The car was waiting for them; and within five minutes of the arrival of the train they were whirling through London’s traffic to the house of James Saunderson.

It lay in that quaint backwater, remote from motorbus highways﻿—Dulwich Common, and was a rambling red-tiled building which at some time had been a farmhouse. As the big car pulled up at the gate, Saunderson, a large-boned Scotchman, tawny-eyed, and with his grey hair worn long and untidily, came out to meet them. Myra Duquesne stood beside him. A quick blush coloured her face momentarily; then left it pale again.

Indeed, her pallor was alarming. As Robert Cairn, leaping from the car, seized both her hands and looked into her eyes, it seemed to him that the girl had almost an ethereal appearance. Something clutched at his heart, iced his blood; for Myra Duquesne seemed a creature scarcely belonging to the world of humanity﻿—seemed already half a spirit. The light in her sweet eyes was good to see; but her fragility, and a certain transparency of complexion, horrified him.

Yet, he knew that he must hide these fears from her; and turning to Mr. Saunderson, he shook him warmly by the hand, and the party of four passed by the low porch into the house.

===Chinese===
说完这话，两人便都沉默了，直到列车分秒不差地放缓车速，驶入查令十字。出于同一份焦灼，凯恩博士父子抢在所有乘客前头，最先通过了出站栅栏。汽车正等着他们；火车到站不过五分钟，他们已在伦敦的车流中疾驰，赶往詹姆斯·桑德森的宅子。

宅子坐落在德威奇公地（Dulwich Common）——一处远离公共汽车大道的古雅僻壤；屋子红瓦铺顶、格局散漫，早先某个年代原是一座农舍。大汽车在院门口停稳时，桑德森迎了出来——他是个骨架粗大的苏格兰人，一双黄褐色的眼睛，灰白的头发蓄得长长的、乱蓬蓬的。迈拉·迪凯纳站在他身旁。一抹急促的红晕霎时染上她的脸庞；随即又褪了下去，只留下原先的苍白。

说实在的，她的苍白触目惊心。罗伯特·凯恩跳下车，一把握住她的双手，望进她的眼睛里，只觉得这姑娘简直有了一种缥缈出尘之态。有什么东西攥紧了他的心，冻凉了他的血；因为迈拉·迪凯纳看上去几乎不像人类世界的一个生灵——仿佛已经是半个魂灵。她那双可爱眼睛里的光彩见了叫人欣慰；可她的孱弱，还有肌肤上某种近乎透明的色泽，却叫他骇然。

然而他知道，这些忧惧必须瞒过她；于是他转身向着桑德森先生，热情地同他握手，一行四人由低矮的门廊进屋去了。

===Original===
In the hallway Miss Saunderson, a typical Scottish housekeeper, stood beaming welcome; but in the very instant of greeting her, Robert Cairn stopped suddenly as if transfixed.

Dr. Cairn also pulled up just within the door, his nostrils quivering and his clear grey eyes turning right and left﻿—searching the shadows.

Miss Saunderson detected this sudden restraint.

“Is anything the matter?” she asked anxiously.

Myra, standing beside Mr. Saunderson, began to look frightened. But Dr. Cairn, shaking off the incubus which had descended upon him, forced a laugh, and clapping his hand upon Robert’s shoulder cried:

“Wake up, my boy! I know it is good to be back in England again, but keep your daydreaming for after lunch!”

===Chinese===
门厅里站着桑德森小姐，一位典型的苏格兰持家妇人，满面堆笑地迎候来客；可就在向她问好的当口，罗伯特·凯恩忽然顿住，像被什么钉在了原地。

凯恩博士也恰在门内停了下来，鼻翼翕张，一双清亮的灰眼睛左右转着——在阴影里搜寻。

桑德森小姐察觉了这突如其来的拘谨。

「有什么不对吗？」她不安地问。

站在桑德森先生身旁的迈拉，脸上开始露出惧色。但凯恩博士抖落那团骤然罩上身来的梦魇，强笑一声，一掌拍在罗伯特肩头，喊道：

「醒一醒，孩子！我知道重回英格兰有多美，可你的白日梦，留到午饭以后再做吧！」

===Original===
Robert Cairn forced a ghostly smile in return, and the odd incident promised soon to be forgotten.

“How good of you,” said Myra as the party entered the dining-room, “to come right from the station to see us. And you must be expected in Half-Moon Street, Dr. Cairn?”

“Of course we came to see *you* first,” replied Robert Cairn significantly.

Myra lowered her face and pursued that subject no further.

No mention was made of Antony Ferrara, and neither Dr. Cairn nor his son cared to broach the subject. The lunch passed off, then, without any reference to the very matter which had brought them there that day.

===Chinese===
罗伯特·凯恩强扯出一个幽魂似的微笑作答，这桩小小的怪事眼看就要被忘在脑后了。

「你们心眼儿真好，」一行人进餐厅时迈拉说道，「一下火车就直接来看我们。凯恩博士，半月街那边一定正等着你们吧？」

「我们当然是先来看『你』的。」罗伯特·凯恩意味深长地答道。

迈拉垂下了脸，不再继续这个话题。

谁也没有提起安东尼奥·费拉拉，凯恩博士父子也不愿先行开口。于是这顿午饭就这样过去了，对那件当天把他们引到这儿来的正事，竟只字未提。

===Original===
It was not until nearly an hour later that Dr. Cairn and his son found themselves alone for a moment. Then, with a furtive glance about him, the doctor spoke of that which had occupied his mind, to the exclusion of all else, since first they had entered the house of James Saunderson.

“You noticed it, Rob?” he whispered.

“My God! it nearly choked me!”

Dr. Cairn nodded grimly.

“It is all over the house,” he continued, “in every room that I have entered. They are used to it, and evidently do not notice it, but coming in from the clean air, it is﻿—”

“Abominable, unclean﻿—unholy!”

===Chinese===
直到将近一个钟头之后，凯恩博士父子才得了片刻独处的机会。这时，博士先悄悄朝四下里瞥了一眼，才说出那件自打踏进詹姆斯·桑德森家门起，便占尽他全部心神、再容不下其他的事。

「你察觉到了吧，罗布？」他低声说。

「我的上帝！它险些叫我透不过气来！」

凯恩博士神情冷峻地点了点头。

「整座宅子都是，」他接着说，「我进过的每一间屋都有。他们住惯了，显然并不觉得；可人从外头干净的空气里一进来，这气味——」

「可憎，不洁——邪渎！」

===Original===
“We know it,” continued Dr. Cairn softly﻿—“that smell of unholiness; we have good reason to know it. It heralded the death of Sir Michael Ferrara. It heralded the death of﻿—another.”

“With a just God in heaven, can such things be?”

“It is the secret incense of Ancient Egypt,” whispered Dr. Cairn, glancing towards the open door; “it is the odour of that Black Magic which, by all natural law, should be buried and lost forever in the tombs of the ancient wizards. Only two living men within my knowledge know the use and the hidden meaning of that perfume; only one living man has ever dared to make it﻿—to use it.﻿ …”

“Antony Ferrara﻿—”

“We knew he was here, boy; now we know that he is using his powers here. Something tells me that we come to the end of the fight. May victory be with the just.”

===Chinese===
「这气味我们认得，」凯恩博士轻声接着说——「那股邪渎之气；我们有十足的理由认得它。它曾预告过迈克尔·费拉拉爵士的死。它还预告过——另一个人的死。」

「天上有公义的上帝在，这样的事竟能容它存在？」

「这是古埃及的秘香，」凯恩博士朝敞开的房门瞥了一眼，低声说，「是那门黑巫术的气味——按一切自然的法则，它本该葬在古代巫师的坟墓里，永远失传。据我所知，当世只有两个活人懂得这种香的使用之法与隐秘含义；只有一个活人，曾经敢配制它——使用它。……」

「安东尼奥·费拉拉——」

「我们本就知道他在伦敦，孩子；如今我们知道，他正在这里施展他的力量了。冥冥中有什么告诉我，我们就要走到这场搏斗的尽头了。愿胜利与正义者同在。」

## XXI The Magician / XXI 魔法师

===Original===
Half-Moon Street was bathed in tropical sunlight. Dr. Cairn, with his hands behind him, stood looking out of the window. He turned to his son, who leant against a corner of the bookcase in the shadows of the big room.

“Hot enough for Egypt, Rob,” he said.

Robert Cairn nodded.

“Antony Ferrara,” he replied, “seemingly travels his own atmosphere with him. I first became acquainted with his hellish activities during a phenomenal thunderstorm. In Egypt his movements apparently corresponded with those of the khamsin. Now,”—he waved his hand vaguely towards the window—“this is Egypt in London.”

“Egypt is in London, indeed,” muttered Dr. Cairn. “Jermyn has decided that our fears are well-founded.”

“You mean, sir, that the will—?”

===Chinese===
半月街沐浴在热带的阳光里。凯恩博士双手负在身后，伫立窗前向外眺望。他转过身来，朝着儿子——年轻人正斜倚在大房间暗处书柜的一角。

「热得跟埃及一样了，罗布。」他说。

罗伯特·凯恩点了点头。

「安东尼奥·费拉拉，」他答道，「看来走到哪儿都随身带着他自己的一份气候。我初次领教他那些地狱般的行径，是在一场异乎寻常的雷暴之中。在埃及，他的行踪显然又总与喀新风相伴。而今，」——他朝窗子那边含糊地挥了挥手——「这可是伦敦城里的埃及。」

「埃及确实到伦敦来了。」凯恩博士喃喃道。「杰明已经认定，我们的忧虑是有根有据的。」

「先生，您是说，那份遗嘱——？」

===Original===
“Antony Ferrara would have an almost unassailable case in the event of—of Myra—”

“You mean that her share of the legacy would fall to that fiend, if she—”

“If she died? Exactly.”

Robert Cairn began to stride up and down the room, clenching and unclenching his fists. He was a shadow of his former self, but now his cheeks were flushed and his eyes feverishly bright.

“Before Heaven!” he cried suddenly, “the situation is becoming unbearable. A thing more deadly than the Plague is abroad here in London. Apart from the personal aspect of the matter—of which I dare not think!—what do we know of Ferrara’s activities? His record is damnable. To our certain knowledge his victims are many. If the murder of his adoptive father, Sir Michael, was actually the first of his crimes, we know of three other poor souls who beyond any shadow of doubt were launched into eternity by the Black Arts of this ghastly villain—”

===Chinese===
「一旦——一旦迈拉——安东尼奥·费拉拉就会在法律上握有一个几乎无懈可击的立场——」

「您是说，万一她——她那份遗产就会落进那恶魔手中？」

「一旦她死了？一点不错。」

罗伯特·凯恩开始在屋里大步踱来踱去，双拳握了又松，松了又握。他形容枯槁，早已大非昔比，此刻双颊却泛着红潮，双眼发着热病似的亮光。

「苍天在上！」他突然喊道，「这局面简直叫人忍无可忍了。一样比瘟疫还要致命的东西，正在这伦敦城里四处游荡。撇开此案的私人一面——那一面我连想都不敢想！——我们对费拉拉的行径究竟知道多少？他的劣迹天理难容。仅就我们确知的来说，他手下的冤魂已为数众多。倘若谋杀养父迈克尔爵士当真是他的头一桩罪行，那么我们还可数出另外三个可怜人，他们毫无疑问是被这个狰狞恶棍的黑巫术送进了永恒——」

===Original===
“We do, Rob,” replied Dr. Cairn sternly.

“He has made attempts upon you; he has made attempts upon me. We owe our survival”—he pointed to a row of books upon a corner shelf—“to the knowledge which you have accumulated in half a lifetime of research. In the face of science, in the face of modern scepticism, in the face of our belief in a benign God, this creature, Antony Ferrara, has proved himself conclusively to be—”

“He is what the benighted ancients called a magician,” interrupted Dr. Cairn quietly. “He is what was known in the Middle Ages as a wizard. What that means, exactly, few modern thinkers know; but I know, and one day others will know. Meanwhile his shadow lies upon a certain house.”

Robert Cairn shook his clenched fists in the air. In some men the gesture had seemed melodramatic; in him it was the expression of a soul’s agony.

===Chinese===
「我们知道得很清楚，罗布。」凯恩博士正色答道。

「他向您下过毒手；也向我下过毒手。我们能活到今日」——他指了指角落搁板上的一排书——「全仗您半生钻研积累下的学识。在科学的面前，在现代怀疑精神的面前，在我们对一位仁慈上帝的信仰面前，这个家伙，安东尼奥·费拉拉，已经确凿无疑地证明了他自己就是——」

「他就是蒙昧的古人所谓的魔法师，」凯恩博士平静地打断了他，「就是中世纪所称的巫师。这究竟意味着什么，当今的思想家少有人懂；但我懂，总有一天别人也会懂。而眼下，他的阴影正笼罩着一座宅子。」

罗伯特·凯恩挥动着紧攥的双拳。这姿态若出自某些人之手，便难免显得夸张做作；而在他，却是一个灵魂苦痛的宣泄。

===Original===
“But, sir!” he cried—“are we to wait, inert, helpless? Whatever he is, he has a human body and there are bullets, there are knives, there are a hundred drugs in the British Pharmacopoeia!”

“Quite so,” answered Dr. Cairn, watching his son closely, and, by his own collected manner, endeavouring to check the other’s growing excitement. “I am prepared at any personal risk to crush Antony Ferrara as I would crush a scorpion; but where is he?”

Robert Cairn groaned, dropping into the big red-leathern armchair, and burying his face in his hands.

“Our position is maddening,” continued the elder man. “We know that Antony Ferrara visits Mr. Saunderson’s house; we know that he is laughing at our vain attempts to trap him. Crowning comedy of all, Saunderson does not know the truth; he is not the type of man who could ever understand; in fact we dare not tell him—and we dare not tell Myra. The result is that those whom we would protect, unwittingly are working against us, and against themselves.”

===Chinese===
「可是，先生！」他叫道——「难道我们就这么干等着，束手无策？不管他是什么，他总有一具人的身体，而有子弹，有刀子，《英国药典》（British Pharmacopoeia）里还有上百种药物！」

「正是这样。」凯恩博士答道，一面紧盯着儿子，一面以自己的从容镇定，尽力遏制对方愈烧愈旺的激动。「我不惜冒任何个人风险，也要像捻死一只蝎子那样消灭安东尼奥·费拉拉；可是，他在哪儿？」

罗伯特·凯恩呻吟一声，颓然跌进那把红色大皮扶手椅里，双手掩住了脸。

「咱们的处境能把人逼疯。」年长的那位继续说道。「我们知道安东尼奥·费拉拉常去桑德森先生的宅子；我们知道他正嘲笑我们诱捕他的徒劳努力。而最最荒唐的是，桑德森本人并不知道真相；他也不是那种人，永远弄不明白这种事；事实上我们不敢告诉他——也不敢告诉迈拉。结果便是：我们想保护的人，正浑然不觉地与我们作对，也与他们自己作对。」

===Original===
“That perfume!” burst out Robert Cairn; “that hell’s incense which loads the atmosphere of Saunderson’s house! To think that we know what it means—that we know what it means!”

“Perhaps *I* know even better than you do, Rob. The occult uses of perfume are not understood nowadays; but you, from experience, know that certain perfumes have occult uses. At the Pyramid of Méydûm in Egypt, Antony Ferrara dared—and the just God did not strike him dead—to make a certain incense. It was often made in the remote past, and a portion of it, probably in a jar hermetically sealed, had come into his possession. I once detected its dreadful odour in his rooms in London. Had you asked me prior to that occasion if any of the hellish stuff had survived to the present day, I should most emphatically have said *no*; I should have been wrong. Ferrara had some. He used it all—and went to the Méydûm pyramid to renew his stock.”

Robert Cairn was listening intently.

===Chinese===
「那股香气！」罗伯特·凯恩脱口喊道；「那弥漫充塞在桑德森宅子空气里的地狱神香！想想吧，我们竟知道它意味着什么——我们竟知道它意味着什么！」

「也许『我』比你知道得还要透彻，罗布。香料的秘术用途如今已无人通晓；可你是身经其事的人，知道某些香料自有秘术的用途。在埃及的美杜姆金字塔，安东尼奥·费拉拉竟敢——而公义的上帝竟没有将他当场击毙——调制一种神香。这香在遥远的古代时常调制，其中一部分，多半封存在一只密不透气的罐子里，竟落进了他的手中。我曾在伦敦他的房间里侦出过那股可怕的气味。倘若在那以前你问我，这种地狱的玩意儿可曾有什么存留到今天，我准会斩钉截铁地答一声『不』；那我就错了。费拉拉手里有一些。他用了个精光——还专程去美杜姆金字塔补足了存货。」

罗伯特·凯恩听得全神贯注。

===Original===
“All this brings me back to a point which I have touched upon before, sir,” he said: “To my certain knowledge, the late Sir Michael and yourself have delved into the black mysteries of Egypt more deeply than any men of the present century. Yet Antony Ferrara, little more than a boy, has mastered secrets which you, after years of research, have failed to grasp. What does this mean, sir?”

Dr. Cairn, again locking his hands behind him, stared out of the window.

“He is not an ordinary mortal,” continued his son. “He is supernormal—and supernaturally wicked. You have admitted—indeed it was evident—that he is merely the adopted son of the late Sir Michael. Now that we have entered upon the final struggle—for I feel that this is so—I will ask you again: *Who is Antony Ferrara?*”

Dr. Cairn spun around upon the speaker; his grey eyes were very bright.

“There is one little obstacle,” he answered, “which has deterred me from telling you what you have asked so often. Although—and you have had dreadful opportunities to peer behind the veil—you will find it hard to believe, I hope very shortly to be able to answer that question, and to tell you who Antony Ferrara really is.”

===Chinese===
「这一切又把我带回我从前触及过的那个疑点，先生，」他说。「据我确切所知，已故的迈克尔爵士与您本人，对埃及黑色奥秘的钻研之深，本世纪无人能及。然而安东尼奥·费拉拉年岁不大，不过是个大孩子，却已掌握了一些您苦研多年仍未能参透的秘密。这意味着什么，先生？」

凯恩博士再次双手交锁在背后，凝望着窗外出神。

「他不是寻常的血肉凡胎，」他的儿子继续道。「他是个超常的存在——而且邪恶得超乎自然。您已经承认——事实上这也显而易见——他不过是已故迈克尔爵士的养子。既然我们已进入最后的搏斗——因为我感到确是如此——我要再问您一遍：『安东尼奥·费拉拉究竟是谁？』」

凯恩博士倏地转身，面向说话的人；他那双灰眼睛亮得出奇。

「有一重小小的障碍，」他答道，「使我迟迟没有把你问了这么多遍的事情说出来。虽然——而你也曾有过可怕的机会，得以窥见帷幕之后——你会觉得难以置信，我却希望不久之后便能回答那个问题，告诉你安东尼奥·费拉拉究竟是谁。」

===Original===
Robert Cairn beat his fist upon the arm of the chair.

“I sometimes wonder,” he said, “that either of us has remained sane. Oh! what does it mean? What can we do? What can we do?”

“We must watch, Rob. To enlist the services of Saunderson, would be almost impossible; he lives in his orchid houses; they are his world. In matters of ordinary life I can trust him above most men, but in this—”

He shrugged his shoulders.

“Could we suggest to him a reason—any reason but the real one—why he should refuse to receive Ferrara?”

===Chinese===
罗伯特·凯恩一拳捶在椅子扶手上。

「有时我简直纳闷，」他说，「咱们俩怎么居然都还保得住清醒的神志。噢！这到底意味着什么？我们能做什么？我们能做什么？」

「我们必须监视，罗布。要借助桑德森之力，几乎不可能；他整天待在他的兰室里；那就是他的整个世界。在日常事务上，我信任他胜过信任大多数人，可在这件事上——」

他耸了耸肩。

「我们能不能向他进一个理由——除真实缘由之外的任何理由——好叫他拒绝接待费拉拉？」

===Original===
“It might destroy our last chance.”

“But sir,” cried Robert wildly, “it amounts to this: we are using Myra as a lure!”

“In order to save her, Rob—simply in order to save her,” retorted Dr. Cairn sternly.

“How ill she looks,” groaned the other; “how pale and worn. There are great shadows under her eyes—oh! I cannot bear to think about her!”

“When was *he* last there?”

===Chinese===
「那也许会毁掉我们最后的机会。」

「可是先生，」罗伯特狂乱地喊道，「事情说穿了就是：我们是在拿迈拉当诱饵啊！」

「为了救她，罗布——纯粹是为了救她。」凯恩博士厉声反驳道。

「她瞧上去病得多重啊，」另一个呻吟道；「多么苍白，多么憔悴。她眼睛底下压着深深的暗影——噢！我不忍去想她的模样！」

「『他』上一次去那儿是什么时候？」

===Original===
“Apparently some ten days ago. You may depend upon him to be aware of our return! He will not come there again, sir. But there are other ways in which he might reach her—does he not command a whole shadow army! And Mr. Saunderson is entirely unsuspicious—and Myra thinks of the fiend as a brother! Yet—she has never once spoken of him. I wonder. …”

Dr. Cairn sat deep in reflection. Suddenly he took out his watch.

“Go around now,” he said—“you will be in time for lunch—and remain there until I come. From today onward, although actually your health does not permit of the strain, we must watch, watch night and day.”

===Chinese===
「大概十天前吧。您尽管放心，他一定已经知道我们回来了！他不会再上那儿去了，先生。可他还有别的路子够到她——他岂不是统领着一整支影子大军！而桑德森先生毫无戒备——迈拉更把那恶魔当作兄长！可是——她还从来没有提起过他一次。我纳闷。……」

凯恩博士陷入了深深的沉思。突然，他掏出怀表。

「现在就过去，」他说——「你还赶得上午饭——然后守在那儿，一直等到我来。从今天起，尽管实际上你的身体根本经不起这般操劳，我们也必须监视，日夜不休地监视。」

## XXII Myra / XXII 迈拉

===Original===
Myra Duquesne came under an arch of roses to the wooden seat where Robert Cairn awaited her. In her plain white linen frock, with the sun in her hair and her eyes looking unnaturally large, owing to the pallor of her beautiful face, she seemed to the man who rose to greet her an ethereal creature, but lightly linked to the flesh and blood world.
An impulse, which had possessed him often enough before, but which hitherto he had suppressed, suddenly possessed him anew, set his heart beating, and filled his veins with fire. As a soft blush spread over the girl’s pale cheeks, and, with a sort of timidity, she held out her hand, he leapt to his feet, threw his arms around her, and kissed her; kissed her eyes, her hair, her lips!
There was a moment of frightened hesitancy﻿ ﻿… and then she had resigned herself to this sort of savage tenderness which was better in its very brutality than any caress she had ever known, which thrilled her with a glorious joy such as, she realised now, she had dreamt of and lacked, and wanted; which was a harbourage to which she came, blushing, confused﻿—but glad, conquered, and happy in the thrall of that exquisite slavery.

===Chinese===
迈拉·迪凯纳穿过一道玫瑰花拱门，走向罗伯特·凯恩等候着她的那条木凳。她穿着素净的白色亚麻布裙，阳光映在发间，美丽的脸庞一片苍白，衬得那双眼睛大得出奇——在起身相迎的男人眼里，她俨然是个空灵的生灵，与血肉的尘世之间不过轻牵一线。
一股冲动——此前不知多少回攫住过他，却都被他按捺了下去——倏然重新攫住了他，擂响了他的心房，往他的血脉里灌满了火。当淡淡的红晕漫上姑娘苍白的脸颊、她略带怯意地伸出手来时，他一跃而起，张开双臂把她搂住，吻了她；吻她的眼睛，她的头发，她的嘴唇！
有那么一瞬，她惊惶地迟疑着……随即便听任自己顺从了这种粗野的温柔——单凭它的粗野，便胜过她所领受过的任何爱抚；它令她战栗着涌起一种辉煌的欢喜，她此刻才明白，这正是她梦见过、欠缺过、也渴求着的；它是一处栖泊的港湾，她红着脸、心慌意乱地投了进来——却是欢喜的、被征服的，在那甘美奴役的束缚中幸福不已。

===Original===
“Myra,” he whispered, “Myra! have I frightened you? Will you forgive me?﻿—”
She nodded her head quickly and nestled upon his shoulder.
“I could wait no longer,” he murmured in her ear. “Words seemed unnecessary; I just wanted you; you are everything in the world; and,”﻿—he concluded simply﻿—“I took you.”
She whispered his name, very softly. What a serenity there is in such a moment, what a glow of secure happiness, of immunity from the pains and sorrows of the world!

===Chinese===
「迈拉，」他低声道，「迈拉！我吓着你了吗？你能原谅我吗？——」
她急急地点了点头，偎依在他肩头。
「我再也等不下去了，」他在她耳边低语。「言语似乎是多余的；我只是要你；你就是这世上的一切；还有，」——他干脆利落地收了尾——「于是我占有了你。」
她轻轻唤了他的名字，轻得不能再轻。这样的时刻里有着怎样的安宁，有着怎样一团安稳的幸福之光，怎样一种免于尘世伤痛哀愁的庇护啊！

===Original===
Robert Cairn, his arms about this girl, who, from his early boyhood, had been his ideal of womanhood, of love, and of all that love meant, forgot those things which had shaken his life and brought him to the threshold of death, forgot those evidences of illness which marred the once glorious beauty of the girl, forgot the black menace of the future, forgot the wizard enemy whose hand was stretched over that house and that garden﻿—and was merely happy.
But this paroxysm of gladness﻿—which Eliphas Lévi, last of the Adepts, has so marvellously analysed in one of his works﻿—is of short duration, as are all joys. It is needless to recount, here, the broken sentences (punctuated with those first kisses which sweeten the memory of old age) that now passed for conversation, and which lovers have believed to be conversation since the world began. As dusk creeps over a glorious landscape, so the shadow of Antony Ferrara crept over the happiness of these two.
Gradually that shadow fell between them and the sun; the grim thing which loomed big in the lives of them both, refused any longer to be ignored. Robert Cairn, his arm about the girl’s waist, broached the hated subject.

===Chinese===
罗伯特·凯恩搂着这个姑娘——打从少年时代起，她便是他心中女性的理想、爱的理想、爱所意味着的一切的理想——忘掉了那些撼动过他生命、把他带到死亡门槛前的事，忘掉了那些损毁姑娘昔日光艳之美的病征，忘掉了未来的黑色威胁，忘掉了那位巫师仇敌——他的手正罩着这座宅子、这片园子——只管自顾自地幸福着。
然而这一阵欢喜的狂潮——最后一位法师埃利法斯·莱维（Eliphas Lévi）曾在自己的一部著作里做过精妙绝伦的分析——是短促的，一如所有的欢乐。此处无需赘述，那些断断续续的语句（其间以初吻作标点——正是它们，把老年的回忆酿成甘甜）如今如何权充了谈话——而自开天辟地以来，恋人们就一直相信那就是谈话。正如暮色悄悄漫过壮丽的景色，安东尼奥·费拉拉的暗影也这样悄悄漫过了这两个人的幸福。
那道暗影渐渐落到他们与太阳之间；那件在两人生命里都赫然耸现的狰狞之事，再也不肯被置之不理了。罗伯特·凯恩揽着姑娘的腰，提起了那个可恨的话题。

===Original===
“When did you last see﻿—Ferrara?”
Myra looked up suddenly.
“Over a week﻿—nearly a fortnight, ago﻿—”
“Ah!”

===Chinese===
「你上回见到——费拉拉，是什么时候？」
迈拉猛地抬起头来。
「有一个多星期了——快两个星期了——」
「啊！」

===Original===
Cairn noted that the girl spoke of Ferrara with an odd sort of restraint for which he was at a loss to account. Myra had always regarded her guardian’s adopted son in the light of a brother; therefore her present attitude was all the more singular.
“You did not expect him to return to England so soon?” he asked.
“I had no idea that he was in England,” said Myra, “until he walked in here one day. I was glad to see him﻿—then.”
“And should you not be glad to see him now?” inquired Cairn eagerly.

===Chinese===
凯恩注意到，姑娘说起费拉拉时带着一种古怪的拘束，叫他无从解释。迈拉一向把监护人的养子当作兄长看待；因此她眼下的态度就愈发异样了。
「你没想到他会这么快就回英国？」他问。
「我根本不知道他到了英国，」迈拉说，「直到有一天他走进了这里。见到他我很高兴——在那时候。」
「那么如今见到他，你不也该高兴吗？」凯恩急切地问。

===Original===
Myra, her head lowered, deliberately pressed out a crease in her white skirt.
“One day, last week,” she replied slowly, “he﻿—came here, and﻿—acted strangely﻿—”
“In what way?” jerked Cairn.
“He pointed out to me that actually we﻿—he and I﻿—were in no way related.”

===Chinese===
迈拉垂着头，慢条斯理地抚平白裙上的一道褶痕。
「上个星期有一天，」她缓缓答道，「他——到这儿来过，而且——举止怪异——」
「怎么个怪法？」凯恩脱口问道。
「他向我指出，实际上我们——他和我——没有任何亲缘关系。」

===Original===
“Well?”
“You know how I have always liked Antony? I have always thought of him as my brother.”
Again she hesitated, and a troubled expression crept over her pale face. Cairn raised his arm and clasped it about her shoulders.
“Tell me all about it,” he whispered reassuringly.

===Chinese===
「嗯？」
「你知道我一向多喜欢安东尼奥吧？我一直把他当作我的兄长。」
她又迟疑起来，一层忧色悄悄爬上她苍白的脸。凯恩抬起手臂，环住她的双肩。
「把一切都告诉我吧，」他宽慰地低声道。

===Original===
“Well,” continued Myra in evident confusion, “his behaviour became﻿—embarrassing; and suddenly﻿—he asked me if I could ever love him, not as a brother, but﻿—”
“I understand!” said Cairn grimly. “And you replied?”
“For some time I could not reply at all: I was so surprised, and so﻿—horrified. I cannot explain how I felt about it, but it seemed horrible﻿—it seemed horrible!﻿—”

===Chinese===
「唉，」迈拉接着说，显然心慌意乱，「他的举止变得——叫人难堪；随后突然——他问我，我将来能不能爱他，不是当作兄长，而是——」
「我明白了！」凯恩冷冷地说。「你是怎么回答的？」
「有好一阵子我根本答不上话来：我太惊讶了，也太——惊骇了。我说不清自己当时是什么感受，只觉得可怕——实在可怕！——」

===Original===
“But of course, you told him?”
“I told him that I could never be fond of him in any different way﻿—that I could never *think* of it. And although I endeavoured to avoid hurting his feelings, he﻿—took it very badly. He said, in such a queer, choking voice, that he was going away﻿—”
“Away!﻿—from England?”
“Yes; and﻿—he made a strange request.”

===Chinese===
「不过，你当然是跟他挑明了的？」
「我告诉他，我永远不可能换一种方式喜欢他——那种事我连『想』都不可能去想。尽管我竭力避免伤他的心，他还是——大受打击。他说他要走了——那声音又古怪又发哽——」
「走！——离开英国？」
「是的；而且——他提了一个古怪的请求。」

===Original===
“What was it?”
“In the circumstances﻿—you see﻿—I felt sorry for him﻿—I did not like to refuse him; it was only a trifling thing. He asked for a lock of my hair!”
“A lock of your hair! And you﻿—”
“I told you that I did not like to refuse﻿—and I let him snip off a tiny piece, with a pair of pocket scissors which he had. Are you angry?”

===Chinese===
「什么请求？」
「在当时那种情形下——你是知道的——我替他难过——不忍心拒绝他；那不过是桩微不足道的小事。他要了我的一绺头发！」
「你的一绺头发！那你——」
「我说过我不忍心拒绝——就让他剪下了一小绺，用的是他随身带着的一把袖珍剪刀。你生气了吗？」

===Original===
“Of course not! You﻿—were almost brought up together. You﻿—?”
“Then﻿—” she paused﻿—“he seemed to change. Suddenly, I found myself afraid﻿—dreadfully afraid﻿—”
“Of Ferrara?”
“Not of Antony, exactly. But what is the good of my trying to explain! A most awful dread seized me. His face was no longer the face that I have always known; something﻿—”

===Chinese===
「当然没有！你们——差不多是一块儿长大的。你——？」
「后来——」她顿了顿——「他像是变了一个人。突然间，我发觉自己害怕起来——怕得要命——」
「怕费拉拉？」
「倒不完全是怕安东尼奥。可我再怎么解释又有什么用呢！一种莫大的恐惧攫住了我。他的脸不再是我一向认识的那张脸了；有什么东西——」

===Original===
Her voice trembled, and she seemed disposed to leave the sentence unfinished; then:
“Something evil﻿—sinister, had come into it.”
“And since then,” said Cairn, “you have not seen him?”
“He has not been here since then﻿—no.”

===Chinese===
她的声音颤抖起来，似乎打算就让这半截话悬着；接着还是开了口：
「某种邪恶的——阴森的东西，进了那张脸。」
「那么自那以后，」凯恩说，「你就再没见过他？」
「自那以后他就没来过这儿——没有。」

===Original===
Cairn, his hands resting upon the girl’s shoulders, leant back in the seat, and looked into her troubled eyes with a kind of sad scrutiny.
“You have not been fretting about him?”
Myra shook her head.
“Yet you look as though something were troubling you. This house”﻿—he indicated the low-lying garden with a certain irritation﻿—“is not healthily situated. This place lies in a valley; look at the rank grass﻿—and there are mosquitoes everywhere. You do not look well, Myra.”

===Chinese===
凯恩双手搭在姑娘肩上，朝椅背上一靠，带着一种凄然的端详，望进她那双烦忧的眼睛。
「你没有一直在为他忧心吧？」
迈拉摇了摇头。
「可你看上去倒像有什么事烦心。这座宅子」——他略带愠意地朝那片低洼的园子一指——「所处的位置不利于健康。这地方坐落在一道谷地里；瞧瞧这些疯长的荒草——而且四处都是蚊子。你气色不好，迈拉。」

===Original===
The girl smiled﻿—a little wistful smile.
“But I was so tired of Scotland,” she said. “You do not know how I looked forward to London again. I must admit, though, that I was in better health there; I was quite ashamed of my dairymaid appearance.”
“You have nothing to amuse you here,” said Cairn tenderly; “no company, for Mr. Saunderson only lives for his orchids.”
“They are very fascinating,” said Myra dreamily, “I, too, have felt their glamour. I am the only member of the household whom he allows amongst his orchids﻿—”

===Chinese===
姑娘笑了——一丝怅然的浅笑。
「可我在苏格兰实在待腻了，」她说，「你不知道我一直盼着再回伦敦来。不过我得承认，我在那边身体要好些；我都为自己那副挤奶女工的模样感到害臊了。」
「你在这儿没有任何消遣，」凯恩怜惜地说，「也没有人做伴，桑德森（Saunderson）先生活着就只为他的那些兰花。」
「它们实在迷人，」迈拉梦呓似的说，「我也领受过它们的魔魅。这户人家里，只有我一个是他准许踏进那间兰室的——」

===Original===
“Perhaps you spend too much time there,” interrupted Cairn; “that superheated, artificial atmosphere﻿—”
Myra shook her head playfully, patting his arm.
“There is nothing in the world the matter with me,” she said, almost in her old bright manner﻿—“now that you are back﻿—”
“I do not approve of orchids,” jerked Cairn doggedly. “They are parodies of what a flower should be. Place an Odontoglossum beside a rose, and what a distorted unholy thing it looks!”

===Chinese===
「也许你在那儿待的时间太长了，」凯恩插话道，「那种过热的人造空气——」
迈拉顽皮地摇了摇头，拍了拍他的手臂。
「我一点儿毛病都没有，」她说，语气几乎又像从前那般明快——「如今你回来了——」
「我看不惯兰花，」凯恩执拗地脱口道，「它们是对花之本相的戏仿。把一株齿舌兰（Odontoglossum）放到一朵玫瑰旁边，它显得多么扭曲、多么邪气！」

===Original===
“Unholy?” laughed Myra.
“Unholy﻿—yes!﻿—they are products of feverish swamps and deathly jungles. I hate orchids. The atmosphere of an orchid-house cannot possibly be clean and healthy. One might as well spend one’s time in a bacteriological laboratory!”
Myra shook her head with affected seriousness.
“You must not let Mr. Saunderson hear you,” she said. “His orchids are his children. Their very mystery enthrals him﻿—and really it is most fascinating. To look at one of those shapeless bulbs, and to speculate upon what kind of bloom it will produce, is almost as thrilling as reading a sensational novel! He has one growing now﻿—it will bloom some time this week﻿—about which he is frantically excited.”

===Chinese===
「邪气？」迈拉笑了。
「邪气——正是！——它们是瘴热沼泽和死寂丛林的产物。我恨兰花。兰室里的空气绝不可能又洁净又卫生。与其在那儿消磨时光，还不如上细菌学实验室里坐着呢！」
迈拉煞有介事地摇了摇头。
「你可千万别让桑德森先生听见这话，」她说，「他的兰花就是他的孩子。正是那份神秘劲儿让他着迷——说真的，那实在引人入胜。瞧着那样一个不成形状的球茎，揣想它会开出什么样的花来，几乎和读一部惊险小说一样叫人心跳！他眼下正养着一株——本星期里哪一天就要开花——他为它兴奋得坐立不安。」

===Original===
“Where did he get it?” asked Cairn without interest.
“He bought it from a man who had almost certainly stolen it! There were six bulbs in the parcel; only two have lived and one of these is much more advanced than the other; it is *so* high﻿—”
She held out her hand, indicating a height of some three feet from the ground.

===Chinese===
「他打哪儿弄来的？」凯恩问道，兴味索然。
「他是从一个人手里买来的，而那球茎十有八九是那人偷来的！包裹里本来有六个球茎，只活下来两个，其中一个又比另一个长得快得多；它已经长到『这么』高了——」
她伸出手来，比了比离地约三英尺的高度。

===Original===
“It has not flowered yet?”
“No. But the buds﻿—huge, smooth, egg-shaped things﻿—seem on the point of bursting at any moment. We call it the ‘Mystery,’ and it is my special care. Mr. Saunderson has shown me how to attend to its simple needs, and if it proves to be a new species﻿—which is almost certain﻿—he is going to exhibit it, and name it after me! Shall you be proud of having an orchid named after﻿—”
“After my wife?” Cairn concluded, seizing her hands. “I could never be more proud of you than I am already.﻿ ﻿…”

===Chinese===
「还没开花？」
「没有。可那些花苞——又大又光滑、蛋形的东西——看样子随时都会胀裂开来。我们管它叫『神秘』，由我专门照料。桑德森先生教了我怎样照料它那几样简单的需求，倘若它果真是一个新种——这几乎是一定的——他就要拿它去展出，还要用我的名字给它命名！若是有一种兰花以……为名，你会感到骄傲吗——」
「以我妻子为名？」凯恩接过话头，一把握住她的双手。「我已不可能比现在更为你骄傲了。……」

## XXIII The Face in the Orchid-House / XXIII 兰室中的脸

===Original===
Dr. Cairn walked to the window, with its old-fashioned leaded panes. A lamp stood by the bedside, and he had tilted the shade so that it shone upon the pale face of the patient﻿—Myra Duquesne.

Two days had wrought a dreadful change in her. She lay with closed eyes, and sunken face upon which ominous shadows played. Her respiration was imperceptible. The reputation of Dr. Bruce Cairn was a well deserved one, but this case puzzled him. He knew that Myra Duquesne was dying before his eyes; he could still see the agonised face of his son, Robert, who at that moment was waiting, filled with intolerable suspense, downstairs in Mr. Saunderson’s study; but, withal, he was helpless. He looked out from the rose-entwined casement across the shrubbery, to where the moonlight glittered among the trees.

Those were the orchid-houses; and with his back to the bed, Dr. Cairn stood for long, thoughtfully watching the distant gleams of reflected light. Craig Fenton and Sir Elwin Groves, with whom he had been consulting, were but just gone. The nature of Myra Duquesne’s illness had utterly puzzled them, and they had left, mystified.

===Chinese===
凯恩博士走到窗前，窗上是老式的铅条玻璃。床头立着一盏灯，他把灯罩拨斜，好让灯光照在病人苍白的脸上——迈拉·迪凯纳。

两天工夫，已在她身上造成了可怕的变化。她阖着双眼躺在那里，凹陷的脸上浮动着不祥的暗影，呼吸轻得难以察觉。布鲁斯·凯恩博士的名声得来无愧，可这个病例却难住了他。他知道迈拉·迪凯纳正在自己眼前死去；他脑中仍能映出儿子罗伯特那张痛苦扭曲的脸——此刻他正满怀难以忍受的煎熬，在楼下桑德森先生的书房里等候；然而，尽管如此，他无能为力。他从玫瑰缠绕的窗扉间望出去，越过灌木丛，望向月光在树影间闪烁的地方。

那边便是兰室；凯恩博士背朝着床，久久伫立，若有所思地望着远处映光闪动的微芒。与他一同会诊的克雷格·芬顿和埃尔温·格罗夫斯爵士才刚离去。迈拉·迪凯纳病症的性质把他们彻底难住了，两人走得满腹疑团。

===Original===
Downstairs, Robert Cairn was pacing the study, wondering if his reason would survive this final blow which threatened. He knew, and his father knew, that a sinister something underlay this strange illness﻿—an illness which had commenced on the day that Antony Ferrara had last visited the house.

The evening was insufferably hot; not a breeze stirred in the leaves; and despite open windows, the air of the room was heavy and lifeless. A faint perfume, having a sort of sweetness, but which yet was unutterably revolting, made itself perceptible to the nostrils. Apparently it had pervaded the house by slow degrees. The occupants were so used to it that they did not notice it at all.

Dr. Cairn had busied himself that evening in the sickroom, burning some pungent preparation, to the amazement of the nurse and of the consultants. Now the biting fumes of his pastilles had all been wafted out of the window and the faint sweet smell was as noticeable as ever.

===Chinese===
楼下，罗伯特·凯恩正在书房里来回踱步，心里琢磨着，眼看这威胁着要来的最后一击，自己的神智究竟撑不撑得过去。他清楚，他父亲也清楚，这场怪病底下潜藏着某种阴森的东西——这场病，正是在安东尼奥·费拉拉最后一次登门那天开始的。

这天晚上闷热难当；树叶间没有一丝风动；尽管窗子敞着，屋里的空气仍旧滞重而死沉。一缕淡淡的香气，带着几分甜意，却又说不出的令人作呕，直钻进人的鼻端。它显然是一点一点浸透了整座宅子。宅中的人对它已如此习惯，竟全然不曾察觉。

这天晚上，凯恩博士一直守在病房里忙碌，焚烧着某种辛辣的药剂，叫护士和会诊医生们大为惊诧。此刻，他那些药锭的呛人烟气已尽数从窗口飘散，那股淡淡的甜香却又和先前一样分明可闻了。

===Original===
Not a sound broke the silence of the house; and when the nurse quietly opened the door and entered, Dr. Cairn was still standing staring thoughtfully out of the window in the direction of the orchid-houses. He turned, and walking back to the bedside, bent over the patient.

Her face was like a white mask; she was quite unconscious; and so far as he could see showed no change either for better or worse. But her pulse was slightly more feeble and the doctor suppressed a groan of despair; for this mysterious progressive weakness could only have one end. All his experience told him that unless something could be done﻿—and every expedient thus far attempted had proved futile﻿—Myra Duquesne would die about dawn.

He turned on his heel, and strode from the room, whispering a few words of instruction to the nurse. Descending the stairs, he passed the closed study door, not daring to think of his son who waited within, and entered the dining-room. A single lamp burnt there, and the gaunt figure of Mr. Saunderson was outlined dimly where he sat in the window seat. Crombie, the gardener, stood by the table.

===Chinese===
没有一点声响打破宅中的寂静；护士轻轻推门进来时，凯恩博士依旧站在那里，朝着兰室的方向出神地凝望窗外。他转过身，走回床边，俯身查看病人。

她的脸像一副白面具；她全然不省人事；就他所能察见的，病情既不见好转，也不见恶化。可她的脉搏又虚了几分，博士强忍住一声绝望的呻吟；因为这神秘的、步步加深的虚弱，只可能有一个结局。他全部的经验都告诉他：除非能想出法子——而迄今试过的种种应急手段都已证明徒劳——迈拉·迪凯纳到天亮前后就会死去。

他倏地转身，大步走出房去，低声向护士交代了几句。下楼时，他经过那扇关着的书房门，不敢去想门内等候着的儿子，径直进了餐厅。屋里只点着一盏灯，桑德森先生清瘦的身影坐在窗座上，轮廓朦朦胧胧。园丁克伦比（Crombie）站在桌旁。

===Original===
“Now, Crombie,” said Dr. Cairn, quietly, closing the door behind him, “what is this story about the orchid-houses, and why did you not mention it before?”

The man stared persistently into the shadows of the room, avoiding Dr. Cairn’s glance.

“Since he has had the courage to own up,” interrupted Mr. Saunderson, “I have overlooked the matter: but he was afraid to speak before, because he had no business to be in the orchid-houses.” His voice grew suddenly fierce﻿—“He knows it well enough!”

“I know, sir, that you don’t want me to interfere with the orchids,” replied the man, “but I only ventured in because I thought I saw a light moving there﻿—”

“Rubbish!” snapped Mr. Saunderson.

===Chinese===
「好了，克伦比，」凯恩博士随手掩上房门，平静地说，「兰室那桩事到底是怎么回事？你先前为什么不提？」

那人只管直勾勾盯着屋里的暗处，避开凯恩博士的目光。

「既然他有了坦白的勇气，」桑德森先生插话道，「这事我就既往不咎了：只是他先前不敢说，因为他本来就不该进兰室。」他的声音陡然严厉起来——「这一点他自己清楚得很！」

「我知道，先生，您不愿意我去动那些兰花，」那人答道，「可我壮着胆子进去，只因我像是看见那儿有一团光在动——」

「胡扯！」桑德森先生厉声道。

===Original===
“Pardon me, Saunderson,” said Dr. Cairn, “but a matter of more importance than the welfare of all the orchids in the world is under consideration now.”

Saunderson coughed dryly.

“You are right, Cairn,” he said. “I shouldn’t have lost my temper for such a trifle, at a time like this. Tell your own tale, Crombie; I won’t interrupt.”

===Chinese===
「恕我直言，桑德森，」凯恩博士说，「但眼下要考虑的事，比普天之下所有兰花的安危都要紧。」

桑德森干咳了一声。

「你说得对，凯恩，」他说，「这种时候，我不该为这点小事动火。你自己讲吧，克伦比；我不插嘴了。」

===Original===
“It was last night then,” continued the man. “I was standing at the door of my cottage smoking a pipe before turning in, when I saw a faint light moving over by the orchid-houses﻿—”

“Reflection of the moon,” muttered Saunderson. “I am sorry. Go on, Crombie!”

“I knew that some of the orchids were very valuable, and I thought there would not be time to call you; also I did not want to worry you, knowing you had worry enough already. So I knocked out my pipe and put it in my pocket, and went through the shrubbery. I saw the light again﻿—it seemed to be moving from the first house into the second. I couldn’t see what it was.”

===Chinese===
「那是昨天夜里的事，」那人接着说，「我正站在自己小屋门口，睡前抽着烟斗，忽然瞧见兰室那边有一团微光在移动——」

「是月亮的反光吧，」桑德森咕哝道，「对不住。接着讲，克伦比！」

「我知道有些兰花贵重得很，心想怕是来不及去叫您；再说，我知道您烦心的事已经够多，也不愿再搅扰您。于是我把烟斗磕空，揣进兜里，穿过了灌木丛。我又看见了那团光——它像是从第一间兰室挪进第二间去。我看不清那究竟是什么。」

===Original===
“Was it like a candle, or a pocket-lamp?” jerked Dr. Cairn.

“Nothing like that, sir; a softer light, more like a glowworm; but much brighter. I went around and tried the door, and it was locked. Then I remembered the door at the other end, and I cut round by the path between the houses and the wall, so that I had no chance to see the light again, until I got to the other door. I found this unlocked. There was a close kind of smell in there, sir, and the air was very hot﻿—”

“Naturally, it was hot,” interrupted Saunderson.

“I mean much hotter than it should have been. It was like an oven, and the smell was stifling﻿—”

“What smell?” asked Dr. Cairn. “Can you describe it?”

“Excuse me, sir, but I seem to notice it here in this room tonight, and I think I noticed it about the place before﻿—never so strong as in the orchid-houses.”

===Chinese===
「像蜡烛，还是袖珍灯？」凯恩博士脱口问道。

「都不是，先生；那光柔和些，倒更像萤火虫，只是亮得多。我绕过去推了推门，门锁着。这时我想起另一头还有一扇门，便从兰室和院墙之间的那条小径绕过去，因此一路上再没机会看见那团光，直到来到那扇门前。我发现这扇门没锁。里头有一股闷浊的气味，先生，而且空气热得很——」

「热，那自然是热的，」桑德森插嘴道。

「我是说，热得远超过应有的分寸。里头像座火炉，那气味更闷得人喘不上气——」

「什么气味？」凯恩博士问。「你说得出是个什么味儿吗？」

「恕我直言，先生，今晚这间屋子里，我像是也闻到了这气味；而且我想，先前在这宅子里也闻到过——只是从没有兰室里那么浓。」

===Original===
“Go on!” said Dr. Cairn.

“I went through the first house, and saw nothing. The shadow of the wall prevented the moonlight from shining in there. But just as I was about to enter the middle house, I thought I saw﻿—a face.”

“What do you mean you *thought* you saw?” snapped Mr. Saunderson.

“I mean, sir, that it was so horrible and so strange that I could not believe it was real﻿—which is one of the reasons why I did not speak before. It reminded me of the face of a gentleman I have seen here﻿—Mr. Ferrara﻿—”

Dr. Cairn stifled an exclamation.

“But in other ways it was quite unlike the gentleman. In some ways it was more like the face of a woman﻿—a very bad woman. It had a sort of bluish light on it, but where it could have come from, I don’t know. It seemed to be smiling, and two bright eyes looked straight out at me.”

===Chinese===
「讲下去！」凯恩博士说。

「我穿过第一间兰室，什么也没看见。围墙的影子挡着，月光照不进那屋里。可就在我要走进中间那间时，我觉得自己看见了——一张脸。」

「你说你『以为』自己看见了，这话是什么意思？」桑德森先生厉声问。

「我是说，先生，它那么可怕、那么古怪，我简直不能相信它是真的——这也是我先前不肯开口的原因之一。它让我想起我在这宅子里见过的一位先生的脸——费拉拉先生——」

凯恩博士强自咽下一声惊呼。

「可从别的方面看，它又全然不像那位先生。有些地方，它更像一张女人的脸——一个非常坏的女人。那张脸上泛着一层幽幽的蓝光，至于那光是打哪儿来的，我就不知道了。它像是在微笑，一双亮眼睛直勾勾地朝我望着。」

===Original===
Crombie stopped, raising his hand to his head confusedly.

“I could see nothing but just this face﻿—low down as if the person it belonged to was crouching on the floor; and there was a tall plant of some kind just beside it﻿—”

“Well,” said Dr. Cairn, “go on! What did you do?”

“I turned to run!” confessed the man. “If you had seen that horrible face, you would understand how frightened I was. Then when I got to the door, I looked back.”

===Chinese===
克伦比停了下来，恍恍惚惚地抬手摸向自己的头。

「除了这张脸，我什么也看不见——它位置低低的，仿佛长着这张脸的人正蹲伏在地上；紧挨着它旁边，还立着一株高高的什么植物——」

「嗯，」凯恩博士说，「讲下去！你当时做了什么？」

「我转身就跑！」那人招认道。「你们要是亲眼见过那张可怕的脸，就会明白我吓成了什么样。等跑到门口，我回头望了一眼。」

===Original===
“I hope you had closed the door behind you,” snapped Saunderson.

“Never mind that, never mind that!” interrupted Dr. Cairn.

“I had closed the door behind me﻿—yes, sir﻿—but just as I was going to open it again, I took a quick glance back, and the face had gone! I came out, and I was walking over the lawn, wondering whether I should tell you, when it occurred to me that I hadn’t noticed whether the key had been left in or not.”

“Did you go back to see?” asked Dr. Cairn.

===Chinese===
「但愿你进去时是把门随手关上了的，」桑德森厉声道。

「那个不用管，不用管！」凯恩博士打断道。

「我进去后是把门关上了的——是的，先生——可就在我正要再开门出去的当口，我飞快回头一瞥，那张脸已经不见了！我走了出来，正走过草坪，心里盘算着要不要告诉您，忽然想起自己没留意钥匙有没有留在锁眼里。」

「你回去看了吗？」凯恩博士问。

===Original===
“I didn’t want to,” admitted Crombie, “but I did﻿—and﻿—”

“Well?”

“The door was locked, sir!”

“So you concluded that your imagination had been playing you tricks,” said Saunderson grimly. “In my opinion you were right.”

===Chinese===
「我本不想回去，」克伦比承认道，「可我还是去了——而且——」

「怎么样？」

「门锁上了，先生！」

「于是你便断定，是自己的想象力在捉弄你，」桑德森冷冷地说。「依我看，你断得没错。」

===Original===
Dr. Cairn dropped into an armchair.

“All right, Crombie; that will do.”

Crombie, with a mumbled “Good night, gentlemen,” turned and left the room.

“Why are you worrying about this matter,” inquired Saunderson, when the door had closed, “at a time like the present?”

“Never mind,” replied Dr. Cairn wearily. “I must return to Half-Moon Street, now, but I shall be back within an hour.”

===Chinese===
凯恩博士颓然跌坐进一把扶手椅里。

「好了，克伦比；就到这儿吧。」

克伦比含含糊糊道了声「晚安，各位先生」，转身出了屋。

「你为什么还要为这桩事操心，」等门关严实了，桑德森问道，「偏偏在眼下这种时候？」

「先别问这个，」凯恩博士疲惫地答道。「眼下我必须回半月街去一趟，不过一个钟头之内就回来。」

===Original===
With no other word to Saunderson, he stood up and walked out to the hall. He rapped at the study door, and it was instantly opened by Robert Cairn. No spoken word was necessary; the burning question could be read in his too-bright eyes. Dr. Cairn laid his hand upon his son’s shoulder.

“I won’t excite false hopes, Rob,” he said huskily. “I am going back to the house, and I want you to come with me.”

Robert Cairn turned his head aside, groaning aloud, but his father grasped him by the arm, and together they left that house of shadows, entered the car which waited at the gate, and without exchanging a word en route, came to Half-Moon Street.

===Chinese===
他没有再对桑德森多说一个字，起身走进门厅。他敲了敲书房的门，门随即被罗伯特·凯恩打开了。无需一句言语；那桩焦灼的问题，从他亮得反常的眼睛里便能读出来。凯恩博士把一只手搭在儿子肩上。

「我不愿激起虚妄的希望，罗布，」他嗓音沙哑地说。「我这就回那宅子去，我要你跟我一道。」

罗伯特·凯恩把头扭向一旁，出声地呻吟起来，但他父亲一把攥住他的手臂；父子二人一同离开了那座暗影幢幢的宅子，上了候在大门口的汽车，一路不曾交一言，来到了半月街。

## XXIV Flowering of the Lotus / XXIV 莲花的绽放

===Original===
Dr. Cairn led the way into the library, switching on the reading-lamp upon the large table. His son stood just within the doorway, his arms folded and his chin upon his breast.

The doctor sat down at the table, watching the other.

Suddenly Robert spoke:

“Is it possible, sir, is it possible﻿—” his voice was barely audible﻿—“that her illness can in any way be due to the orchids?”

===Chinese===
凯恩博士在前头引路进了书房，拧亮大桌子上那盏台灯。他儿子就站在门内侧，双臂交抱，下巴抵在胸前。

博士在桌旁坐下，注视着儿子。

罗伯特突然开口：

「可能吗，先生，可能吗——」他的声音低得几乎听不见——「她的病，会不会跟兰花有什么干系？」

===Original===
Dr. Cairn frowned thoughtfully.

“What do you mean, exactly?” he asked.

“Orchids are mysterious things. They come from places where there are strange and dreadful diseases. Is it not possible that they may convey﻿—”

“Some sort of contagion?” concluded Dr. Cairn. “It is a point that I have seen raised, certainly. But nothing of the sort has ever been established. I have heard something, tonight, though, which﻿—”

===Chinese===
凯恩博士若有所思地蹙起眉头。

「你究竟是什么意思？」他问。

「兰花是神秘的东西。它们来的地方，有着种种古怪可怖的疾病。它们会不会传带——」

「某种传染？」凯恩博士替他接完。「这个问题确实见人提出过。但这类事情还从未得到过证实。不过今晚，我倒是听到了一桩事情——」

===Original===
“What have you heard, sir?” asked his son eagerly, stepping forward to the table.

“Never mind at the moment, Rob; let me think.”

He rested his elbow upon the table, and his chin in his hand. His professional instincts had told him that unless something could be done﻿—something which the highest medical skill in London had thus far been unable to devise﻿—Myra Duquesne had but four hours to live. Somewhere in his mind a memory lurked, evasive, taunting him. This wild suggestion of his son’s, that the girl’s illness might be due in some way to her contact with the orchids, was in part responsible for this confused memory, but it seemed to be associated, too, with the story of Crombie the gardener﻿—and with Antony Ferrara. He felt that somewhere in the darkness surrounding him there was a speck of light, if he could but turn in the right direction to see it. So, whilst Robert Cairn walked restlessly about the big room, the doctor sat with his chin resting in the palm of his hand, seeking to concentrate his mind upon that vague memory, which defied him, whilst the hand of the library clock crept from twelve towards one; whilst he knew that the faint life in Myra Duquesne was slowly ebbing away in response to some mysterious condition, utterly outside his experience.

===Chinese===
「您听到了什么，先生？」他儿子急切地问，抢步走向桌前。

「这会儿先别管，罗布；让我想想。」

他把手肘支在桌上，下巴搁进手心。职业本能告诉他：除非能想出法子——某种连伦敦最高明的医术至今都无从想出的法子——迈拉·迪凯纳就只剩四个钟头好活了。他心底某处潜伏着一段记忆，躲躲闪闪，偏生撩拨他。儿子这个近乎狂想的揣测——说姑娘的病或许同她接触兰花有些干系——是搅起这段混沌记忆的部分缘由；可它似乎又与园丁克伦比的那番讲述、与安东尼奥·费拉拉纠缠在一处。他只觉得，四周黑暗的某处有一点微光，只要朝对的方向转过身去，便能望见。于是，当罗伯特·凯恩在那间大屋里焦躁不安地踱来踱去时，博士始终坐着，下巴抵着手掌，竭力要把心神凝在那段偏不就范的模糊记忆上；书房座钟的指针从十二点向一点爬行；他知道，迈拉·迪凯纳那缕微弱的生命，正在某种全然超出他阅历的神秘病况中，一点一点枯竭下去。

===Original===
Distant clocks chimed *One*! Three hours only!

Robert Cairn began to beat his fist into the palm of his left hand convulsively. Yet his father did not stir, but sat there, a black-shadowed wrinkle between his brows.﻿ ﻿…

“By God!”

The doctor sprang to his feet, and with feverish haste began to fumble amongst a bunch of keys.

===Chinese===
远处的钟敲响了『一』点！只剩三个钟头了！

罗伯特·凯恩开始痉挛似的把拳头一下下擂进左手掌心。他的父亲却纹丝不动，只是坐在那里，眉宇间拧着一道阴影深重的皱纹。……

「我的上帝！」

博士霍地跳起身来，带着发热般的急切，动手在一串钥匙里翻拣。

===Original===
“What is it, sir! What is it?”

The doctor unlocked the drawer of the big table, and drew out a thick manuscript written in small and exquisitely neat characters. He placed it under the lamp, and rapidly began to turn the pages.

“It is hope, Rob!” he said with quiet self-possession.

Robert Cairn came round the table, and leant over his father’s shoulder.

===Chinese===
「怎么了，先生！怎么了？」

博士打开大桌的抽屉锁，取出一部厚厚的手稿，满纸细小而精美齐整的字迹。他把手稿摊到灯下，飞快地翻动起来。

「是希望，罗布！」他说，神色安然镇定。

罗伯特·凯恩绕过桌子，俯身到他父亲肩后。

===Original===
“Sir Michael Ferrara’s writing!”

“His unpublished book, Rob. We were to have completed it, together, but death claimed him, and in view of the contents, I﻿—perhaps superstitiously﻿—decided to suppress it.﻿ ﻿… Ah!”

He placed the point of his finger upon a carefully drawn sketch, designed to illustrate the text. It was evidently a careful copy from the Ancient Egyptian. It represented a row of priestesses, each having her hair plaited in a thick queue, standing before a priest armed with a pair of scissors. In the centre of the drawing was an altar, upon which stood vases of flowers; and upon the right ranked a row of mummies, corresponding in number with the priestesses upon the left.

“By God!” repeated Dr. Cairn, “we were both wrong, we were both wrong!”

===Chinese===
「迈克尔·费拉拉爵士的手迹！」

「他生前未刊的书，罗布。我们本打算一道把它写完，死神却把他召去了；而念及书里的内容，我——或许是出于迷信吧——决定将它压下，永不出世。……啊！」

他把指尖按在一幅精心绘制的插图上，那图原是为正文作解说的，显然是照古埃及原本细心摹绘的一件摹本。图上画着一列女祭司，人人头上编着一条粗重的发辫，伫立在一名手执剪刀的祭司面前。画面正中是一座祭坛，坛上供着一瓶瓶花；右首排着一列木乃伊，数目恰与左首的女祭司一一相当。

「我的上帝！」凯恩博士又说了一遍，「咱们俩都想错了，咱们俩都想错了！」

===Original===
“What do you mean, sir? for Heaven’s sake, what do you mean?”

“This drawing,” replied Dr. Cairn, “was copied from the wall of a certain tomb﻿—now reclosed. Since we knew that the tomb was that of one of the greatest wizards who ever lived in Egypt, we knew also that the inscription had some magical significance. We knew that the flowers represented here, were a species of the extinct sacred Lotus. All our researches did not avail us to discover for what purpose or by what means these flowers were cultivated. Nor could we determine the meaning of the cutting off,”﻿—he ran his fingers over the sketch﻿—“of the priestesses’ hair by the high priest of the goddess﻿—”

“What goddess, sir?”

“A goddess, Rob, of which Egyptology knows nothing!﻿—a mystical religion the existence of which has been vaguely suspected by a living French savant﻿ ﻿… but this is no time﻿—”

===Chinese===
「您是什么意思，先生？看在老天爷份上，您到底是什么意思？」

「这幅图，」凯恩博士答道，「是从某座陵墓的壁上临摹下来的——那墓如今已重新封死了。我们既知道那墓是埃及有史以来最伟大的巫师之一的安息之所，便也知道这壁上的图文自有某种巫术上的深意。我们知道图上所绘的花，是一种早已绝迹的神圣莲花（sacred Lotus）。可我们历来的种种查考，都未能查明这些花是为了什么目的、用什么法子栽培出来的。我们也断不出这剪除之举是什么意思，」——他的手指顺着那幅图抚过——「女祭司的头发，由女神的大祭司亲手剪除——」

「哪位女神，先生？」

「一位埃及学一无所知的女神，罗布！——一种神秘的宗教，当今在世的一位法国学者曾隐约疑心过它的存在……可眼下不是时候——」

===Original===
Dr. Cairn closed the manuscript, replaced it and relocked the drawer. He glanced at the clock.

“A quarter past one,” he said. “Come, Rob!”

Without hesitation, his son followed him from the house. The car was waiting, and shortly they were speeding through the deserted streets, back to the house where death in a strange guise was beckoning to Myra Duquesne. As the car started﻿—

===Chinese===
凯恩博士合上手稿，放回原处，重新锁好抽屉。他瞥了一眼座钟。

「一点一刻，」他说。「走吧，罗布！」

他儿子毫不迟疑，跟着他出了宅门。汽车正候着；不多时，他们便驶过空寂的街道，朝那座宅子飞驰而去——在那里，一个乔装改扮的死神正向迈拉·迪凯纳招手。汽车刚一启动——

===Original===
“Do you know,” asked Dr. Cairn, “if Saunderson has bought any orchids﻿—*quite* recently, I mean?”

“Yes,” replied his son dully; “he bought a small parcel only a fortnight ago.”

“A fortnight!” cried Dr. Cairn excitedly﻿—“you are sure of that? You mean that the purchase was made since Ferrara﻿—”

“Ceased to visit the house? Yes. Why!﻿—it must have been the very day after!”

===Chinese===
「你知道不知道，」凯恩博士问，「桑德森买没买过兰花——我是说，『就在』最近？」

「买过，」他儿子木然答道；「就在两个星期前，他买了一小批。」

「两个星期！」凯恩博士激动地喊道——「你拿得准？你是说，这批花是在费拉拉——」

「不再登门之后买的？拿得准。哎呀！——那准是就在他走后的第二天！」

===Original===
Dr. Cairn clearly was labouring under tremendous excitement.

“Where did he buy these orchids?” he asked, evenly.

“From someone who came to the house﻿—someone he had never dealt with before.”

The doctor, his hands resting upon his knees, was rapidly drumming with his fingers.

===Chinese===
凯恩博士显然正被一股强烈的激动煎熬着。

「他这些兰花是从哪儿买的？」他问道，声气平稳。

「从一个上门来的人手里——一个他从前从没打过交道的人。」

博士双手搭在膝上，手指急速地敲着点子。

===Original===
“And﻿—did he cultivate them?”

“Two only proved successful. One is on the point of blooming﻿—if it is not blooming already. He calls it the ‘Mystery.’ ”

At that, the doctor’s excitement overcame him. Suddenly leaning out of the window, he shouted to the chauffeur:

“Quicker! Quicker! Never mind risks. Keep on top speed!”

===Chinese===
「那么——他把它种起来了？」

「只有两株种活了。有一株眼看就要开花——说不定这会儿已经开了。他管它叫『神秘』。」

听得这话，博士再也按捺不住激动。他突然探身窗外，朝司机高声喊道：

「再快些！再快些！别管什么风险。开足马力！」

===Original===
“What is it, sir?” cried his son. “Heavens! what is it?”

“Did you say that it might have bloomed, Rob?”

“Myra”﻿—Robert Cairn swallowed noisily﻿—“told me three days ago that it was expected to bloom before the end of the week.”

“What is it like?”

===Chinese===
「怎么回事，先生？」他儿子喊道。「天哪！到底怎么回事？」

「你方才说，它可能已经开了，罗布？」

「迈拉」——罗伯特·凯恩重重地咽了口唾沫——「三天前告诉我，说这花指望在本周结束前开。」

「它什么样？」

===Original===
“A thing four feet high, with huge egg-shaped buds.”

“Merciful God grant that we are in time,” whispered Dr. Cairn. “I could believe once more in the justice of Heaven, if the great knowledge of Sir Michael Ferrara should prove to be the weapon to destroy the fiend whom we raised!﻿—he and I﻿—may we be forgiven!”

Robert Cairn’s excitement was dreadful.

“Can you tell me nothing?” he cried. “What do you hope? What do you fear?”

“Don’t ask me, Rob,” replied his father; “you will know within five minutes.”

===Chinese===
「一株四英尺高的东西，结着巨大的蛋形花苞。」

「仁慈的上帝，求您保佑我们来得及，」凯恩博士低声道。「倘若迈克尔·费拉拉爵士的渊博学识，最终竟成了诛灭那恶魔的利器——那恶魔是我们亲手养大的呀！——我便能再一次相信上天的公道！——他和我——愿我们得到宽恕！」

罗伯特·凯恩的激动已到了可怕的地步。

「您就一点也不能告诉我吗？」他喊道。「您盼的是什么？您怕的又是什么？」

「别问我，罗布，」他父亲答道；「不出五分钟，你就全知道了。」

===Original===
The car indeed was leaping along the dark suburban roads at a speed little below that of an express train. Corners the chauffeur negotiated in racing fashion, so that at times two wheels thrashed the empty air; and once or twice the big car swung round as upon a pivot only to recover again in response to the skilled tactics of the driver.

They roared down the sloping narrow lane to the gate of Mr. Saunderson’s house with a noise like the coming of a great storm, and were nearly hurled from their seats when the brakes were applied, and the car brought to a standstill.

Dr. Cairn leapt out, pushed open the gate and ran up to the house, his son closely following. There was a light in the hall and Miss Saunderson who had expected them, and had heard their stormy approach, already held the door open. In the hall﻿—

===Chinese===
汽车果真在黑暗的郊区道路上飞掠向前，速度只比特快列车略逊一筹。司机过弯全用赛车的路数，有几回两只车轮直在空荡的夜气里扑打；有一两次，大车身整个儿像绕着枢轴似的甩转过去，全凭驾驶人娴熟的身手才又稳住了。

他们轰鸣着冲下那条坡陡路窄的小巷，直抵桑德森先生宅前的大门，声势如一场大风暴压境；刹车咬住、汽车停稳的那一霎，两人险些从座位上甩出去。

凯恩博士纵身下车，推开院门，直奔宅子，儿子紧随其后。门厅里亮着灯；桑德森小姐早料到他们要来，又听见了这阵疾风骤雨似的声势，已经把门敞开。在门厅里——

===Original===
“Wait here one moment,” said Dr. Cairn.

Ignoring Saunderson, who had come out from the library, he ran upstairs. A minute later, his face very pale, he came running down again.

“She is worse?” began Saunderson, “but﻿—”

“Give me the key of the orchid-house!” said Dr. Cairn tersely.

===Chinese===
「在这儿稍等片刻，」凯恩博士说。

他不理会从书房里出来的桑德森，径自奔上楼去。一分钟后，他又奔了下来，脸色惨白。

「她更糟了？」桑德森开口道，「可是——」

「把兰室的钥匙给我！」凯恩博士简截地说。

===Original===
“Orchid-house!﻿—”

“Don’t hesitate. Don’t waste a second. Give me the key.”

Saunderson’s expression showed that he thought Dr. Cairn to be mad, but nevertheless he plunged his hand into his pocket and pulled out a key-ring. Dr. Cairn snatched it in a flash.

“Which key?” he snapped.

===Chinese===
「兰室！——」

「别犹豫。一秒钟也别耽搁。把钥匙给我。」

桑德森的神情分明是认定凯恩博士疯了，可他还是把手伸进口袋，掏出一串钥匙。凯恩博士闪电般一把夺了过来。

「哪一把？」他厉声问。

===Original===
“The Chubb, but﻿—”

“Follow me, Rob!”

Down the hall he raced, his son beside him, and Mr. Saunderson following more slowly. Out into the garden he went and over the lawn towards the shrubbery.

The orchid-houses lay in dense shadow; but the doctor almost threw himself against the door.

===Chinese===
「查布（Chubb）的那把，可是——」

「跟我来，罗布！」

他顺着门厅一路疾奔，儿子与他并肩，桑德森先生跟得慢些。他奔出屋外进了花园，越过草坪，直向灌木丛而去。

兰室沉在浓重的黑影里；博士几乎是整个人朝那扇门撞去。

===Original===
“Strike a match!” he panted. Then﻿—“Never mind﻿—I have it!”

The door flew open with a bang. A sickly perfume swept out to them.

“Matches! matches, Rob! this way!”

They went stumbling in. Robert Cairn took out a box of matches﻿—and struck one. His father was further along, in the centre building.

===Chinese===
「划根火柴！」他喘着气说。随即——「不用了——我打开了！」

门砰地一声弹开。一股病腻的香气向他们兜头卷来。

「火柴！火柴，罗布！这边来！」

两人深一脚浅一脚地闯了进去。罗伯特·凯恩掏出一盒火柴——划着了一根。他父亲已在更前头，在那排屋子正中的那一间里。

===Original===
“Your knife, boy﻿—quick! *quick!*”

As the dim light crept along the aisle between the orchids, Robert Cairn saw his father’s horror-stricken face﻿ ﻿… and saw a vivid green plant growing in a sort of tub, before which the doctor stood. Four huge, smooth, egg-shaped buds grew upon the leafless stems; two of them were on the point of opening, and one already showed a delicious, rosy flush about its apex.

Dr. Cairn grasped the knife which Robert tremblingly offered him. The match went out. There was a sound of hacking, a soft *swishing*, and a dull thud upon the tiled floor.

As another match fluttered into brief life, the mysterious orchid, severed just above the soil, fell from the tub. Dr. Cairn stamped the swelling buds under his feet. A profusion of colourless sap was pouring out upon the floor.

===Chinese===
「你的刀，孩子——快！『快！』」

微光顺着兰花中间的通道慢慢爬过去，罗伯特·凯恩看见了他父亲那张惊骇万状的脸……又看见一株鲜绿鲜明的植物，长在一只桶状的大盆里，博士就立在盆前。四颗硕大光滑的蛋形花苞结在光秃秃的茎干上；两颗眼看就要绽开，有一颗的顶端已透出一抹可人的绯红。

凯恩博士一把攥过罗伯特颤巍巍递上的刀。火柴熄了。只听一阵劈砍之声，一声轻软的『嗖嗖』，又在瓷砖地面上响起一声闷闷的坠落。

又一根火柴闪起短促的微光，只见那株神秘的兰花已齐土面被斩断，正从盆里倒下去。凯恩博士把那些胀鼓鼓的花苞踩在脚下碾踏。大股无色的汁液泼洒在地板上。

===Original===
Above the intoxicating odour of the place, a smell like that of blood made itself perceptible.

The second match went out.

“Another﻿—”

Dr. Cairn’s voice rose barely above a whisper. With fingers quivering, Robert Cairn managed to light a third match.
